### 2026-09-16 — Does catching extractor crashes rescue OKEZONE live? (SYS-02)
- **Question / hypothesis:** OKEZONE's `_get_text` does `soup.find(...).find(...)` on a container that no longer exists → `AttributeError` → the outer `except BaseException` returned `None`, so the article was discarded *and* never marked attempted. Wrapping `_get_reporter_from_text` and `_get_whole_text` individually should turn the crash into "extractor failed" and let trafilatura take over.
- **Method:** 4 new unit tests in `tests/unit/test_crawler_base.py::TestExtractorCrashFallsThrough` (body raises / reporter raises / both / warning logged); live `check_crawlers.py --website OKEZONE --sample-size 3`.
- **Result:** unit suite 149 passed. Live: `OKEZONE OK 100 links, 3/3 (4047ch)` — each sample logged `WARNING Site extractor raised … AttributeError` then `Trafilatura extraction successful`. Before: `PARTIAL 0/3`.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed. Both PARTIAL outlets from the audit are now OK-by-fallback (KUMPARAN via SYS-01, OKEZONE via SYS-02); their selectors are still dead → SRC-07 (#18). Residual: a *fetch* failure (`get_soup` → `None`) still returns `None` and is re-fetched every run — split out as SYS-09 (#27) because it needs a cooldown/failure-count column, not a crawler change. Note OKEZONE's first sitemap branch still serves 2025-08 articles; that's SRC-07's branch-selection point.
- **Links:** SYS-02 (#3) fixed · SYS-09 (#27) new · SRC-07 (#18).

### 2026-09-16 — Does re-raising in `get_session` make a DB outage visible? (SYS-03)
- **Question / hypothesis:** `SQLAlchemyClient.get_session` caught every exception, rolled back and returned normally, so `save_sitemaps` fell through to `return []` and the crawl logged `completed`. Adding `raise` after the rollback should make the failure propagate to the existing `log_crawl_failed` / per-batch handlers, and make a DB-down startup exit non-zero.
- **Method:** traced all 29 `get_session()` call sites for what catches the propagated error (crawl_log writers, web UI and `check_crawlers --log-db` already wrap; sitemap/article/chunk writes propagate to `crawl_sitemaps`/`crawl_newsdetails` → `log_crawl_failed` + re-raise, or to the per-batch `except` in `_crawl_newsdetails_inner`; `load_last_time_crawling` runs in `init_crawler()` *outside* `process_event`'s try). 5 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_sqlalchemy_client.py`, mocked session) + 3 live tests (`tests/integration/test_sqlalchemy_client_live.py`: bad SQL raises, session reusable afterwards, port-1 client raises `DBAPIError` from `save_chunks`). End-to-end: `POSTGRES_DB_PORT=1 python -c "process_event({'task':'sitemap','website':'KOMPAS'})"`.
- **Result:** unit 178 passed; integration 30 passed / 1 skipped (backfill file excluded, see SYS-10). End-to-end: `ERROR … Database error, rolling back: OperationalError: … port 1 failed: Connection refused`, traceback, **exit code 1**, the post-call print never reached. Baseline integration run *before* the change was `9 failed / 3 errors` for unrelated reasons (leftover fixture rows → DBT-06; `nomic-embed-text` on HF → SYS-10); truncating `newsaggregator_test` gave a clean 27-pass baseline.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed. A DB outage is now loud at every layer that matters. Two things learned the hard way: (1) run the integration suite against a *truncated* test DB or its failures are noise; (2) `scripts/backfill_chunks.py` has been unrunnable since the MiniLM switch — don't use it until SYS-10 lands.
- **Links:** SYS-03 (#4) fixed · SYS-10 (#28), DBT-06 (#29) new · SYS-06 (#7) still needed so `process_event`-level failures mid-run also exit non-zero.

### 2026-09-16 — EMITENNEWS: what does its sitemap actually look like, and does the rewrite hold up? (SRC-01)
- **Question / hypothesis:** the crawler crashed on every run (`TypeError: argument of type 'NoneType' is not iterable`). Suspected `_scrape` passing `<loc>` tags to a `_get_link` that looks for a `<loc>` *inside* them. Also `_get_timestamp` returned `now()`, which would change the `(link, posted_at)` dedup key daily.
- **Method:** fetched the live index and children with `RequestsPageLoader`; inspected tag structure and `<lastmod>` ordering across children 1/2/3/23; checked `DateTimeReader.convert_date('2026-09-16')` and the base class's UTC conversion. Rewrote `_scrape` to iterate `<url>`, read `<lastmod>` (kept in WIB), cap children at `max_child_sitemaps = 2`; 10 offline unit tests (`tests/unit/test_emitennews_crawler.py`); live `check_crawlers.py --website EMITENNEWS --sample-size 3`; then ran the real sitemap crawl into **`newsaggregator_test`** twice via `NewsDataSource.save_sitemap` and counted rows (cleaned up after).
- **Result:**
  - Index = 23 children `sitemap-current-N.xml`, all `lastmod`=today; each child = 1,000 `<url><loc/><lastmod/></url>` (no `news:` tags); **chronological newest-first** (child 1: 2026-09-20→08-24, child 2: 08-24→08-03, child 3: 08-03→07-13). Child 23 has < 2 entries.
  - `convert_date('2026-09-16')` → `2026-09-16 00:00+07:00`; the base `_get_timestamp`'s `.astimezone(UTC)` turns that into `2026-09-15 17:00Z` → stored **20260915**. Filed as SYS-11 (#30) — affects every source for 00:00–06:59 WIB articles.
  - Health: `EMITENNEWS OK 2000 links, 3/3 (2625ch)`. 2 of 3 samples used trafilatura: one is the future-dated stub (`lastmod 2026-09-20`, no `div.news-page-item`), one an awards/event page. The selector works on normal articles (5,198 chars on the third sample).
  - Test-DB double run: run 1 inserted 2,000 rows / 2,000 distinct links (posted_at 2026-08-03..09-20); run 2 → still 2,000 rows, **no duplicates** ✔ — but `date-gate passed 0/2000`: `NewsDataSource.save_sitemap` dropped every row before dedup because max posted_at is now the future-dated 2026-09-20.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** SRC-01's own criteria pass. **SYS-07 (#8) is a hard blocker for EMITENNEWS after its first run** — with date-only timestamps and a future-dated stub, the pre-dedup gate discards everything until the calendar passes 2026-09-20, and even then same-day articles. Fix SYS-07 before the next scheduled crawl. Don't raise `max_child_sitemaps` for the daily crawl; use `None` only for a one-off backfill. `?page=all` is harmless on emitennews.com.
- **Links:** SRC-01 (#12) fixed · SYS-07 (#8) now urgent · SYS-11 (#30) new.

### 2026-09-16 — Re-audit of the four P0 fixes (SYS-01/02/03, SRC-01)
- **Question / hypothesis:** did today's fixes introduce anything, or miss a path? Re-read each diff and probed the interactions between them.
- **Method:** three targeted repros: (A) extractor raises + trafilatura and newspaper both empty; (B) `CrawlerAPI.crawl_websites_in_batch` with a service that raises on source 1 of 5; (C) re-read `KnowledgeExtractor.extract_all` / `NewsletterGenerator.generate` for per-item handling now that `get_session` re-raises; (D) grep of every site extractor for non-string list items (would break `_text_length`).
- **Result:** (A) **miss** — `extracted_text=None` → `NewsDetailsDTO` validation error (`List[str]`) → `_get_news_details` returns `None` → never marked attempted. SYS-02's tests only covered "trafilatura succeeds". (B) **pre-existing bug** — only `['BERITASATU']` attempted, 4 skipped, exception escapes; SRC-01 (21 sources) and SYS-03 (DB errors propagate) both raised its blast radius today. (C) extractor has a per-article `try/except`; newsletter `save_digest` is wrapped; no regression. (D) none found. Trade-off noted on SYS-01: `MIN_ARTICLE_CHARS=200` + "keep the longer" can let boilerplate-laden trafilatura output beat clean short site text.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** when a fix's purpose is "the sitemap gets marked attempted", the test must cover the *all-fallbacks-fail* branch, not just the rescue branch. Any change that makes errors propagate (SYS-03-style) needs the enclosing loops checked for per-item isolation — `crawl_websites_in_batch` had none. Reopened SYS-02; filed SYS-12 (#31).
- **Links:** SYS-02 (#3) reopened · SYS-12 (#31) new · SYS-01 (#2) comment.

### 2026-09-16 — Does per-source isolation keep a batch alive past a failing source? (SYS-12)
- **Question / hypothesis:** with a `try/except` per source in `CrawlerAPI.crawl_websites_in_batch`, a raising source should be logged and skipped, the rest should run, and one `RuntimeError` should surface at the end.
- **Method:** 7 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_crawler_api.py`); end-to-end `process_event({'task':'sitemap','website':'KOMPAS,BOGUS,IDXCHANNEL'})` against `newsaggregator_test` (BOGUS is not in `CRAWLER_DICT` → `AttributeError` inside `crawl_sitemaps`). First attempt used SUARA as the "broken" source — it doesn't raise: `URL.SUARA == ""` → `get_soup("")` → `None` → 0 links → `completed`. A silent zero, not an exception (SRC-02 / SYS-04 territory).
- **Result:** unit 177 passed. End-to-end: `ERROR BOGUS failed (sitemap): AttributeError …` → `Saved 129 sitemaps` for IDXCHANNEL → `Batch 'sitemap' finished: ok=2 failed=1 | failed: BOGUS (…)` → `crawl_log`: KOMPAS completed, BOGUS failed, IDXCHANNEL completed. `process_event` still logs the final error at INFO and exits 0 — that's SYS-06 (#7).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed. Note for future "does source X fail?" checks: a dead/empty sitemap URL is *not* an exception path — it shows up only as `article_count=0`/0 links, so use `check_crawlers.py`, not the crawl log, to detect it until OPS-02 lands.
- **Links:** SYS-12 (#31) fixed · SYS-06 (#7) next for the exit code.

### 2026-09-16 — Does removing the date gate unblock EMITENNEWS and backfills? (SYS-07)
- **Question / hypothesis:** `NewsDataSource.save_sitemap` dropped any entry not strictly newer than the stored max `posted_at` for its (source, category), *before* `save_sitemaps`' `(link, posted_at)` dedup. Removing it should (a) let older never-seen entries in, (b) let date-only sources keep accumulating after run 1, and (c) not create duplicates because the dedup already handles re-listed links.
- **Method:** deleted the gate; stopped eager-loading `last_time_crawling` in `SQLAlchemySitemapDataSource.__init__` (full-table `GROUP BY` at every start; crashes on a NULL max); scoped the dedup pre-fetch to the batch's own `sources` since a batch can now span weeks. 4 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_news_data_source.py`, the audit repro), 3 live (`tests/integration/test_sitemap_backfill_live.py`: older-after-newer inserted, same batch twice = no dups, EMITENNEWS-style consecutive days). Re-ran the morning's EMITENNEWS double-run into `newsaggregator_test`, plus a run 3 adding one article dated *before* the future-dated stub.
- **Result:** unit 181 / integration 34 passed. EMITENNEWS: run 1 `2000/2000 → 2000 rows`; run 2 `2000/2000 → 2000 rows` (this morning: `0/2000`); run 3 `2001/2001 → 2001 rows` — the new pre-stub article is inserted. Distinct links == rows throughout.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed; EMITENNEWS is now viable across runs. The dedup pre-fetch is now `WHERE sources IN (batch) AND posted_at >= earliest` — for a 6-week EMITENNEWS batch that's that source's rows only, not every source's. `load_last_time_crawling()` still exists (DS08/DS09 guard it) but nothing in the crawl path calls it.
- **Links:** SYS-07 (#8) fixed · SRC-01 (#12) now truly done · SYS-11 (#30) still applies to timestamped sources.

### 2026-09-16 — QA re-audit: does `task=all` re-crawl already-articled sitemaps now that the date gate is gone? (SYS-07)
- **Question / hypothesis:** the removed gate only let strictly-newer entries through, so `crawl_sitemaps`' return value used to be ≈ "new since last run". Post-244d9de `save_sitemaps` returns *every* entry with an ID, and `crawl_website("all")` hands that list to `crawl_newsdetails(specific_sitemaps=…)`, which never consults `load_all_sitemaps`' no-article/cooldown filter. Suspected: every listed URL is re-fetched per run and re-saved as a new `articles` row.
- **Method:** real `NewsDataSource` against `newsaggregator_test` (env forced), no network: 3 synthetic `REAUDIT07` entries → `save_sitemap` → `save_newsdetails` for each returned ID (simulating step 2) → `save_sitemap` again with the same batch → compare with `load_sitemap` → `save_newsdetails` again. Cleaned up after. Schema check with `pg_constraint`/`pg_indexes`.
- **Result:**
  ```
  run 1: save_sitemap returned 3 ids [2048, 2049, 2050]        articles after run 1: 3
  run 2: save_sitemap returned 3 ids [2048, 2049, 2050]        load_sitemap (filtered path) would return 0
  articles after run 2: 6 for 3 distinct sitemaps
  ```
  `articles` has only `articles_pk` + `articles_sitemap_fk` — no unique on `sitemap_id`. Production already: 12,506 rows / 11,889 distinct `sitemap_id` (617 extra over 437 sitemaps, max 4 copies; GRIDID +196, JPNN +122, PIKIRANRAKYAT +102 …) from the older `delta.seconds > 0` loophole. Also: `crawl_log.article_count` for `task='sitemap'` now records entries listed, not rows inserted.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** SYS-07 reopened — the fix is correct for `sitemaps` but regresses `task=all`, the `__main__.py` default. Recommended fix: `task=all` should call `crawl_newsdetails(website_name)` with no `specific_sitemaps` (the code comment already claims it loads from the DB), which gives cooldown + no-article + 1,000-limit semantics for free. Lesson: when a filter is removed, grep for who consumed its *output* — here the gate doubled as the "what to crawl next" selector. Filed DBT-07 for the unique index (the safety net that would have made this loud).
- **Links:** SYS-07 (#8) reopened · DBT-07 (#34) new · SYS-13 (#32) related.

### 2026-09-16 — QA re-audit: is a DB outage still loud after SYS-07, and what about the commit path? (SYS-03)
- **Question / hypothesis:** SYS-03's closing note said "DB-down at startup exits non-zero" because `SQLAlchemySitemapDataSource.__init__` queried the DB inside `init_crawler()`, outside `process_event`'s try. SYS-07 removed that query. Does the done-when still hold? And is `save_sitemaps`' own `try/except` around `session.commit()` a swallow of the same class?
- **Method:** (a) `POSTGRES_DB_PORT=1`, `Crawler.get_news_in_bulk` patched to return one fake KOMPAS entry, `process_event({'task':'sitemap','website':'KOMPAS'})`, exit code captured. (b) mocked session whose `commit()` raises `OperationalError`, real `SQLAlchemySitemapDataSource.save_sitemaps` with 3 entries.
- **Result:** (a) `ERROR Database error, rolling back: OperationalError … port 1 … Connection refused` + traceback → `ERROR KOMPAS failed (sitemap)` (SYS-12) → `Batch 'sitemap' finished: ok=0 failed=1` → `INFO Failed to process task 'sitemap'. Reason: 1 of 1 sources failed` → **exit code 0**. `crawl_log` cannot be written at all in this state. (b) `ERROR Critical commit error: … [SQL: COMMIT]` then `RETURNED NORMALLY: 3 sitemaps with ids [101, 102, 103]` — the caller logs `Saved 3 sitemaps`, marks `crawl_log` completed, and (under `task=all`) crawls articles against IDs that were rolled back; FAISS would keep orphan vectors because the index is written before the SQL FK fails.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** SYS-03 stays closed — the re-raise is intact and the outage is loud in the log — but the "exits non-zero" claim is void; SYS-06 (#7) is now the *only* guard against a green scheduler run during an outage (commented there). The commit swallow is SYS-13 (#32). Rule for future call-site audits: look for `try/except` around `commit()` inside the `with get_session()` block, not just around the block.
- **Links:** SYS-03 (#4) comment · SYS-06 (#7) comment · SYS-13 (#32) new.

### 2026-09-16 — QA re-audit: EMITENNEWS serves its home page with HTTP 200 for missing articles (SRC-01)
- **Question / hypothesis:** `check_crawlers.py --website EMITENNEWS,KUMPARAN,OKEZONE --sample-size 3` → all three `OK 3/3`, but EMITENNEWS's `sehatkan-keuangan-ptpp-…` (a normal-looking slug) fell to trafilatura. Is the selector flaky, or is the page not an article?
- **Method:** fetched the URL with/without `?page=all` and a nonsense slug via `requests` with the project headers, printing `status`, `history`, final URL, `<link rel=canonical>`, `div.news-page-item` count. Then ran the real `_get_news_details` on it and fed the DTO to `SemanticImportanceFilter`. Rate check: first 15 + 25 random entries from the 2 child sitemaps; then the new reusable `scripts/experiments/probe_soft404.py --website EMITENNEWS --sample 30`. Selector quality: 30 random articles, site-selector vs trafilatura char counts, paragraph dump for one.
- **Result:** every missing slug → `200 hist=[307] final=https://www.emitennews.com/404 canonical=…/404`, title `Emitennews.com - Berita Emiten, Saham dan Investasi`, 184 `<p>`, no `news-page-item`. `_get_news_details` → DTO with 1 paragraph / 1,377 chars = the home-page index ticker (`IDXINDUST 1.03% IDXINFRA -0.22% …`); `is_important -> True`. Soft-404s: 2/40 in the first probe (the `lastmod 2026-09-20` unpublished stub + the awards page, both among the 15 newest), 0/30 random. Selector: 30/30 real articles extracted 1.9–22.9 k chars of distinct paragraphs; trafilatura returned ~40 % of the body (e.g. 3,121 vs 1,569 chars) — the selector is the better primary. `lastmod` vs publication date: **inconclusive** — article pages have no `datePublished`, `<time>` or date meta at all (12/12 sampled).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** SRC-01 stays closed; the crawler is right. The pipeline-level hole is `get_soup` accepting any 200 without looking at `response.url`/`history` → SYS-14 (#33); combined with SYS-01's "≥ 200 chars is usable" it saves ticker tape as an article body, and the newest-first queue fetches exactly these not-yet-published entries first. Run `probe_soft404.py` on the other healthy outlets before assuming this is EMITENNEWS-only. Don't try to verify EMITENNEWS dates from the article HTML.
- **Links:** SRC-01 (#12) comment · SYS-14 (#33) new · OPS-02 (#10) (health check counts these as `ok`) · `scripts/experiments/probe_soft404.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-13/SYS-14/DBT-07 fix verification
- **Question / hypothesis:** do the proposed fixes for the three newly-raised tickets actually close them, and does DBT-07's dedup migration handle the `entity_mentions.article_id` FK correctly (the ticket text only mentions `entity_mentions.sitemap_id` surviving, but `entity_mentions` also hard-FKs `articles.articles_id`)?
- **Method:** SYS-13 — removed the `try/except` around `session.commit()` in `save_sitemaps`; mocked-session unit tests assert a failing commit propagates and rolls back via `get_session()`. SYS-14 — added a redirect/canonical check to `get_soup` (`ERROR_PATH_PATTERN` matching `/404`, `/not-found`, `/error`, same as `probe_soft404.py`); unit tests patch `get_url` with fake responses (redirect-to-404, in-place error page via canonical, legit redirect to a real path). DBT-07 — wrote `scripts/migrate_articles_unique_sitemap.sql` (rank duplicates: entity_mentions-referenced row first, then `knowledge_extracted=1`, then lowest `articles_id`; abort via `RAISE EXCEPTION` if two duplicates for one sitemap are both referenced by `entity_mentions`) and switched `save_newsdetails` to `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (sitemap_id) DO NOTHING`; added `unique=True` to the ORM column. Ran the migration directly against `newsaggregator_test` (psql isn't on PATH in this shell, used psycopg2) with three synthetic scenarios: (A) plain duplicates, no entity_mentions; (B) one duplicate referenced by entity_mentions but not the lowest id / not knowledge_extracted; (C) two duplicates for the same sitemap both referenced by entity_mentions (the abort case). Verified `save_newsdetails` idempotency end-to-end against the same test DB, then cleaned up every row the verification created.
- **Result:** first migration draft had a `GroupingError` (`SELECT em.article_id ... GROUP BY a.sitemap_id` — selected a non-grouped column instead of `a.sitemap_id`); fixed before it touched real data. After the fix: scenario C aborted with `DBT-07: 1 sitemap_id(s) have entity_mentions referencing more than one duplicate articles row` and changed nothing; scenario A+B together went from 14→11 rows, scenario A kept the `knowledge_extracted=1` row, scenario B kept the entity_mentions-referenced row (no FK violation); final state back to the original 8/8. `save_newsdetails` called twice for the same `sitemap_id` left exactly 1 row. Full unit suite: 191 passed (23 new). `tests/integration` has 10 pre-existing failures unrelated to these fixes — traced to already-tracked DBT-06 (#29, uncommitted fixture rows breaking DS01/backfill cleanup) and SYS-10 (#28, `scripts/backfill_chunks.py` embeds with `nomic-embed-text`/768-dim instead of the pipeline's `EMBEDDING_MODEL_HF`, 401s against a gated HF model). Filed a duplicate of SYS-10 (DBT-08, #35) before noticing the existing ticket — closed it as a dup immediately after.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** the ticket's own suggested dedup rule ("keep lowest articles_id, entity_mentions/knowledge_extracted survive because they key off sitemap_id") undersells the risk — `entity_mentions.article_id` is a real FK to `articles.articles_id`, so a naive lowest-id dedup can throw a `ForeignKeyViolation` mid-migration. Always rank duplicate-row survivors by "is this row FK-referenced elsewhere" before falling back to an arbitrary tie-break, and add a pre-flight abort for the case where two duplicates are both referenced (don't guess). Confirmed `articles_sitemap_id_uq` now exists on `newsaggregator_test` — a fresh `newsaggregator` (prod) run of `scripts/migrate_articles_unique_sitemap.sql` is still needed and wasn't done here (out of scope — no prod access from this session).
- **Links:** SYS-13 (#32) fixed · SYS-14 (#33) fixed · DBT-07 (#34) fixed · DBT-06 (#29) hit, not fixed · SYS-10 (#28) hit, not fixed · `scripts/migrate_articles_unique_sitemap.sql`, `tests/unit/test_save_sitemaps_commit.py`, `tests/unit/test_soft404_detection.py`, `tests/unit/test_save_newsdetails_upsert.py`, `tests/integration/test_save_newsdetails_idempotent.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — QA re-audit of SYS-13/14/DBT-07 fixes
- **Question / hypothesis:** the dev's own verification entry above ("SYS-13/SYS-14/DBT-07 fix verification") claims all three are fixed, based on unit tests plus a dry run against `newsaggregator_test` only. Do the fixes hold up against the real code paths and the actual working `newsaggregator` DB, and does the DBT-07 rewrite (per-row `session.execute` instead of deferred `session.add`+flush) introduce anything new?
- **Method:** SYS-13 — read `save_sitemaps` and `SQLAlchemyClient.get_session` together to confirm the propagation chain (`session.commit()` → `get_session` rollback+re-raise → `crawl_sitemaps` → `log_crawl_failed` + re-raise); ran the full unit suite. SYS-14 — same static read of `get_soup`/`ERROR_PATH_PATTERN`, then live: called `RequestsPageLoader.get_soup()` directly against the exact bad URL from the original SYS-14 evidence (`emitennews.com/news/sehatkan-keuangan-ptpp-...`) and against 5 fresh real EMITENNEWS articles via `probe_soft404.py --website EMITENNEWS --sample 5`. DBT-07 — queried the real `newsaggregator` DB directly (`pg_indexes`, `SELECT COUNT(*)-COUNT(DISTINCT sitemap_id)`) instead of trusting the EXPERIMENTS.md note that prod wasn't touched; then, since the upsert code now calls `session.execute()` per row instead of `session.add()`, tested what happens when one row in a batch hits a real DB error: inserted a synthetic `sitemaps` row on `newsaggregator_test`, called `save_newsdetails([bad_row_with_nonexistent_sitemap_id, good_row])`, and checked whether the good row survived and whether the call raised.
- **Result:** SYS-13 — confirmed correct; no code issue. SYS-14 — confirmed correct and works live: `get_soup(".../sehatkan-keuangan-ptpp-...")` → `None` (redirect to `/404` detected), 5/5 fresh real articles unaffected. DBT-07 — **code is correct on `newsaggregator_test` but the migration was never applied to `newsaggregator`**: `pg_indexes` shows only `articles_pk`, and `COUNT(*)-COUNT(DISTINCT sitemap_id)` is still 617. Live-tested `save_newsdetails` against `newsaggregator` with an existing duplicated `sitemap_id` (76271): it raised `psycopg2.errors.InvalidColumnReference: there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification`, caught internally and logged, no row added — meaning **every** `save_newsdetails` call against the real DB currently no-ops. Then, on `newsaggregator_test` (where the index does exist), a batch of `[bad_row (FK violation), good_row]` produced:
  ```
  bad row  -> psycopg2.errors.ForeignKeyViolation (expected)
  good row -> psycopg2.errors.InFailedSqlTransaction: current transaction is aborted, commands ignored until end of transaction block
  save_newsdetails returned normally (no exception)
  rows for GOOD sitemap_id after batch: 0
  ```
  Postgres treats `COMMIT` on an already-aborted transaction as an implicit rollback, not an error, so `save_newsdetails` reports success while persisting nothing from the batch. The old `session.add()` + single deferred flush had the same all-or-nothing transaction semantics but the failure surfaced *at* `session.commit()`, uncaught by anything in the function, so it propagated to `_crawl_newsdetails_inner`'s `except Exception: logger.error("Error saving batch...")` — loud. The new per-row `try/except: log; continue` around `session.execute()` swallows it instead — silent, and logged as if it succeeded.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** don't accept "verified against `newsaggregator_test`" as evidence the fix is live — always check the actual working DB the pipeline reads from before treating a migration-dependent ticket as closed (`grep`/`psql` `pg_indexes` takes 10 seconds). Also: when rewriting a bulk save from ORM `session.add()`+deferred-flush to Core `session.execute()` per row inside a loop, either isolate each row in its own `session.begin_nested()` savepoint (as `save_sitemaps` already does) or re-raise on failure — catching per-row and calling a single `session.commit()` afterward silently discards the whole batch on Postgres if any one row poisons the transaction, because `COMMIT` after an aborted transaction is a no-op, not an error. Filed SYS-15 (#36) for this; commented on DBT-07 (#34) that its own "Done when" (prod duplicate count = 0) is unmet. Did not run the migration against `newsaggregator` myself (destructive to the working DB; needs explicit sign-off) and did not attempt to fix SYS-15 (this was a re-audit, not the fix).
- **Links:** SYS-13 (#32) confirmed fixed · SYS-14 (#33) confirmed fixed, verified live · DBT-07 (#34) comment — not actually fixed on `newsaggregator` · SYS-15 (#36) new.

FAISS L1 has 10,680 sitemap_ids with no article row, L2 9,497 (all VIVA 2026-06-02 — posted on SYS-08), L1 1,335 / L2 152 with no chunks row, 187 sitemaps with two L1 vectors (OPS-04). 1,035 duplicate `(sitemap_id, level, index)` chunk rows with distinct embedding_ids, VIVA 1,026 (DBT-09). `newsletter_digests` and `sitemaps.cluster_id` absent on both DBs; `digests/` never created; `get_stats()` → `{}` → `/api/health` = `ok` with zeros (OPS-05, OPS-06). `chunks.article_id` NULL on 101,359/101,359, `is_embedded` True on 11,889/11,889 (DBT-11). Extractor: same poison article selected 3 runs in a row, 3 Gemini calls, 0 marked (SYS-19). `mention_count` drift on 5/54 entities after 6 articles (SYS-20). `crawl_websites_in_batch(task='fulltext')` → `ok=2 failed=0`, no service call (SYS-21). `_get_keywords` → `["Badan","Gizi","Nasional","(BGN),",…]` (SYS-22). Web: all routes `async def` around blocking calls, no lock on lazy init (DBT-15); index loaded once, never reloaded (DBT-16). `crawl_log` write failures at DEBUG (OPS-07). Lesson for future audits: for every persisted field, `SELECT LEFT(col, 100)` on production once — the storage format is not what the type hint says. And a claim that a fix is "verified" must include the query result on the DB the pipeline actually uses, not the test DB. ### 2026-09-16 — SYS-15 fix: does a per-row savepoint stop a bad row from dropping the whole batch?
- **Question / hypothesis:** SYS-15's fix proposal was to wrap each row's `session.execute()` in its own `session.begin_nested()` savepoint, matching `save_sitemaps`' existing pattern, so a bad row (FK violation, etc.) rolls back only itself instead of poisoning the whole transaction. Does that actually fix the reproduced failure mode?
- **Method:** wrapped the `pg_insert(...).on_conflict_do_nothing(...)` + `session.execute(stmt)` in `save_newsdetails` in `with session.begin_nested():` inside the existing per-row `try/except BaseException`. Added `tests/integration/test_save_newsdetails_savepoint.py`, reproducing the exact SYS-15 evidence shape against `newsaggregator_test`: `save_newsdetails([bad_row (sitemap_id=-999999, FK violation), good_row (real sitemap_id)])`, then asserted the good row's `articles` count is 1. Ran it plus the full `tests/unit` and `tests/integration` suites (both before and after the change, via `git stash`, to separate pre-existing failures from anything the fix touched).
- **Result:** with the savepoint, the bad row's FK violation is caught and only its own nested transaction rolls back; the good row inserts and the final `session.commit()` persists it — new test passes. `tests/unit` (191 passed) and the existing `test_save_newsdetails_idempotent.py`/`test_save_newsdetails_upsert.py` are unaffected. `tests/integration` has 10 pre-existing failures (`test_backfill_pipeline.py`, `test_data_sources.py::TestSaveSitemaps::test_DS01_...`) — confirmed identical failure set with `git stash` on unmodified `dev`, i.e. unrelated to this change (matches the known "integration suite needs a clean `newsaggregator_test`" trap below).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** the `session.begin_nested()` savepoint pattern is the right fix for "per-row Core `session.execute()` inside a loop, single commit at the end" — apply it (or re-raise) any time this shape shows up elsewhere, not just in `save_newsdetails`. Confirmed via the actual failure mode (poisoned transaction), not just a mocked unit test.
- **Links:** SYS-15 (#36) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_articles_data_source.py` · `tests/integration/test_save_newsdetails_savepoint.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — QA re-verification of the SYS-15 savepoint fix
- **Question / hypothesis:** independently re-confirm the SYS-15 fix (ff0ae26) beyond the dev's own test — does the per-row savepoint hold up under orderings and repeats the dev's test didn't cover, and does the fix's precondition (the `articles_sitemap_id_uq` unique index from DBT-07) actually hold on the real `newsaggregator` DB, not just `newsaggregator_test`?
- **Method:** ran `tests/unit` (full suite) and `tests/integration` (full suite) as-is. Ran the dev's `tests/integration/test_save_newsdetails_savepoint.py` plus the related `test_save_newsdetails_idempotent.py` / `test_save_newsdetails_upsert.py`. Then wrote an independent script (not reusing the dev's test) against `newsaggregator_test` covering 4 scenarios `save_newsdetails` didn't have a test for: (1) bad row before good row (the issue's exact evidence order), (2) good row before bad row (reverse order), (3) bad row on both sides of a good row, (4) re-submitting the same batch a second time to check the savepoint fix didn't break `on_conflict_do_nothing` idempotency. Also ran a read-only check against the real `newsaggregator` DB (`pg_indexes`, `pg_constraint`) confirming `articles_sitemap_id_uq` and `articles_sitemap_fk` both exist there — no writes made to prod.
- **Result:** all 4 independent scenarios: good row persisted with `count=1` in every case, bad row's FK violation still logged via `logger.error` (failure visibility preserved), no duplicate row on re-submit, no leftover rows after cleanup. `tests/unit`: 191 passed. `tests/integration`: 10 failed / 34 passed / 1 skipped — the 10 failures are exactly `test_backfill_pipeline.py::*` (blocked by SYS-10, unrelated model auth) and `test_data_sources.py::TestSaveSitemaps::test_DS01_...` (the documented "leftover fixture rows" trap on `sitemaps`, unrelated to `articles`/`save_newsdetails`) — none touch the code path this fix changed. Prod `newsaggregator`: `articles_sitemap_id_uq` and `articles_sitemap_fk` both present, 11,889 article rows.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** SYS-15 is confirmed fixed — order of bad/good rows in a batch no longer matters, savepoint isolation is symmetric, and the fix doesn't regress `on_conflict_do_nothing` upsert idempotency. Closing #36. The pre-existing 10 integration failures are known traps (SYS-10, DS01 leftovers), not regressions — don't re-file them against SYS-15.
- **Links:** SYS-15 (#36) closed · same files as above.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-06 fix: does re-raising in `process_event` make a mid-run failure exit non-zero?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `process_event` caught `BaseException`, logged at INFO, and returned normally, so a scheduler always saw exit 0 no matter what failed underneath (confirmed live in the SYS-12 and SYS-03 entries above: `crawl_websites_in_batch`'s `RuntimeError` reached `process_event` and was swallowed there). Narrowing the catch to `Exception`, logging with `logger.exception` at ERROR, and re-raising should (a) let `KeyboardInterrupt`/`SystemExit` propagate untouched, (b) put a full traceback in the log at ERROR instead of a one-line INFO, and (c) make `python __main__.py` exit non-zero on any real failure.
- **Method:** 4 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_lambda_handler.py`, `init_crawler` patched so `crawl_websites_in_batch` raises/returns normally) covering: failure re-raises, failure is logged at ERROR with `exc_info` set, `KeyboardInterrupt` is neither caught nor logged, success returns normally. Then a live repro reusing SYS-03's DB-outage setup: `POSTGRES_DB_PORT=1 CRAWL_WEBSITES=KOMPAS CRAWL_TASK=sitemap venv\Scripts\python.exe __main__.py`, output redirected to a file (piping through `tail` first hid the real exit code — `$?` after a pipe is the last command's, not python's), exit code read separately.
- **Result:** unit 195 passed (191 + 4 new). Live: `ERROR … Failed to process task 'sitemap'` with full traceback (`RuntimeError: 1 of 1 sources failed: KOMPAS`) logged, then the same traceback re-raised out of `__main__.py`, **exit code 1**. Full `tests/integration` run: 10 failed / 34 passed / 1 skipped — identical to the documented SYS-10/DS01 baseline, none in the changed code path.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed; this was the last guard missing after SYS-03/SYS-07/SYS-12/SYS-13 — a mid-run failure is now loud in the log *and* exits non-zero. Did not add the ticket's optional `sources=N ok=N failed=[...] articles=N` summary line beyond what SYS-12 already logs (`Batch '{task}' finished: ok=N failed=N | failed: ...`) — no article-count plumbing exists yet (that's SYS-04, #5) and the ticket's own "Done when" only requires the exit code. When timing `$?` after a shell command, redirect to a file instead of piping through `tail`/`head` — the pipe's own exit code shadows the command you actually want.
- **Links:** SYS-06 (#7) fixed · `newscrawler/application/api/lambda_function/lambda_handler.py` · `tests/unit/test_lambda_handler.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-13 was already fixed; closed the stale GitHub issue
- **Question / hypothesis:** the P1 list still showed #32 (SYS-13) open. Given the "QA re-audit of SYS-13/14/DBT-07 fixes" entry above already says "SYS-13 — confirmed correct; no code issue", is there any code left to do, or just bookkeeping?
- **Method:** re-read `save_sitemaps` (`sql_alchemy_sitemap_data_source.py:141`) and `crawl_sitemaps` (`crawler_service_impl.py:41-56`) against the ticket's exact "Done when"; ran `tests/unit/test_save_sitemaps_commit.py`.
- **Result:** `session.commit()` has no surrounding try/except (commit 25868b1); a failed commit propagates through `get_session()` to `crawl_sitemaps`'s `except Exception: log_crawl_failed(...); raise`. 4/4 existing tests pass.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** no code change needed — the fix and its re-audit both landed 2026-09-16, the GitHub issue was just never closed. Closed #32 with a comment pointing at the fix commit and the re-audit entry. Lesson: an EXPERIMENTS.md "confirmed fixed" note doesn't close the GitHub issue by itself — check `gh issue view` state, don't assume it was closed just because the log says fixed.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-18 fix: does joining paragraphs at the write boundary stop the array-literal corruption?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `save_newsdetails` binds `NewsDetailsModel.extracted_text` (a `List[str]` of paragraphs) straight to the `articles.extracted_text` `text` column; psycopg2 adapts the list as a Postgres array literal instead of storing prose. Joining the paragraphs with `"\n\n"` before the value reaches the driver (matching how `crawler.py`'s trafilatura fallback already splits its own output on `"\n\n"`) should make new writes store clean prose. Existing rows still need a migration.
- **Method:** fixed both write paths that hand the raw list to the column (`save_newsdetails`'s `pg_insert(...).values(...)` and the otherwise-unused `NewsArticlesTable.__init__`, per the ticket's own "Where"). 5 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_extracted_text_persistence.py`, mocked session, asserting the compiled statement's bound `extracted_text` param) + 1 live integration test (`tests/integration/test_extracted_text_persistence.py`, real `newsaggregator_test`, paragraphs containing a comma and an embedded quote — the exact characters that trigger array-literal quoting). Wrote `scripts/migrate_extracted_text_prose.sql` for existing rows (rewrite via `array_to_string(extracted_text::text[], E'\n\n')`, reset `knowledge_extracted` for rows already extracted off the mangled text) and verified it against `newsaggregator_test` with 4 synthetic corrupted rows reproduced via raw psycopg2 (not hand-typed strings) covering: multi-paragraph with comma+quote, single paragraph, empty list (`'{}'`), and — found only by then checking real `newsaggregator` — an **unquoted first array element** (Postgres only quotes an element when it needs escaping, so a plain word like `CHINA` produces `{CHINA,"...",...}`, which the migration's first draft (`LIKE '{"%'`) missed; broadened to `LIKE '{%'`, matching the audit's own detection query). Before touching real data: read-only scope check on `newsaggregator` (11,889/11,889 rows affected, 6 with `knowledge_extracted=1`), a read-only dry run of the cast+join over all 11,889 rows, and a full JSON backup of the affected rows' `extracted_text`/`knowledge_extracted`/`knowledge_extracted_at` to `scratchpad/sys18_prod_backup_before_migration.json` — then asked the user for sign-off before applying.
- **Result:** unit 200 passed (195 + 5 new, including the fixed `NewsArticlesTable.__init__`); integration 35 passed / 10 failed / 1 skipped (same SYS-10/DS01 baseline as before, plus this ticket's own new passing test). Live round-trip: `'Paragraf pertama, dengan koma.\n\nParagraf kedua "dikutip" di sini.'` stored verbatim, not `{"..."}`. Test-DB migration dry run: all 4 scenarios rewritten correctly, idempotent on a second run. Applied to real `newsaggregator` with the user's sign-off: `extracted_text LIKE '{%'` count 11889 → 0, `knowledge_extracted=1` count 6 → 0, total `articles` row count unchanged at 11,889, sample row now reads as clean prose (`"DUNIA komputasi sedang berada di ambang revolusi besar..."` instead of `{"DUNIA komputasi..."`).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed, on the write path and on all existing rows. The `entity_mentions.context_snippet` artifacts (25/32 rows, noted in the original audit) are untouched — those were captured before this fix and are a historical record, not something this migration should silently rewrite. When writing a migration keyed on a string prefix, don't trust the pattern the ticket itself suggests without checking the real data first — the ticket's own "Done when" used `LIKE '{"%'`, which would have silently left 8 rows corrupted (the unquoted-first-element case only shows up by sampling actual production values, not by reasoning from the write-side bug alone).
- **Links:** SYS-18 (#39) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_articles_data_source.py`, `table.py`, `scripts/migrate_extracted_text_prose.sql`, `tests/unit/test_extracted_text_persistence.py`, `tests/integration/test_extracted_text_persistence.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-05 fix: does fixing the `except A or B` typo actually retry a ConnectionError?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `RequestsPageLoader.get_url` had `except requests.exceptions.ReadTimeout or requests.exceptions.ConnectionError:` in two places -- Python evaluates `A or B` on two class objects to `A` (the first truthy operand), so only `ReadTimeout` was ever caught; a `ConnectionError` fell through to the generic `except BaseException` and got exactly one attempt. Changing both to a tuple `except (A, B):` should make a `ConnectionError` retry `max_retries` times just like `ReadTimeout` already does.
- **Method:** 5 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_requests_page_loader.py`, `requests.get` patched): `ConnectionError` alone gets `max_retries + 1` attempts, `ReadTimeout` alone unchanged at `max_retries + 1`, a `ConnectionError` that succeeds on attempt 2 returns the response, an alternating `ConnectionError`/`ReadTimeout` sequence across the full retry budget never falls through mid-sequence, and an unrelated exception (`ValueError`) still gets exactly 1 attempt (not retried at all -- confirms the fix didn't widen the catch beyond the two named types). Then a live repro: `RequestsPageLoader().get_url("http://127.0.0.1:1/unreachable")` with `max_retries` set to 2 for speed, hitting a real closed port (guaranteed `ConnectionError`, no mocking).
- **Result:** unit 205 passed (200 + 5 new). Live: 2 `"Retry N to connect"` log lines before giving up (was 0 before the fix -- the old code would have hit the generic except immediately, no retry logged at all), ~6s elapsed (2 retries × 2s connect timeout + a few round trips), confirming the retry loop actually re-entered on a real socket-level `ConnectionError`, not just the mocked kind.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed for the required behavior. Deliberately did **not** apply the ticket's optional "while there" suggestion (switch to `requests.Session` + `HTTPAdapter(max_retries=Retry(...))` and delete the hand-rolled loop) -- that changes retry semantics (status-code-triggered retries, different backoff, connection pooling) beyond what the ticket's own "Done when" requires, and swapping the whole retry mechanism deserves its own ticket/review rather than riding along with a one-line typo fix. `HeadlessPageLoader` (selenium-based) has no equivalent bug -- no retry loop, no `or`-exception pattern -- and is out of scope for this ticket.
- **Links:** SYS-05 (#6) fixed · `newscrawler/core/page_loader/requests_page_loader.py` · `tests/unit/test_requests_page_loader.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-04 fix: does returning a real saved-count from _crawl_newsdetails_inner fix crawl_log.article_count?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `crawl_newsdetails` declared `total_saved = 0` but never incremented it, and `_crawl_newsdetails_inner` returned nothing (implicit `None`), so `log_crawl_complete(log_id, total_saved)` always logged 0 for `task='full_text'` regardless of how many articles were actually saved. Threading real counters through the batch loop and returning the final saved count should fix it, and tracking attempted/extracted/passed_filter alongside it gives the "is this scraper rotting" ratio the ticket calls out as the most useful signal.
- **Method:** `_crawl_newsdetails_inner` now tracks `attempted` (sitemaps loaded), `extracted_total` (articles the crawler returned), `passed_filter_total` (survived the semantic filter), `saved_total` (sitemap_ids the chunk/embed step actually returned as represented — same "attempted persistence" semantics the sitemap task already uses, not a DB-confirmed insert count), logs all four in one summary line, and returns `saved_total`; `crawl_newsdetails` passes that into `log_crawl_complete` instead of the dead `total_saved = 0`. 5 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_crawl_log_article_count.py`, mocked repo/crawler/filter): non-zero count on a normal save, correctly-zero when nothing is extracted, correctly-zero when everything is filtered out, correct sum across 9 batches (25 sitemaps, batch_size=3), and correctly-zero when `save_chunk_data` returns no embedded ids even though articles passed the filter. Then 1 live integration test (`tests/integration/test_crawl_log_article_count_live.py`) using the **real** `SQLAlchemyArticleDataSource.log_crawl_start/log_crawl_complete` against `newsaggregator_test` (crawler/filter/chunk-save mocked out to avoid network and FAISS writes), reading the actual `crawl_log` row back afterward — the exact query the ticket's "Done when" specifies.
- **Result:** unit 210 passed (205 + 5 new); the legacy `tests/test_crawler_service.py` (4 tests, pre-existing, exercises the same method) still passes unaffected. Live: `SELECT article_count, status FROM crawl_log WHERE website='SYS04_TEST' AND task='full_text'` → `(1, 'completed')`, proving the value survives the full round trip through the service layer into a real Postgres row, not just a mocked return value. Full `tests/integration`: 36 passed (35 + this one) / 10 failed — same pre-existing SYS-10/DS01 baseline.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed. Did not add new `crawl_log` columns for the attempted/extracted/passed_filter breakdown (the ticket's "Done when" only requires `article_count`; a schema change for the other three numbers would need its own migration and is better done together with OPS-05's schema-tracking work rather than bundled here) — they're logged as a rich INFO summary line instead, matching the project's existing `Batch '{task}' finished: ok=N failed=N` pattern (SYS-12). `saved_total` counts articles handed to persistence (chunk-embedded + `save_newsdetails_data` called), not confirmed-committed rows — same "attempted" semantics `crawl_log.article_count` already uses for `task='sitemap'` per the SYS-07 re-audit note above; a per-row DB failure inside `save_newsdetails` (SYS-15's savepoint path) would still count as "saved" here even if that one row's insert failed. That gap is inherent to the existing counting convention across both tasks, not something this ticket introduced.
- **Links:** SYS-04 (#5) fixed · `newscrawler/domain/services/crawler_service_impl.py` · `tests/unit/test_crawl_log_article_count.py`, `tests/integration/test_crawl_log_article_count_live.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-07 (reopened) fix: does routing task=all through the DB filter stop the re-crawl?
- **Question / hypothesis:** the 2026-09-16 QA re-audit found that `CrawlerAPI.crawl_website("all")` passed `crawl_sitemaps`' own return value (every currently-listed sitemap, existing rows included — `save_sitemaps` returns ids for matched-existing entries too, not just new ones) straight into `crawl_newsdetails(specific_sitemaps=...)`, bypassing `load_all_sitemaps`' "no articles row yet AND cooldown elapsed" filter entirely. Every URL a site's sitemap lists would be re-fetched and re-saved on every `task=all` run. The recommended fix — `crawl_website("all")` calls `crawl_newsdetails(website_name)` with no `specific_sitemaps`, falling through to the same DB-filtered path `task=full_text` already uses — should make a second `all` run on an unchanged sitemap add zero new articles. Separately, `crawl_log.article_count` for `task='sitemap'` recorded `len(sitemaps_dtos)` ("entries listed"), not the real new-row count that `save_sitemaps` already computes internally but never returns.
- **Method:** two-line fix in `crawler_api.py` (see diff). For the count, rather than changing `save_sitemaps`' return *contents* (still every entry, new and existing, with ids populated — no test or caller actually needed that to change, and narrowing it would be a surprising behavior change for a generically-named "save these and give me their identity" method), added `SitemapSaveResult` (`newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/model/news_data_model.py`), a `list` subclass carrying a `.new_count` attribute so `len()`/iteration/indexing are identical to a plain list for every existing caller, threaded through `SQLAlchemySitemapDataSource.save_sitemaps` → `DataFlowRepositoryImpl.save_sitemap_data` (which previously rebuilt a plain list via a comprehension, silently dropping any such attribute — fixed to re-wrap) → `CrawlerServiceImpl.crawl_sitemaps` (`getattr(sitemaps_dtos, "new_count", len(...))`, defensive default for any caller/mock returning a plain list). Verified first that nothing besides the exact line being removed consumed `crawl_sitemaps`' return value (`grep -rn "crawl_sitemaps("` — only the `task=="sitemap"` discard and the `task=="all"` bypass), and that no existing test asserted on `save_sitemaps`' return *contents* including pre-existing entries (only on DB row counts via `SELECT`) — both had to hold before this design was safe.
  Tests: 2 unit (`tests/unit/test_task_all_routing.py`) asserting `crawl_website("all")` never passes `specific_sitemaps`, even when `crawl_sitemaps` returns a 5,000-entry list. 8 unit (`tests/unit/test_sitemap_new_count.py`) covering `SitemapSaveResult`'s list-compatibility, `save_sitemaps` computing the right count with/without a re-listed existing entry, `DataFlowRepositoryImpl` preserving the count across DTO conversion (and falling back gracefully for a plain-list return), and `crawl_sitemaps` passing the right number (not the total) to `log_crawl_complete`. 2 live integration tests (`tests/integration/test_task_all_no_recrawl.py`) against `newsaggregator_test`: (a) direct datasource repro of the QA re-audit's original scenario, now using the fixed two-step flow (`save_sitemap` then `load_all_sitemaps` instead of using `save_sitemap`'s return as `specific_sitemaps`) — confirms 0 sitemaps are eligible on run 2; (b) full `CrawlerAPI.crawl_website(task="all")` end-to-end with a fake repo (real sitemap/article persistence against the test DB, no FAISS/embedding/network) and a mocked `Crawler`, asserting `batch_crawling_details` is not even called on run 2.
- **Result:** unit 220 passed (210 + 10 new). Live: both new integration tests pass with the fix; **before** restoring the fix (git-stashed just `crawler_api.py` to reproduce the pre-fix state), test (a) still passed — it doesn't exercise `CrawlerAPI` at all, only the underlying primitives, which were never the buggy part — but test (b) correctly **failed** (`batch_crawling_details` was called with a real news item on the supposed-to-be-empty run 2), proving it actually catches the regression rather than trivially passing either way. Restored the fix, both pass again. Full `tests/integration`: 38 passed (36 + 2 new) / 10 failed — same pre-existing SYS-10/DS01 baseline.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed on both fronts. When a fix's claim is "this list no longer flows where it used to," write at least one test through the actual call site being changed (here, `CrawlerAPI`), not only through the lower-level primitives it calls — a test built entirely from "first principles" of the fixed design can pass regardless of whether the routing bug is present, which would have been a false sense of coverage. Confirmed this the hard way by deliberately reverting the one-line fix and watching which of the two new integration tests actually turned red. `SitemapSaveResult`'s "looks like a plain list to every existing caller" property was verified, not assumed, before relying on it — same lesson as the DBT-07 dedup migration: check what already depends on a method's current output shape before changing what flows through it.
- **Links:** SYS-07 (#8) fixed (again) · `newscrawler/application/api/crawler_api.py`, `newscrawler/domain/services/crawler_service_impl.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/model/news_data_model.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_sitemap_data_source.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/repositories/dataflow/data_flow_repository_impl.py` · `tests/unit/test_task_all_routing.py`, `tests/unit/test_sitemap_new_count.py`, `tests/integration/test_task_all_no_recrawl.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-16 fix: does a crawl_status terminal marker stop the 73% re-fetch-forever loop?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `load_all_sitemaps`' only signal was `last_crawl_attempt` + cooldown, so a sitemap the semantic filter permanently rejects (same headline/body, same `INTERESTS` list, will fail again) looked identical to one whose extraction merely came back empty this run (a layout fix or a later attempt could still rescue it) -- both got re-selected every 7 days forever. Adding `sitemaps.crawl_status` (`pending`/`rejected_filter`/`empty_extraction`/`saved`) and excluding `rejected_filter` from `load_all_sitemaps` should stop the permanent-rejection half of the 73,364-row queue from cycling, while leaving the transient half retryable.
- **Method:** migration `scripts/migrate_sitemap_crawl_status.sql` (additive column + CHECK constraint + index; backfills `saved` for rows that already have an `articles` row). `SQLAlchemySitemapDataSource.mark_sitemaps_status(ids, status)` (bound parameter, not interpolated -- validated against a `VALID_CRAWL_STATUSES` set before hitting the DB) threaded through `NewsDataSource` / `DataFlowRepository(Impl)`. `_crawl_newsdetails_inner`'s filter loop now classifies each rejected `NewsDetailsDTO` by whether its `extracted_text` was non-blank (`rejected_filter`) or blank (`empty_extraction`) *before* checking the filter's verdict -- a filter *exception* leaves the status untouched (`pending`, retries normally, since an error isn't a deterministic decision). Successfully-embedded-and-saved articles get `crawl_status='saved'` too, for observability (the `articles`-row LEFT JOIN is what actually gates `load_all_sitemaps`, independent of this). `load_all_sitemaps`'s SQL gained one `AND crawl_status != 'rejected_filter'` clause.
  Backfill for the existing 73,364 rows: `scripts/backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py` batch-encodes each row's stored *headline* (the body was never saved -- that's the whole symptom) against the filter's pre-encoded interest embeddings, classifying by the same `>= threshold` rule `is_important()` uses, then a plain string-blank check for `empty_extraction` vs `rejected_filter`. Verified the batching *before* trusting it: `classify_batch()`'s output on 4 headlines matched `is_important()` called individually on each, exactly, side by side (`[True, False, False, True]` both ways) -- batching a matrix op for ~73k rows instead of ~73k individual `model.encode()` calls is what makes this finish in a reasonable time, but only if it's provably equivalent, not just faster.
  Tests: 5 unit (`tests/unit/test_sitemap_terminal_state.py`) covering each classification branch (off-topic-with-text, empty-text, saved, a mixed batch classifying each sitemap independently, and a filter exception leaving status untouched) with a mocked repo. 4 live (`tests/integration/test_sitemap_terminal_state_live.py`) against `newsaggregator_test`: a `rejected_filter` row stays excluded past the cooldown while an `empty_extraction` row and a still-inside-cooldown `pending` row are correctly included/excluded on their own terms; a never-attempted row is eligible; `mark_sitemaps_status` rejects an invalid status string; `crawl_status='saved'` alone (no matching `articles` row) does *not* gate `load_all_sitemaps` -- only the join does -- documented explicitly so a future reader doesn't assume `saved` is a second exclusion mechanism.
  Real-DB steps, in order, each verified before the next: (1) read-only scope check (73,364 eligible, matches the ticket's own audit numbers exactly); (2) schema migration applied to `newsaggregator_test` first, then `newsaggregator` (11,889 rows correctly backfilled to `saved`); (3) full JSON backup of the 73,364 eligible rows (`sitemap_id`, `sources`, `headline`, `crawl_status`) to `scratchpad/sys16_prod_backup_before_backfill.json`; (4) classification backfill run for real on `newsaggregator` (no `--website` filter): 46,751 → `rejected_filter`, 0 → `empty_extraction` (no sitemap in the batch had a literally blank headline -- the *body* was what failed to extract, not the headline, so this bucket is structurally near-empty for a headline-only backfill; not a bug), 26,613 left `pending`.
- **Result:** unit 225 passed (220 + 5 new). Live: all 4 new integration tests pass; full `tests/integration`: 42 passed (38 + 4) / 10 failed -- same pre-existing SYS-10/DS01 baseline. **Caught before finalizing, not after:** checking the per-source breakdown of the just-applied backfill showed VIVA (still-open SYS-08: healthy extractor, 0 articles saved, root cause unknown) got 21,788 of its 39,262 sitemaps marked `rejected_filter` purely from headline text -- but if SYS-08 turns out to be a systemic bug that made *every* VIVA extraction look empty (not a topic problem), this backfill would have permanently locked those exact rows out of ever being retried once that bug is fixed, before anyone understood what the bug even was. Reverted all 21,788 VIVA rows back to `pending` (`UPDATE sitemaps SET crawl_status='pending' WHERE sources='VIVA' AND crawl_status='rejected_filter'`) and added an explicit `CAUTION` block to the backfill script's docstring so a future re-run doesn't repeat this. Final real-DB state: `pending`=62,885 (includes all 39,262 VIVA rows, pending SYS-08), `rejected_filter`=24,963, `saved`=11,889 -- retry queue drops from 73,364 to 48,401.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed for every source except VIVA, which is deliberately left as-is pending SYS-08. Lesson for any future bulk classification/backfill: check the per-source breakdown against every *currently open, related* ticket before finalizing -- a heuristic that's reasonable in aggregate can be actively harmful for the one source whose root cause is still being investigated, and the ticket's own text calling out "VIVA 37,381 (see SYS-08)" was the clue that should have prompted checking this *before* running the backfill, not after. Re-run `scripts/backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py --website VIVA` once SYS-08 resolves. Added the corresponding `OPERATIONS.md` query and `CLAUDE.md` migration-order entry.
- **Links:** SYS-16 (#37) fixed (VIVA excepted, pending SYS-08 (#26)) · `scripts/migrate_sitemap_crawl_status.sql`, `scripts/backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`, `newscrawler/domain/services/crawler_service_impl.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/{sql_alchemy_sitemap_data_source.py,table.py}`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/news_data_source.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/repositories/dataflow/data_flow_repository_impl.py`, `newscrawler/domain/repositories/data_flow_repository/data_flow_repository.py` · `tests/unit/test_sitemap_terminal_state.py`, `tests/integration/test_sitemap_terminal_state_live.py` · backup `scratchpad/sys16_prod_backup_before_backfill.json` (session-local).

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-08 fix: does fixing reporter=None let VIVA save articles?
- **Question / hypothesis:** the ticket's own deep-audit evidence already narrowed this to candidate (b) -- article INSERT failing, not the filter (candidate a) or `save_chunk_data` (candidate c) -- because 9,497 VIVA `sitemap_id`s have `chunks` rows (proof they passed the filter and were embedded) but 0 `articles` rows. Its suggested next step was to run a real VIVA crawl through the now-fixed pipeline (SYS-03/15/DBT-07 all landed since the June 2 backfill that produced this data) and log the outcome per article. Did it still fail, and if so, exactly where?
- **Method:** fetched 5 real VIVA sitemaps with existing `chunks` rows and no `articles` row directly from `newsaggregator`, built them into `SitemapDTO`s, and called the real `CrawlerServiceImpl.crawl_newsdetails("VIVA", specific_sitemaps=...)` against the **real** `newsaggregator` DB (not `_test` -- deliberately, since this is a live diagnostic of production data, matching the ticket's own "run 5 VIVA sitemaps" instruction) with real network fetches, no mocking.
- **Result:** immediate, unambiguous log line: `Error saving batch N for VIVA: 1 validation error for NewsDetailsModel / reporter / Input should be a valid list [type=list_type, input_value=None, input_type=NoneType]`. `attempted=50 extracted=50 passed_filter=50 saved=0` (ran the full 50, not just 5, since the first 5 all failed identically). Traced it: `NewsDetailsModel.reporter: List[str] = None` -- a Python default, not `Optional[List[str]]`, which pydantic v2 rejects for an actual `None`. `VivaCrawler` has no `_get_reporter_from_text` override; `Crawler` is a plain class (not `abc.ABC`), so `@abstractmethod` doesn't enforce anything, and the inherited base method body is just `pass` (implicit `None`). Grepped every crawler file for the override: only two are missing it -- `viva_crawler.py` and `tribun_crawler.py`. TRIBUN is on the `HEALTHY` list in `__main__.py`; confirmed directly (`TribunCrawler()._get_reporter_from_text(soup)` → `None`) that it has the identical defect, but it has 0 sitemap rows in the DB yet (never actually crawled since being added to `HEALTHY`), so there's no production symptom to point at -- filed as its own ticket, SRC-09 (#57), rather than folding it into SYS-08 (the underlying pydantic-typing bug is shared, but TRIBUN's own fix additionally needs a real byline selector, which is separate implementation work; the discovering-a-bug-while-working-on-another rule applies even when the mechanism is shared).
  Fix: `NewsDetailsModel.reporter` → `Optional[List[str]] = None`; added a real `VivaCrawler._get_reporter_from_text` (found the byline markup live: `div.main-content-author > ul > li > a`, checked across 3 different VIVA categories -- bisnis, gaya-hidup, berita/nasional -- all consistent). 5 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_news_details_model_reporter.py`) confirming `reporter=None`/omitted no longer raises, `[]` and a populated list still work, and a genuinely wrong type (a bare string) is still rejected (the fix widened nullability, not type-checking generally). 5 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_viva_crawler.py`) for the new selector: single author, multiple authors, missing author element (→ `[]`, never `None`), author element present with no link, blank name skipped.
  Re-ran the exact same 50-sitemap live repro against `newsaggregator` after the fix (no other change) to prove it, not just the unit tests in isolation.
- **Result (post-fix):** `Full-text crawl finished for VIVA: attempted=50 extracted=50 passed_filter=50 saved=50`; `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM articles WHERE sitemap_id = ANY(...)` for the same 50 targeted ids → 50. Ticket's "Done when" (≥50 sitemaps, ≥1 article) exceeded outright (50/50). Full unit suite: 235 passed (225 + 10 new). `tests/integration`: 42 passed / 10 failed -- same pre-existing SYS-10/DS01 baseline, unrelated.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed for the specific 50 targeted sitemaps; the remaining ~39,212 VIVA sitemaps (most needing a fresh embed, not just the already-embedded recovery path this test exercised) are not yet backfilled -- that's `scripts/backfill_missing_articles.py --website VIVA`, a separate, much longer-running operation, left for the user to trigger explicitly rather than run unprompted as part of closing this ticket. Lesson: `Crawler` not being an `abc.ABC` means "forgot to override an abstract method" is a silent, permanent, 100%-failure-rate bug class with zero signal from `check_crawlers.py` (which never exercises the save path -- that gap is OPS-02) -- the one-line `grep -L` sweep across every crawler file that found SRC-09 in ten seconds should become a standing check, not a one-off. Also: writing to the real `newsaggregator` DB directly (not `_test`) for a live diagnostic is appropriate when the bug can only be reproduced against real historical data (synthetic rows wouldn't have the "already embedded in FAISS from June 2" precondition that made this repro fast and cheap) -- but it's still a real write, so keep the blast radius small (5→50 targeted rows, not the whole queue) until the fix is proven.
- **Links:** SYS-08 (#26) fixed · SRC-09 (#57) filed, not fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/model/news_details_model.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/viva/viva_crawler.py` · `tests/unit/test_news_details_model_reporter.py`, `tests/unit/test_viva_crawler.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-17 fix: does SQL-based dedup repair the FAISS-orphan chunks?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `save_chunk_data` deduped on FAISS docstore membership (`_embed_batch`'s `existing_ids`) instead of the `chunks` table -- FAISS is `save_local`'d to disk before the SQL commit, so if that commit ever failed (or was skipped), FAISS kept the vectors and every later attempt saw "already embedded" and skipped the SQL insert again, permanently. Production symptom: 152 WARTAEKONOMI `articles` rows with vectors in both `NEWS_L1`/`NEWS_L2` and zero `chunks` rows. Switching the dedup source to `SQLAlchemyChunkDataSource.get_existing_sitemap_ids` and having `ChunkFAISSDatasource` recover the *real* embedding_id for anything already in the index (instead of `None`) should make this self-healing, and a repair pass reusing `articles.extracted_text` (now clean prose since SYS-18) should fix the existing 152 without a network re-crawl.
- **Method:** `SQLAlchemyChunkDataSource.get_existing_sitemap_ids(ids)` (new) queries `chunks` directly. `DataFlowRepositoryImpl.save_chunk_data` now filters the input against that set *before* calling `chunk_faiss_datasource.save()` at all -- a sitemap already in `chunks` is skipped entirely (no re-embed attempt, no re-insert); everything else goes through FAISS as before. `ChunkFAISSDatasource._get_existing_embedding_ids` (replaces `_get_existing_sitemap_ids`) now maps `(sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index) -> docstore key`, not just a `sitemap_id` set, so `_embed_batch` can attach the *real* embedding_id to an already-present chunk via `dataclasses.replace` instead of returning it with `embedding_id=None` -- keying on all three fields (not just sitemap_id) matters because one article has multiple L2 chunks, each needing its own correct id.
  Rewrote `tests/unit/test_save_chunk_data.py` -- its 4 existing tests encoded the *old* buggy assumption verbatim (`embedding_id=None` == "already has a SQL row, don't re-insert"), which is exactly what SYS-17 fixes; kept the still-valid Bug A guarantee (all sitemap_ids returned) while replacing the mechanism under test. Added `tests/unit/test_chunk_faiss_embedding_recovery.py` (7 tests: key-based lookup, real-id recovery, mixed batches, multiple chunks per sitemap matched independently) and `tests/unit/test_sqlalchemy_chunk_data_source.py` (3 tests). Then a **live** integration test (`tests/integration/test_chunk_faiss_orphan_repair_live.py`) using a **real** `ChunkFAISSDatasource` (real embedding model, real FAISS ops) pointed at a temp directory (monkeypatched `VECTOR_DB_NAME_L1/L2`, never touches production vectors) plus real `newsaggregator_test`: embeds a chunk into FAISS only (simulating the exact bug precondition), confirms 0 `chunks` rows, then runs the real `save_chunk_data` and confirms the row now exists *with the same embedding_id FAISS already had* (no duplicate vector) -- plus a second test confirming a genuinely-already-saved sitemap is never touched again.
  Repair: `scripts/backfill_orphan_chunks.py` -- finds `articles` rows with no `chunks` row, re-splits `articles.extracted_text` back into paragraphs (`.split("\n\n")`, the exact inverse of SYS-18's join), re-chunks via the real `HierarchicalChunker`, and calls the fixed `save_chunk_data`. Verified against `newsaggregator_test` first with a synthetic orphan (real FAISS embed, no SQL row, temp index dir): repaired on first run, correctly reported 0 orphans and made no further FAISS/SQL calls on a second run (idempotent).
- **Result:** unit 247 passed (235 + 12 new: 6 rewritten `test_save_chunk_data.py` + 7 embedding-recovery + 3 sqlalchemy-datasource, net +12 after replacing 4 old tests with 6 new ones). Live (temp FAISS + `newsaggregator_test`): both new tests passed with the real embedding model. `tests/integration` overall: 44 passed (42 + 2) / 10 failed -- same pre-existing SYS-10/DS01 baseline. **Real production repair** (with the user's sign-off, dry-run first): `scripts/backfill_orphan_chunks.py` against `newsaggregator` (no `--website` filter) -- `repaired=152 empty=0 failed=0`. Ticket's exact "Done when" query: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM articles a WHERE NOT EXISTS (chunks) ` → **0** (was 152).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed and the existing production orphans repaired. **Found while verifying the repair, filed separately (not fixed):** `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks WHERE embedding_id IS NULL` → 142, across 26 distinct sitemap_ids, all `chunk_status='pending_embedding'`, all created in one ~10-minute window on 2026-06-01 (the day *before* the SYS-17 incident) -- confirmed none of these are new (none dated within the last hour, i.e. not created by this session's repair run). This is the mirror-image bug: a `chunks` row that exists but was never actually embedded into FAISS. Filed as DBT-17 (#58) rather than investigated further here, since it's a different write path/failure mode than SYS-17's (FAISS-has-it-SQL-doesn't vs. SQL-has-it-FAISS-doesn't) and deserves its own root-cause pass. Lesson: after fixing one direction of a "two stores must agree" bug, always check the OTHER direction on real data before declaring done -- the two are easy to conflate but need separate root causes and separate fixes.
- **Links:** SYS-17 (#38) fixed · DBT-17 (#58) filed, not fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/repositories/dataflow/data_flow_repository_impl.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/faiss/chunk_faiss_data_source.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_chunk_data_source.py` · `scripts/backfill_orphan_chunks.py` · `tests/unit/test_save_chunk_data.py` (rewritten), `tests/unit/test_chunk_faiss_embedding_recovery.py`, `tests/unit/test_sqlalchemy_chunk_data_source.py`, `tests/integration/test_chunk_faiss_orphan_repair_live.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — SYS-19 fix: does attempt tracking stop a poison article from burning a Gemini call forever?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `get_articles_pending_extraction` selected the same newest-N `knowledge_extracted=0` rows every run with no way to distinguish "never tried" from "tried and failed repeatedly" -- an unparseable Gemini response, or an extraction whose shape crashes `_process_extraction`, left `knowledge_extracted=0` forever and got re-selected (and re-billed against the daily Gemini quota) on every subsequent run. Adding `knowledge_extract_attempts`/`last_extract_error`, excluding exhausted rows from the query, and wrapping each article's entity writes + `mark_article_extracted` in one transaction (so a partial failure doesn't leave some mentions committed while the article stays pending) should close all three of the ticket's "Done when" criteria.
- **Method:** migration (`scripts/migrate_knowledge_extract_attempts.sql`) adds the two columns + an index; `KNOWLEDGE_EXTRACT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default 3) new constant. `get_articles_pending_extraction` gained `AND knowledge_extract_attempts < :max_attempts` and reordered to `attempts ASC, posted_at DESC`. New `record_extraction_failure(article_id, error_msg)` (its own commit, deliberately never passed a session, so it survives even when the same article's atomic extraction session just rolled back). `upsert_entity`/`insert_entity_mention`/`mark_article_extracted` now accept an optional `session` -- when given, they execute on it without committing, so `KnowledgeExtractor` can drive one transaction per article via the new `atomic_extraction_session()`. `_valid_dicts()` filters out non-dict entries before `_process_extraction` touches them (Gemini can return a list of bare strings for one of the three entity lists while still producing overall-valid JSON; `person.get("name")` on a `str` used to crash the whole article). `_call_gemini` now requests `response_mime_type: "application/json"` and explicitly guards `response.text is None` (a safety-blocked response) with its own log message, though this path was already non-crashing via the existing broad `except Exception`.
  Rewrote `tests/unit/test_knowledge_extractor.py` (19 tests, +5 over the original 14) covering: the prose-prefix and `text=None` repros from the ticket's own evidence section (confirmed non-crashing, as they already were), the JSON mime-type request, non-dict entities being skipped rather than crashing, a failed extraction recording an attempt, a mid-article crash recording an attempt via the outer `except` and never calling `mark_article_extracted`, and that every entity/mention write inside `_process_extraction` carries the *same* shared session. New `tests/unit/test_sqlalchemy_knowledge_data_source.py` (10 tests) for the query's attempt-filter/ordering, `record_extraction_failure`'s truncation and its own-commit behavior, and the session-aware vs. standalone-commit branches of `upsert_entity`/`mark_article_extracted`/`insert_entity_mention`.
  Live (`tests/integration/test_knowledge_extraction_poison_pill_live.py`, real `newsaggregator_test`, Gemini mocked): (1) atomic rollback -- two articles in one batch, the first's processing crashes mid-way (a `get_l1_chunk_id_for_article` failure after its entity was already upserted in the same session); confirms 0 `entity_mentions` *and* 0 leftover `entities` row for the crashed article (the whole transaction rolled back, not just the mention), while the second, good article in the same batch still gets marked extracted -- proving per-article atomicity doesn't regress per-batch isolation. (2) exhaustion -- calls `record_extraction_failure` 3 times directly against a real inserted article and confirms `get_articles_pending_extraction` excludes it afterward, having confirmed it was eligible beforehand.
- **Result:** unit 262 passed (247 + 15: net +5 from the rewritten knowledge_extractor file, +10 new sqlalchemy-datasource tests). Live: both new tests passed on 3 repeated runs. **Hit and fixed a genuine flake first:** an earlier draft of the exhaustion test called the real `extract_all()` in a loop with `batch_size=1`, assuming a far-future `posted_at` would make the test's own row win every tie-break -- but the shared `newsaggregator_test` DB turned out to carry *multiple* other leftover pending rows with similarly-future dates (the documented "integration suite needs a clean newsaggregator_test" trap, worse than previously characterized), so each `batch_size=1` call picked a *different* row nondeterministically and the test's own row was sometimes excluded from its own exhaustion loop. Fixed by testing `get_articles_pending_extraction`'s exclusion directly (call `record_extraction_failure` 3 times, then query) instead of driving it through `extract_all()`'s batch selection at all -- the orchestration wiring itself is already covered by the mocked unit tests, so the live test only needed to prove the real SQL query works, not re-prove the wiring against a polluted shared table. Applied the migration to real `newsaggregator` (additive, no backfill needed -- all 11,939 existing articles default to `attempts=0`).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed on all three "Done when" criteria. The "far-future `posted_at` for test isolation" trick is fragile against a shared, cumulatively-polluted integration DB the moment *more than one* test uses it -- prefer testing a query's filter/ordering directly (call the SQL-facing method with a large limit and assert on membership) over trying to win a natural ordering race in a table you don't fully control. Did not attempt to live-test the `response_mime_type` config against the real Gemini API (would need a real key and would burn real quota just to confirm an SDK parameter name); confirmed instead via the SDK's own `GenerateContentConfig` object accepting the field, and via a unit test asserting it's passed on every call.
- **Links:** SYS-19 (#40) fixed · `newscrawler/core/knowledge_extractor.py`, `newscrawler/core/constants.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_knowledge_data_source.py`, `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/table.py` · `scripts/migrate_knowledge_extract_attempts.sql` · `tests/unit/test_knowledge_extractor.py` (rewritten), `tests/unit/test_sqlalchemy_knowledge_data_source.py`, `tests/integration/test_knowledge_extraction_poison_pill_live.py`.

### 2026-09-16 — OPS-01: does scheduling check_crawlers.py plus a Telegram alert actually reach a real device?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `check_crawlers.py` already supports `--quiet`/`--log-db`/exit codes/JSON reports (all built for exactly this ticket already) but nothing invokes it on a schedule, so breakage is found by a human noticing missing articles, sometimes weeks later. Wrapping it in a small script that also sends a notification on failure, then registering that as a Windows Task Scheduler job, should close the gap without needing to touch `check_crawlers.py` itself.
- **Method:** new `scripts/notify_telegram.py` (`send(message) -> bool`, Telegram Bot API, never raises -- missing config or a network error both just log and return `False`) and `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py` (subprocess-runs `check_crawlers.py --quiet --log-db`, sends a Telegram message with the summary only on nonzero exit, propagates the same exit code). User chose Telegram over email/Windows-toast and asked for the scheduled task to be created now rather than just handed over as a command; they didn't have bot credentials ready yet, so built everything against `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` env vars that are currently unset (notifications degrade to a logged no-op, confirmed live below) rather than blocking the whole ticket on that setup step.
  9 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_notify_telegram.py`, `tests/unit/test_run_scheduled_health_check.py`, both loaded via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` matching `test_check_crawlers.py`'s existing pattern for testing a `scripts/*.py` file with no package `__init__.py`) covering: missing-token/missing-chat-id/network-exception/non-200 all return `False` without raising; a healthy run sends nothing; an unhealthy run sends exactly one message containing the summary; the exit code always matches `check_crawlers.py`'s own regardless of notification outcome. Then a full live run: `venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts\run_scheduled_health_check.py` for real (all 32 outlets, real network, no mocking) followed by `schtasks /Create` to actually register the daily 05:30 job, then `schtasks /Query ... /V` to confirm it.
- **Result:** unit 271 passed (262 + 9 new).
