Live: real health check ran (**21 OK / 10 BROKEN / 1 ERROR of 32** today -- a materially different snapshot from the 2026-09-16 morning's 18/32, e.g. OKEZONE/EMITENNEWS/KUMPARAN/TEMPO/BERITASATU now healthy via earlier fixes, not investigated further here), `EXIT_CODE=1` propagated correctly from the wrapper, and the notification path logged `WARNING TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN/TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID not set -- skipping notification` exactly as designed instead of crashing. `schtasks /Create` succeeded; `/Query /V` confirmed `Next Run Time: 18/09/2026 05:30:00`, `Status: Ready`, `Run As User: bdrex`, `Schedule Type: Daily`, and the exact two-argument command line (python.exe + script path, both quoted for the space in "news-scraping 2").
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed working end-to-end except the final "message actually arrives on a phone" hop, which needs the user's own Telegram bot token/chat id (they're setting this up separately) -- re-run `venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts\notify_telegram.py "test"` once `.env` has real credentials to confirm delivery, and check the task's `Last Run Result` the morning after. This is the first ticket this session to make a persistent change to the user's actual OS (a real recurring Task Scheduler job, not just repo files or DB rows) -- confirmed the user's explicit choice on both the notification channel and "create it now vs. hand me the command" before doing it, matching how DB migrations were handled (ask before anything that reaches outside the repo/test DB), not just for destructive actions.
- **Links:** OPS-01 (#9) fixed · `newscrawler/core/constants.py` · `scripts/notify_telegram.py`, `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py` · `tests/unit/test_notify_telegram.py`, `tests/unit/test_run_scheduled_health_check.py` · Task Scheduler job "NewsAggregator Crawler Health Check" (not a repo artifact -- lives on the user's machine).

### 2026-09-16 — OPS-02 fix: does tracking which extractor won catch the four known-FALLBACK sources?
- **Question / hypothesis:** `_get_news_details` already picks the best of site/trafilatura/newspaper via `_best_of`, but discards which one won -- `check_crawlers.py` (and `articles`) only ever see the final text, so "OK" can't be told apart from "the site's own selector is dead and a fallback is quietly doing 100% of the work" (CNBC, CNN, KONTAN, PIKIRANRAKYAT, confirmed as fallback-only back in the 2026-09-15 health check). Recording `meta_data["extractor"]` at each fallback stage (using identity comparison against `_best_of`'s return value, since it returns one of its two inputs unchanged rather than merging) and adding a `FALLBACK` status to `check_crawlers.py` for "detail_ok > 0 but no sample used 'site'" should catch exactly these four without touching the extraction logic itself.
- **Method:** `extractor_used` starts `"site"`, gets overwritten to `"trafilatura"`/`"newspaper"` only when `_best_of` actually returns that candidate (not just "a fallback ran" -- a fallback that returns something shorter than the current text must not steal the label), and resets to `None` if the final result still isn't usable (a short-but-nonzero text surviving isn't meaningfully "site"'s success). Piggybacks on the existing `meta_data` dict, which already flows unchanged into `articles.meta_data` (JSON column) via `_to_newsdetail_data_model` -- no new column or persistence-layer change needed. `check_crawlers.py`: `classify()` gained an `extractors` param (the label for each *successful* sample only); `STATUS_FALLBACK` is deliberately kept out of `UNHEALTHY` (exit code, `write_report`'s `"unhealthy"` key) but added to a new `NEEDS_ATTENTION` set used by `summary_line`'s "needs attention" listing and a new `"needs_attention"` key in the JSON/report output -- "needs attention but non-fatal" per the ticket, not "silently ignored."
  5 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_crawler_base.py::TestExtractorMetadata`, reusing the existing `_FixedTextCrawler`/`_run` harness from the SYS-01 fallback tests) covering all four extractor labels plus the "short site text beats an even-shorter fallback, but neither is usable -> None" edge case (caught a wrong assumption in the test itself here -- see below). 7 new/updated tests in `tests/unit/test_check_crawlers.py` (`classify()`'s FALLBACK branch and its backward-compatible no-`extractors`-arg default; `check_one()` producing FALLBACK end-to-end; `summary_line`/`NEEDS_ATTENTION` reporting). Updated the pre-existing `_detail()` test helper to carry `meta_data={"extractor": "site"}` by default, since every real `NewsDetailsDTO.meta_data` will have this key going forward -- the helper predated the feature, not a case worth special-casing in production code.
  Live: `check_crawlers.py --website CNBC,CNN,KONTAN,PIKIRANRAKYAT --sample-size 3` (real network, real extraction, no mocking) and a separate `--quiet` run to confirm the exit code.
- **Result:** unit 283 passed (271 + 5 + 7). Caught one wrong assumption while writing the tests: expected "short site text beats an even-shorter trafilatura" to stay labeled `"site"`, but the code (correctly, on reflection) labels it `None` since the *final* combined result still isn't usable -- fixed the test's expectation rather than the code, since a misleadingly-confident `"site"` label on unusable text would be worse. Live: **CNBC, CNN, KONTAN, PIKIRANRAKYAT all now show `FALLBACK`** (was `OK`) with notes like `"site selector produced nothing; rescued by trafilatura"`, all with `100`/`100`/`25`/`200` sitemap links and `3/3` samples extracted -- exit code stayed `0` (confirmed FALLBACK is non-fatal, matching the ticket). `SELECT meta_data->>'extractor', count(*) FROM articles GROUP BY 1` on the real DB runs cleanly and returns `(NULL, 11939)` -- every existing row predates this fix and never had the field; it'll start populating from the next real crawl onward, not retroactively (there's no way to know which extractor originally produced already-saved text).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** confirmed fixed and live-verified against the exact four sources the ticket named. The distinction between `check_crawlers.py`'s own "any nonzero characters" success criterion and `crawler.py`'s internal `_has_usable_text` (`>= MIN_ARTICLE_CHARS`, default 200) is a real, if narrow, seam -- a sample could have nonzero-but-short text with `extractor=None` (nothing "usable" internally) while still counting as `detail_ok` in the health check; `classify()` treats that correctly as FALLBACK too (`None not in extractors` is true), which is the conservative and correct call, not a bug to special-case. Added the `OPERATIONS.md` query per the ticket.
- **Links:** OPS-02 (#10) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/crawler.py`, `scripts/check_crawlers.py` · `tests/unit/test_crawler_base.py` (added `TestExtractorMetadata`), `tests/unit/test_check_crawlers.py` (extended).

### 2026-09-16 — OPS-05 fix: does scripts/migrate.py plus a real newsletter run close out the tracker, and what's actually still broken?
- **Question / hypothesis:** step 1 (applying `migrate_phase3.sql`) landed earlier the same day; remaining: (2) a `schema_migrations` table + `scripts/migrate.py` applying whichever `migrate_*.sql` a DB doesn't have, in order; (3) extend `test_schema.py` to catch drift generically instead of one hand-listed column at a time; (4) split `get_stats()`'s seven-sub-selects-in-one-statement so one missing table doesn't blank every number. If all four land, `task=newsletter` should finally complete against real data.
- **Method:** while auditing the exact documented migration order (CLAUDE.md) against `scripts/`'s real directory contents, found two files CLAUDE.md's list had silently drifted from: `migrate_extracted_text_prose.sql` (SYS-18's fix, applied to production the same day it was written, but never added to the documented list -- my own oversight from earlier in this session) and `migrate_posted_at.sql` (dead: converts `sitemaps.posted_at` TIMESTAMP->INTEGER "after the database was created with init.sql", but `init.sql` doesn't exist in this repo -- it was the orphaned `worktree-phase1-migration` branch's own bootstrap flow, never adopted, and `posted_at` is already INTEGER on both real DBs via whatever created the base schema). Fixed CLAUDE.md's list and added an `IGNORED_MIGRATIONS` set (with the reasoning above) to `scripts/migrate.py` so it doesn't warn about the dead file forever.
  `scripts/migrate.py` uses raw psycopg2 (not SQLAlchemy sessions) to run each file's SQL, matching the pattern already proven reliable for multi-statement scripts throughout this session -- deliberately does **not** attempt to auto-detect "is this migration already applied" by re-running everything and treating "already exists" errors as success, since the migration files are explicitly not idempotent as a set (e.g. `migrate_articles_columns.sql`'s `is_embedded` type conversion has no re-run guard) and a partially-failing multi-statement file could silently skip real work. Instead: `--seed-existing` records files as applied without running their SQL (for bootstrapping onto a DB that already has them, verified first via `information_schema` fingerprint checks against both real DBs -- confirmed identical schema on both, all 10 core migrations present), and plain `apply` mode only ever runs a file the *first* time it sees no `schema_migrations` row for it.
  Verified the tool's actual apply-from-scratch logic (not just the seed path) against a **genuinely fresh** throwaway database (`CREATE DATABASE migrate_fresh_test`, minimal `sitemaps`/`articles` stub tables matching what the migrations assume already exist -- their original DDL predates every captured `migrate_*.sql` file and isn't reconstructable from anything in this repo, a real gap noted but out of scope for this ticket) -- all 11 files applied cleanly in order, second run was a no-op, dropped the database after.
  6 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_migrate.py`, mocked psycopg2 connection) covering dry-run/seed/apply/ordering/the-dead-file-is-not-flagged. Extended `tests/integration/test_schema.py` with a generic ORM-vs-real-schema reflection test (iterates `Base.metadata.tables` from both `table.py` and `knowledge_table.py`, catching drift for any ORM-declared table/column at once instead of one hand-picked assertion at a time -- exactly what DBT-11's "reflection test" would have been, had it existed), explicit checks for the three raw-SQL-only artifacts (`newsletter_digests`, `sitemaps.cluster_id`, `crawl_log`), and a `schema_migrations`-completeness test comparing the DB's recorded rows against `scripts/migrate.py`'s own `MIGRATIONS` list.
  `get_stats()`: replaced the one seven-sub-select statement with one query per metric, each in its own try/except (`_STATS_QUERIES` dict of `{key: (sql, needs_str_conversion)}`). 4 unit tests (`tests/unit/test_get_stats_isolation.py`) proving a `newsletter_digests`-missing exception blanks only `digests_generated`, not the other six -- using a plain-list `side_effect` (not a generator) since a generator that raises is exhausted afterward and would have wrongly failed every later metric too in the test itself, a subtlety worth remembering for future Mock-based "one call fails, the rest succeed" tests.
  **Live end-to-end proof, not just schema checks:** called `NewsletterGenerator.generate(date(2026, 6, 2))` directly against the real `newsaggregator` DB (580+ real articles from the last real crawl). Hit an immediate, unrelated crash.
- **Result:** `save_digest` raised `psycopg2.errors.SyntaxError: syntax error at or near ":"` on `:categories::jsonb, :source_ids::jsonb` -- SQLAlchemy's `text()` does not recognize a `:name` bind param immediately followed by `::` (the Postgres cast operator; a known, documented gotcha), so those two tokens were passed through as literal, unsubstituted SQL text. **`task=newsletter` had never completed on this machine even after the phase3 migration landed** -- a second, independent, pre-existing bug was blocking the exact thing OPS-05's own "Done when" needed to verify. Filed separately as SYS-23 (#59, P0 -- the newsletter feature has silently produced nothing since it was written) since it's not a migration/tracking problem at all, then fixed it inline (blocking my own verification, so per the "keep going unless blocked" rule this one got fixed immediately rather than just filed): `CAST(:categories AS jsonb)` / `CAST(:source_ids AS jsonb)` instead of the `::` shorthand. 3 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_save_digest_sql.py`) compiling the real statement and asserting both params are real bind params, not literal text -- verified these tests actually catch the bug by reverting the fix via `git stash` and watching the first test go red, then restoring.
  Re-ran the same live call after the fix: clustering (56 clusters), Gemini summarization (hit a real `429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` daily quota mid-run -- `newsletter_generator.py` already has a per-cluster fallback to headline-only summaries on API failure, so this degraded gracefully and did not abort the run), `Digest written to digests\2026-06-02.md`, `Newsletter generated in 57900ms: 56 clusters, 589 articles`. `get_stats()` afterward: `digests_generated: 1` (was 0), all seven numbers populated with real values. Confirmed directly: `newsletter_digests` has 1 row (`digest_id=1`, 56 clusters, 589 articles), `digests/2026-06-02.md` exists on disk (114,657 bytes).
  Applied `--seed-existing` to `newsaggregator_test` (no sign-off needed, test DB) and, with the user's sign-off, to real `newsaggregator` (11 rows, purely additive bookkeeping table, zero existing schema/data touched).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** all four of OPS-05's fix points landed, plus the ticket's exact "Done when" criteria independently confirmed against real production data, not just schema presence. The single biggest lesson: **"the table now exists" is a different, weaker claim than "the code that writes to it works"** -- OPS-05's own morning-of comment already showed this gap (`digests_generated: 0` after the migration landed) but nobody had actually tried to run `task=newsletter` end-to-end until this verification pass forced it. Always execute the real write path with real data before declaring a "missing table" ticket done, not just confirm the table's presence. Full unit suite: 296 passed. Full integration: 67 passed / 10 failed (same pre-existing SYS-10/DS01 baseline).
- **Links:** OPS-05 (#53) fixed · SYS-23 (#59) fixed · `scripts/migrate.py` (new), `scripts/migrate_extracted_text_prose.sql`/`scripts/migrate_posted_at.sql` (documentation fixes), `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_newsletter_data_source.py` · `tests/unit/test_migrate.py`, `tests/unit/test_get_stats_isolation.py`, `tests/unit/test_save_digest_sql.py`, `tests/integration/test_schema.py` (extended) · CLAUDE.md (fixed migration order), OPERATIONS.md (new queries).

### 2026-09-17 — QA sign-off pass over the 13 closed P1 tickets
- **Question / hypothesis:** the 2026-09-16/17 session closed every P1 (SYS-04/05/06/07/08/13/16/17/18/19, OPS-01/02/05) and handed the work to QA. Does each ticket's own "Done when" actually hold against the real `newsaggregator` DB and the real code paths — and does each fix have a regression guard with teeth, or only a test that happens to pass?
- **Method:** four independent techniques rather than re-reading the diffs.
  1. **Live DB verification** of every Done-when query against `newsaggregator` (not `newsaggregator_test`) and, for OPS-05, against both.
  2. **A real end-to-end crawl** — `scripts/experiments/probe_p1_end_to_end.py`, which builds the production stack (`DataFlowRepositoryImpl` → `CrawlerServiceImpl`) with a small `limit` and runs `crawl_sitemaps` + `crawl_newsdetails` for one source, then reads back `crawl_log`, `sitemaps.crawl_status`, `articles.extracted_text`, `articles.meta_data->>'extractor'` and the chunk counts. Run on IDXCHANNEL (`--limit 6`, `--limit 12 --sitemap-first`) and CNBC (`--limit 10`).
  3. **Mutation testing** (`scratchpad/mutate.py`, throwaway): re-introduce each original bug with a minimal faithful edit, run the whole unit suite, report whether anything goes red. 17 mutants.
  4. **Two accuracy probes for claims nobody had measured:** `scripts/experiments/probe_sys16_backfill_accuracy.py` (does the headline-only backfill agree with the real title+body filter decision?) and `scripts/experiments/probe_missing_crawler_overrides.py` (is TRIBUN really the only source left with SYS-08's missing-override gap?).
  Also triggered the registered Task Scheduler job by hand, and inspected both FAISS docstores for vectors the fixes might have duplicated.
- **Result:** **11 of 13 pass, 2 fall short**, and 4 new defects came out of the probes.
  - **Live end-to-end (IDXCHANNEL, 12 sitemaps):** `attempted=12 extracted=12 passed_filter=10 saved=10`; `crawl_log` got `task=sitemap article_count=855` (real new rows — confirmed `855` IDXCHANNEL sitemaps with `sitemap_id > 99000`) and `task=full_text article_count=10`; `crawl_status` moved `saved +10`, `rejected_filter +2`; all 10 new `articles` rows are prose (no `{"` prefix), carry `meta_data->>'extractor' = 'site'`, and have L1=1 plus L2≥1 chunks. That single run confirms SYS-04, SYS-07, SYS-16, SYS-17, SYS-18 and OPS-02 persistence at once.
  - **Whole-DB invariants:** `articles LIKE '{"%'` = **0/11,949**; `articles` with no `chunks` row = **0**; all **101,940** `chunks.embedding_id` values resolve to a real key in the matching FAISS docstore; `sitemaps.crawl_status` = 63,662 pending / 24,981 rejected_filter / 11,949 saved; `schema_migrations` lists all **11** files on **both** DBs; `get_stats()` returns all seven metrics populated; `newsletter_digests` has a real row and `digests/2026-06-02.md` exists.
  - **SYS-06 live:** `CRAWL_WEBSITES=NOSUCHSITE CRAWL_TASK=sitemap python __main__.py` → **exit 1** with a full traceback ending in `RuntimeError: 1 of 1 sources failed: NOSUCHSITE`.
  - **Mutation testing:** 15 of 16 real mutants **CAUGHT** by `tests/unit` alone. One survived: deleting `AND a.crawl_status != 'rejected_filter'` from `load_all_sitemaps` — SYS-16's central line — leaves all 296 unit tests green. It is caught only by `tests/integration/test_sitemap_terminal_state_live.py`, in a suite that is red by default (→ DBT-18 (#64)). (A 17th "mutant" that only renamed a local was correctly not caught; discarded as a bad mutant, not a gap.)
  - **SYS-16 backfill accuracy — the number nobody had:** 90 sampled `rejected_filter` rows across 12 sources, each re-fetched through the real crawler and scored both ways. **90/90 agreement, 0 false positives.** The headline-only heuristic that permanently excluded 24,963 rows is sound for the non-VIVA sources it was applied to.
  - **SYS-08 completeness:** swept all 32 crawlers for inherited-instead-of-overridden abstract methods. `_get_branches` and `_get_whole_text`: 32/32 overridden. `_get_reporter_from_text`: **TRIBUN only** — already SRC-09 (#57), deliberately deferred. No undiscovered source carries the bug.
  - **FAISS duplicates are not a regression:** 187 sitemap_ids have duplicate vectors in both indexes (187 L1 + 848 L2 docs), but their `chunks` rows date to **2026-06-02** (185 VIVA, 1 GRIDID, 1 PIKIRANRAKYAT) — pre-existing DBT-09 territory, untouched by SYS-17. The 723 chunks written on 2026-09-16 by the orphan repair added none.
  - **Two Done-whens not met:** SYS-04 (#5) — `crawl_log.article_count` counts rows *handed to* `save_newsdetails`, not rows persisted; with every row failing its savepoint the run still reports `article_count = 3, rows actually written = 0` and stamps all three sitemaps `crawl_status='saved'` (→ SYS-24 (#60)). OPS-01 (#9) — the job is registered and, triggered by hand, ran correctly (exit 1, 32 `crawl_log` rows, a fresh `crawler_health/2026-09-17.json`), but `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` are absent from `.env` so nothing is delivered anywhere, and `StartWhenAvailable=False` + `LogonType=Interactive` mean a 05:30 window missed while the machine is off is skipped rather than caught up (→ OPS-08 (#61)).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** the P1 batch is genuinely good work — the fixes hold against production data, not just against their own tests, and mutation testing found only one unguarded line in sixteen. Three lessons worth keeping: (1) **mutation testing is the cheapest way to tell a regression guard from a passing test** — it found in minutes what reading diffs would not have; (2) **an accuracy claim embedded in a one-way data migration needs a measured error rate before the rows are written**, not after (SYS-16 happened to be right at 90/90, but nothing in the process would have caught it if it were wrong — and `rejected_filter` is only reversible because the status is itself the record); (3) **a fix that makes a metric trustworthy has to make it trustworthy in both directions** — SYS-04 removed "always 0" and replaced it with a count that can read healthy while nothing persists, which is the same blindness inverted. Do not re-derive: the 187 duplicate FAISS vectors and the 9,447 VIVA sitemaps with chunks but no article row are June 2026 artifacts, already tracked, not caused by any P1 fix.
- **Links:** verified SYS-05 (#6), SYS-06 (#7), SYS-07 (#8), SYS-08 (#26), SYS-13 (#32), SYS-16 (#37), SYS-17 (#38), SYS-18 (#39), SYS-19 (#40), OPS-02 (#10), OPS-05 (#53) · **reopened** SYS-04 (#5) and OPS-01 (#9), whose own Done-whens were not met — the first drafts of those findings, SYS-24 (#60) and OPS-08 (#61), were folded back into the reopened parents and closed as duplicates · new tickets SYS-25 (#62), OPS-09 (#63), SRC-10 (#65) · DBT-18 (#64) was my own duplicate of DBT-06 (#29) — filed before cross-checking the open P2 list, closed, with its two genuinely-new findings moved onto #29 · `scripts/experiments/probe_p1_end_to_end.py`, `scripts/experiments/probe_sys16_backfill_accuracy.py`, `scripts/experiments/probe_missing_crawler_overrides.py`

### 2026-09-17 — DBT-06, SYS-04 (reopened), OPS-01 (reopened): making the integration gate trustworthy again and closing the two Done-when gaps from the QA pass
- **Question / hypothesis:** three tickets to fix before anything else, in this order: DBT-06 (#29, P2, cheapest-highest-value — the integration suite is red by default so SYS-16's and OPS-05's only regression guards go unread), then SYS-04 (#5, reopened) and OPS-01 (#9, reopened), whose own Done-whens the 2026-09-17 QA pass found unmet.
- **Method / fix:**
  1. **DBT-06 (#29).** Root-caused two independent leak sources instead of just patching symptoms: (a) `tests/integration/test_backfill_pipeline.py`'s cleanup fixture matched rows by content (`extracted_text LIKE '%BACKFILL_TEST%'`), which misses `TestEmptyTextHandling`'s article (`extracted_text='[]'`) — that leftover row then blows up the *next* `DELETE FROM sitemaps` with `ForeignKeyViolation: articles_sitemap_fk`. Rewrote it to resolve `sitemap_id`s first (by headline) and delete children by id, not content. (b) `tests/integration/test_data_sources.py`'s `TestCrawlAttemptCooldown` tests commit fixed-link rows (`http://recent.com` etc.) every run with no cleanup at all — found **29 duplicates of each of 4 links** in `newsaggregator_test` before the fix, which breaks DS11 (an old, now-past-cooldown duplicate becomes eligible again). Added a **session-scoped autouse `TRUNCATE ... RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE`** fixture in `conftest.py` (guarded by `current_database() = 'newsaggregator_test'`) so every `pytest tests/integration` run starts from empty tables regardless of what a prior run left behind — this is what makes "green twice in a row from any starting state" achievable rather than order-dependent. Also rewrote DS01 to insert unique (uuid-suffixed) links and assert by link instead of "last 3 rows by `sitemap_id DESC`", since a leftover row sharing a fixed link would make `save_sitemaps`' `(link, posted_at)` dedup skip the insert and silently pick up unrelated rows. Marked `test_backfill_pipeline.py` `xfail(strict=False)` module-wide for the still-open SYS-10 (`nomic-embed-text` 401).
  2. **SYS-04 reopened (#5).** `save_newsdetails` now adds `.returning(NewsArticlesTable.sitemap_id)` to the upsert and returns the list of sitemap_ids `RETURNING` actually gave back — `on_conflict_do_nothing` silently returns no row for a skip, telling an insert apart from a no-op in the same statement. Threaded that return value through `save_newsdetails_data` → `save_scraped_data` → `_crawl_newsdetails_inner`, so both `saved_total` and the `mark_sitemaps_status(..., "saved")` call are now driven by what was actually committed, not `len(to_save)`.
  3. **OPS-01 reopened (#9).** Ran the two PowerShell lines against the live registered task: `New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet -StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun -ExecutionTimeLimit (New-TimeSpan -Hours 2)` + `Set-ScheduledTask`. Verified before/after: `StartWhenAvailable`/`WakeToRun` were `False`/`False`, now `True`/`True`. Left `LogonType=Interactive` alone (ticket said "consider", re-registering with stored credentials is a bigger, separate change) — documented the tradeoff in OPERATIONS.md instead. Added the absence-detection check: `run_scheduled_health_check.py` now calls `_hours_since_last_health_check()` (`SELECT EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (NOW() - MAX(finished_at)))/3600 FROM crawl_log WHERE task='health_check'`) *before* invoking `check_crawlers.py` (so the gap reflects the *previous* run, not this one), and sends a distinct Telegram alert if it's over 36h. Documented the same query in OPERATIONS.md under "Detect a missed/broken schedule" so the gap is checkable by a human without opening Task Scheduler, independent of whether the notifier itself is working.
- **Result:**
  - `pytest tests/integration -q`: **69 → 71 passed, 9 xfailed**, run twice in a row from a DB that started with 149 KOMPAS + 116 COOLDOWNTEST (with 29 duplicates of 4 fixed links) + 107 NULL + 29 INTTEST leftover sitemap rows — both runs identical. `tests/unit -q`: 298 → 302 passed (298 after DBT-06 alone, +4 new SYS-04 unit tests). `tests/test_crawler_service.py`: still 4 passed, unaffected (asserts on call *args*, not the changed return value).
  - **SYS-04 live-verified at the SQL layer**, not just mocked: against `newsaggregator_test`, `save_newsdetails([bad_fk_row, bad_fk_row])` → `[]` (both raise `ForeignKeyViolation`, caught per-savepoint, nothing committed); `save_newsdetails([bad_fk_row, good_row])` → `[good_row.sitemap_id]`, and `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM articles WHERE sitemap_id = good_id` confirmed exactly 1 row. Matches the QA reproduction exactly (`attempted=3 extracted=3 saved=3, rows actually persisted=0` was the bug; now `saved` only counts what `articles` actually holds).
  - **OPS-01 live-verified**: `(Get-ScheduledTask ...).Settings` shows `StartWhenAvailable=True WakeToRun=True ExecutionTimeLimit=PT2H` post-fix (was `False/False/PT72H`). New live integration test (`test_run_scheduled_health_check_live.py`) inserts a `crawl_log` row `finished_at = NOW() - INTERVAL '50 hours'` and confirms `_hours_since_last_health_check()` returns `49.9–50.1` against the real `TIMESTAMP` column (no tz surprises from `EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM ...)`), and `None` when no `health_check` row exists.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** don't fix a `LIKE '%marker%'` cleanup pattern in place — check whether any test in the same file can produce a row that pattern won't match (an empty/degenerate case is the classic miss). For a suite where several fixtures commit directly (bypass rollback), a single session-scoped `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` guarded on `current_database()` is cheaper and more robust than chasing every individual leak. For a "count reflects reality" metric, don't stop at "counts something non-zero" — trace it to what `RETURNING`/an actual row check confirms was persisted, since `len(list_handed_to_a_function)` is never proof of a write. `StartWhenAvailable`/`WakeToRun` are off by default on `schtasks /Create`; a scheduled job you actually need to fire needs both, plus an independent way to notice if it stops firing at all (the absence check), since a completely silent scheduler failure looks identical to a healthy day.
- **Links:** DBT-06 (#29) fixed · SYS-04 (#5) fixed · OPS-01 (#9) fixed · `tests/integration/conftest.py`, `tests/integration/test_backfill_pipeline.py`, `tests/integration/test_data_sources.py` · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_articles_data_source.py`, `news_data_source.py`, `data_flow_repository_impl.py`, `data_flow_repository.py`, `crawler_service_impl.py` · `tests/unit/test_crawl_log_article_count.py`, `tests/unit/test_sitemap_terminal_state.py`, `tests/integration/test_crawl_log_article_count_live.py` · `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py`, `tests/unit/test_run_scheduled_health_check.py`, `tests/integration/test_run_scheduled_health_check_live.py` (new) · OPERATIONS.md

### 2026-09-17 — SYS-25, OPS-09, SRC-10: the three new tickets from the QA pass
- **Question / hypothesis:** three new (not reopened) tickets came out of the 2026-09-17 QA sign-off: SYS-25 (#62, a sitemap missing chunks at only one level is unrepairable), OPS-09 (#63, SYS-19's attempt-cap can be tripped by a Gemini outage and has no visibility/reset), SRC-10 (#65, IDXCHANNEL's byline selector matches the publication date). User asked to fix all three before going back to QA.
- **Method / fix:**
  1. **SYS-25 (#62).** `save_chunk_data`'s dedup (`get_existing_sitemap_ids`) and `backfill_orphan_chunks.py`'s selection (`NOT EXISTS chunks WHERE sitemap_id=...`) both operated at sitemap granularity -- a sitemap holding chunks at one level and missing the other looked "fully saved" to both, permanently. Renamed the dedup query to `get_existing_sitemap_level_pairs` (keyed on `(sitemap_id, chunk_level)`), updated `save_chunk_data`'s filter to match, and widened the backfill script's WHERE to "missing L1 OR missing L2" -- `save_chunk_data`'s new per-level dedup then means re-chunking a partially-saved article only inserts what's actually missing.
  2. **OPS-09 (#63).** `_call_gemini` conflated "the API call itself failed" (network/timeout/5xx/429/auth -- nothing to do with this article) with "we got a response back but couldn't use it" (safety block, unparseable JSON -- IS about this article). Introduced `GeminiTransientError`, raised only for the former; `extract_all` catches it separately, skips that article (not the whole batch), and never calls `record_extraction_failure` or `increment_api_call_count` for it -- only a response actually received still counts against the cap or the daily quota. Added `scripts/reset_extraction_attempts.py` (`--all-retired` / `--like PATTERN` / `--sitemap-ids`, always supports `--dry-run`, refuses to run with no filter) and an OPERATIONS.md query splitting `pending` into `pending_retryable` vs `retired`.
  3. **SRC-10 (#65).** Fetched a live IDXCHANNEL page: the real byline is `<a class="text-body--2 creator">`, not the `<div class="text-body--2">` the old selector's `find("div", ...)` landed on (that div is the timestamp -- the two elements just happen to share one class). Verified `class_="creator"` picks the right element across 5 live categories (banking/economics/news/syariah/milenomic) before touching code. Also added a shared guard, `Crawler._looks_like_a_name`, applied to every crawler's `_get_reporter_from_text` output in the base class's `_get_news_details` (not just IDXCHANNEL's) -- rejects a candidate that's purely digits or date-shaped, so the same "selector drifted onto a timestamp" class of bug can't silently produce a plausible-looking wrong value anywhere else either.
- **Result:**
  - `pytest tests/unit -q`: 307 → 335 passed (28 new: 5 SYS-25, 11 OPS-09 incl. `test_reset_extraction_attempts.py`, 12 SRC-10 incl. the shared-guard tests). `pytest tests/integration -q`: 71 → 72 passed / 9 xfailed (1 new: the 3-run-outage live test), twice in a row.
  - **SYS-25 live-verified and repaired on production**, with the user's confirmation first (dry-run shown, then executed): `scripts/backfill_orphan_chunks.py` found and repaired exactly the 29 known MEDIAINDONESIA sitemaps; the ticket's own Done-when query now returns `no_l1=0 no_l2=0` on `newsaggregator` (was `no_l1=29`).
  - **OPS-09 live-verified**: `tests/integration/test_knowledge_extraction_poison_pill_live.py`'s new test runs 3 consecutive `extract_all()` calls against a real inserted article with `_call_gemini` raising `GeminiTransientError` every time -- `knowledge_extract_attempts` stays exactly 0 and the article is still returned by `get_articles_pending_extraction` afterward, matching the ticket's Done-when verbatim.
  - **SRC-10 verified live** via the real `_get_news_details` path (not just the unit-level selector) against 2 fresh URLs: `reporter=['Desi Angriani']` and `reporter=['Rohman Wibowo']`, both real names. `check_crawlers.py --website IDXCHANNEL` still reports `OK 3/3`. **Historical data left alone, by explicit user choice**: the 615 existing IDXCHANNEL rows still carry the bad numeric writer (confirmed via the ticket's own query, unchanged) -- fixing them needs 615 live re-fetches (no cached HTML to re-parse, unlike SYS-25), which the user chose to defer as P3/low-value-for-the-cost; documented as a known, deliberate exception in OPERATIONS.md's new query rather than left as an unexplained gap.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) when a "no chunks"/"no articles" style orphan check exists, ask whether it should really be "no chunks **at any expected granularity**" -- SYS-17 and SYS-25 are the same bug shape one level apart, and the fix pattern (SQL dedup key, widened backfill WHERE) transfers directly; (2) a per-item retry cap needs to distinguish "this item is bad" from "the service is down" at the point where the exception is caught, not after -- catching everything with one bare `except Exception` is what let a transient error retire a queue entry permanently; (3) verify a scraper selector fix against 3+ live samples *before* writing the code, the way SYS-08 did for VIVA -- would have caught that "text-body--2" alone is ambiguous between two different elements before ever writing the fix; (4) a shared cross-crawler guard (`_looks_like_a_name`) is cheap insurance once you've found one instance of a bug class, and costs nothing when nothing on the input is actually numeric/date-shaped.
- **Links:** SYS-25 (#62) fixed · OPS-09 (#63) fixed · SRC-10 (#65) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/sql_alchemy_chunk_data_source.py`, `data_flow_repository_impl.py`, `scripts/backfill_orphan_chunks.py` · `newscrawler/core/knowledge_extractor.py`, `sql_alchemy_knowledge_data_source.py` (query only), `scripts/reset_extraction_attempts.py` (new) · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/crawler.py` (`_looks_like_a_name`), `idx_channel/idxchannel_crawler.py` · `tests/unit/test_save_chunk_data.py`, `test_sqlalchemy_chunk_data_source.py`, `test_knowledge_extractor.py`, `test_reset_extraction_attempts.py` (new), `test_idxchannel_crawler.py` (new), `test_crawler_base.py` · `tests/integration/test_knowledge_extraction_poison_pill_live.py` · OPERATIONS.md

### 2026-09-17 — SRC-09: TRIBUN's missing byline extractor, the last P1 fix
- **Question / hypothesis:** SRC-09 (#57, P1) was deliberately left open during the 2026-09-16/17 remediation (TRIBUN had 0 sitemap rows, no production symptom yet). User asked to list open P1s, then fix the one that came back: TRIBUN never implements `_get_reporter_from_text`, the identical gap SYS-08 found and fixed for VIVA. Does it still reproduce, and does a real byline selector exist to replace the no-op?
- **Method / fix:** confirmed `NewsDetailsModel.reporter` is already `Optional[List[str]] = None` (SYS-08's shared model fix already landed), so the *crash* half of this bug is gone -- what's left is TRIBUN silently writing `writer=NULL` on every article forever, per the ticket's own fix item 2. Fetched a live TRIBUN article (`tribunnews.com/bisnis/...`): no visible-DOM element with a recognizable `author`/`byline`/`penulis` *class*, but `<div id="penulis">Penulis: <a>Name</a></div>` exists by **id**, not class -- my first regex-on-class search missed it entirely, which is itself the lesson (see below). Verified across 5 categories (bisnis, nasional, regional, seleb, techno) before writing code, matching the SYS-08/SRC-10 bar; `new-economy`'s one sample had no `id="penulis"` at all -- its JSON-LD `author` is the literal "Tribunnews" org account, i.e. genuinely no individual byline, so returning `[]` there is correct, not a miss. Implemented `TribunCrawler._get_reporter_from_text` to read `div#penulis`'s `<a>` text, falling back to stripping the "Penulis:" label if there's no link.
- **Result:**
  - `pytest tests/unit -q`: 335 passed (+4, `tests/unit/test_tribun_crawler.py`, mirroring the VIVA/IDXCHANNEL inline-HTML test pattern).
  - **Live end-to-end, not just the unit-level selector**: TRIBUN had 0 sitemap rows in `newsaggregator` (never actually crawled despite being on `HEALTHY`), so ran a real `crawl_sitemaps` first (900 new TRIBUN sitemap rows landed) then a targeted `crawl_newsdetails(specific_sitemaps=...)` against 10 `category='bisnis'` sitemaps (a random top-N sample hit 0/6 articles passing the semantic filter first, unrelated to this bug -- re-targeted at a finance category to actually exercise the save path). **4 articles saved**, real names captured: `writer=["Sanusi"]`, `["Nitis Hawaroh"]`, `["Rizki Sandi Saputra"]`, and one legitimate `[]` (no byline on that particular article) -- none crashed, matching the ticket's Done-when exactly ("returns a list, possibly empty, never `None`; a live full_text crawl saves at least one article").
  - `check_crawlers.py --website TRIBUN --sample-size 3` still `OK 3/3` (this fix only touches the save path, not extraction health, so no regression expected or found).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** a byline lookup can live on an **id**, not a class -- `find_all(class_=re.compile(...))` searches used for VIVA/IDXCHANNEL's investigation would have silently found nothing here and reported "no byline element" as a false negative; always also grep the raw HTML for the actual label text (`"Penulis"`) when a class-based search comes up empty, don't conclude "no byline exists" from a class search alone. Also: `HEALTHY` in `__main__.py`/`check_crawlers.py` only proves extraction works, not that anything ever actually got crawled -- TRIBUN sat at 0 sitemap rows for the entire 2026-09-16/17 remediation window despite being "healthy" the whole time; check row counts, not just status, before assuming a source is actually flowing data.
- **Links:** SRC-09 (#57) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/tribun/tribun_crawler.py` · `tests/unit/test_tribun_crawler.py` (new) · verified with `scripts/experiments/probe_p1_end_to_end.py --website TRIBUN --limit 6 --sitemap-first` plus an inline targeted-category follow-up (not saved as a script, category-targeting was a one-off)
### 2026-09-17 — QA re-audit (second pass) of the 7-ticket fix batch: DBT-06, SYS-04r, OPS-01r, SYS-25, OPS-09, SRC-10, SRC-09
- **Question / hypothesis:** the dev team fixed the two tickets the first QA pass reopened (SYS-04 (#5), OPS-01 (#9)), the three new ones it filed (SYS-25 (#62), OPS-09 (#63), SRC-10 (#65)), DBT-06 (#29) and SRC-09 (#57), in commits 8d86b9e / 7cba3bf / 8ac9a60. Does each ticket's own Done-when hold against production and the real code paths this time, and does each fix have a regression guard with teeth?
- **Method:** same four techniques as the first pass, on the new batch. (1) Live DB: every Done-when query on `newsaggregator`. (2) Real crawl: `scripts/experiments/probe_p1_end_to_end.py --website IDXCHANNEL --limit 10` (exercises SYS-04's count, SYS-25's dedup, SRC-10's byline on the *write* path, which the fix commit had only checked at the DTO level). (3) Mutation: 20 mutants across the 7 fixes (`scratchpad/mutate.py`, throwaway; byte-level edits so CRLF survives), run against `tests/unit` and, for survivors, `tests/integration`. (4) Direct probes for the claims that weren't tests: the real `save_newsdetails` against `newsaggregator_test` (all-FK-violation / mixed / duplicate batches); the integration suite from a *seeded-dirty* `newsaggregator_test` (stale `recent.com` rows, an FK chain, a DS01 collision row); `--runxfail` on `test_backfill_pipeline.py` to confirm the xfail reason is really SYS-10; the live Task Scheduler XML (`Export-ScheduledTask`) plus `Win32_Battery`/`PCSystemType`; `google-genai 1.47.0`'s `_get_text` source for raise paths; a sweep of all 32 `_get_reporter_from_text` return types for the new shared guard.
- **Result:** **6 of 7 hold; OPS-01 does not.** 4 new tickets.
  - **SYS-04r — holds.** SQL layer: `[bad,bad] → []`, `[bad,good] → [good]`, `[good]` again → `[]` (conflict skip), exactly 1 row. End to end: IDXCHANNEL `attempted=10 extracted=10 passed_filter=8 saved=8`, `crawl_log article_count=8`, `articles` 615 → 623, `crawl_status saved +8 / rejected_filter +2`; TRIBUN's `article_count=4` matches 4 real rows. The `"N/M rows actually committed"` log line works.
  - **DBT-06 — holds.** `72 passed, 9 xfailed` twice from the current state and once from the seeded-dirty state. `--runxfail` shows the real error is `OSError: sentence-transformers/nomic-embed-text is not a valid model identifier` (SYS-10), and teardown is clean, so the rewritten cleanup fixture *is* exercised. `newsletter_digests` is not in the TRUNCATE list; nothing writes to it under test today.
  - **SYS-25 — holds.** `no_l1=0 / no_l2=0 / with_any_chunk=21,400`; articles missing either level = 0. `save_chunks` is one `add_all` + one `commit`, so level granularity is sufficient for the current write path.
  - **OPS-09 — holds.** Runbook query: `extracted 0 / pending_retryable 11,961 / retired 0`; `reset_extraction_attempts.py --dry-run --all-retired` → 0; no-filter → argparse refusal. `GenerateContentResponse.text` never raises in the installed SDK, so its position outside the `try` is safe.
  - **SRC-09 — holds.** 4 TRIBUN articles, writers `["Rizki Sandi Saputra"]`, `["Sanusi"]`, `["Nitis Hawaroh"]`, `[]`; 900 sitemap rows (884 pending / 12 rejected / 4 saved).
  - **SRC-10 — code fix holds on the write path** (the commit had only checked `_get_news_details`): 8/8 fresh IDXCHANNEL rows carry real names; the 615 historical rows are untouched by choice. All 32 crawlers append `str`, so `_looks_like_a_name`'s `.strip()` can't blank a good byline; the regex misses the Indonesian long date form (`Rabu, 16 September 2026`) but no production `writer` has that shape.
  - **OPS-01r — Done-when not met, reopened.** Configuration verified (`StartWhenAvailable/WakeToRun=True`, `PT2H`, Telegram vars set, gap helper returns 12.28h) and both gap-check mutants caught. But: (a) **the machine is a laptop** (`Win32_Battery` present, `PCSystemType=2`) and the task XML still carries the defaults `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=true` / `StopIfGoingOnBatteries=true` — a 05:30 on battery is a *condition not met*, not a *missed start*, so `StartWhenAvailable` never queues it, `WakeToRun` wakes the machine only to skip, and the in-job gap check can't fire because the job didn't run; (b) `RegistrationInfo.Date = 2026-09-17T05:47:53` — after the first 05:30 window — and all three `health_check` batches (05:47, 06:42, 07:21 WIB) are manual triggers, so **no unattended run has ever happened**; (c) Microsoft's `StartWhenAvailable` docs say it applies only to triggers with an end boundary or infinite repetition, and this `CalendarTrigger` has neither — unverified risk, not a confirmed defect. Fix is one flag pair: `-AllowStartIfOnBatteries -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries`.
  - **Mutation: 17/20 caught by `tests/unit`; the 3 survivors also survive `tests/integration`.** M01/M02 (`save_newsdetails` returns handed-in ids / appends regardless of `RETURNING`) — `test_crawl_log_article_count_live.py` mocks `save_newsdetails_data`, so the `RETURNING` line never runs under test; M09 (`backfill_orphan_chunks.py` WHERE back to "no chunks at all") — nothing exercises the script's selection. → DBT-19 (#69).
  - **Side findings:** `crawler_health/2026-09-17.json` holds only TRIBUN — the 13:23 `--website TRIBUN` check overwrote the 07:21 32-outlet report (`open(path, "w")` on a date-keyed name; same on 2026-09-16) → OPS-10 (#66). `extract_knowledge`/`newsletter` write no `crawl_log` row (`SELECT task, COUNT(*) FROM crawl_log` → only `full_text`/`health_check`/`sitemap`), so post-OPS-09 a run where every call fails (dead key) leaves no signal anywhere → OPS-11 (#67). KUMPARAN parses page JSON-LD with `eval()` (works on 3/3 live samples today; code execution from third-party content and breaks on any `true/false/null`) → SRC-11 (#68).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** the batch is solid — six of seven hold against production, not just their own tests, and 17 of 20 mutants die. Three lessons: (1) **a scheduler fix on a laptop has three silent-skip modes, not two** — off, asleep, *and on battery*; `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries` defaults to `true` and no `-StartWhenAvailable`/`-WakeToRun` line touches it; check `Win32_Battery` before trusting any Task Scheduler configuration. (2) **"Live-verified at the SQL layer" in a REPL is not a regression guard** — the two survivors from SYS-04 are exactly the SYS-24 regression, one mocked call away; turn the REPL session into the test while it's still on screen. (3) **A Done-when phrased as an observation ("reported within 24h without anyone running a command") needs the observation, not the configuration** — the task had never fired unattended when it was closed; check `RegistrationInfo.Date` against the trigger's first boundary. Do not re-derive: the `newsletter_digests` TRUNCATE omission and the long-date regex gap are notes, not tickets; the 615 IDXCHANNEL rows stay as they are.
- **Links:** verified SYS-04 (#5), DBT-06 (#29), SYS-25 (#62), OPS-09 (#63), SRC-09 (#57), SRC-10 (#65, still open by design) · **reopened OPS-01 (#9)** · new OPS-10 (#66), OPS-11 (#67), SRC-11 (#68), DBT-19 (#69) · `scripts/experiments/probe_p1_end_to_end.py` (reused, unchanged)

### 2026-09-17 — OPS-01, third fix pass: the battery-skip mode
- **Question / hypothesis:** the second QA re-audit found the registered task still had Task Scheduler's own `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=True`/`StopIfGoingOnBatteries=True` defaults, untouched by the earlier `-StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun` fix, on a machine that has a battery. Does the live task actually carry those defaults right now, and does flipping them stick?
- **Method:** `(Get-ScheduledTask -TaskName "NewsAggregator Crawler Health Check").Settings | Select-Object ... DisallowStartIfOnBatteries, StopIfGoingOnBatteries` before touching anything, then `New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet -AllowStartIfOnBatteries -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries -StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun -ExecutionTimeLimit (New-TimeSpan -Hours 2)` + `Set-ScheduledTask`, then re-read the same properties.
- **Result:** confirmed `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=True`/`StopIfGoingOnBatteries=True` live before the change (matching the QA finding exactly). After `Set-ScheduledTask`: `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=False`, `StopIfGoingOnBatteries=False`, `StartWhenAvailable`/`WakeToRun`/`ExecutionTimeLimit` unchanged at `True`/`True`/`PT2H`.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** this closes the *configuration* half of the ticket only. Per the ticket's own revised Done-when, closing requires an *observed* unattended `health_check` batch (~22:30 UTC / 05:30 WIB, no manual trigger that morning) — first eligible window is 2026-09-18 05:30 WIB. Don't close OPS-01 on settings alone again; check `crawl_log` for that window first. Documented the battery flags in `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py`'s docstring and OPERATIONS.md's "Task settings" section (including the verification `Select-Object` line), plus a note in OPERATIONS.md flagging that no unattended run has happened yet.
- **Links:** OPS-01 (#9), left open pending the 09-18 observation · `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py` (docstring only) · `OPERATIONS.md`

### 2026-09-17 — DBT-17: repairing the 142 pending-embedding chunk rows
- **Question / hypothesis:** DBT-17 (#58, P2) left root cause unidentified: 142 `chunks` rows (26 WARTAEKONOMI sitemap_ids, dated 2026-06-01) exist with `embedding_id IS NULL`/`chunk_status='pending_embedding'`. Can the write path that created them be identified, and can they be repaired without a re-crawl?
- **Method:** walked every commit that has ever touched the chunk-write path -- the original `9b08107`, the SYS-17 fix `103980b`, the SYS-25 fix `7cba3bf` -- plus `scripts/backfill_chunks.py` (already ruled out in the ticket). Checked whether any of them could structurally produce an `embedding_id=NULL` row in `chunks`. Then wrote `scripts/backfill_pending_embeddings.py` to repair in place: fetch the 142 rows (their `text_content` is already intact, no re-chunk/re-crawl needed), back them up to JSON, embed via `ChunkFAISSDatasource.save()`, and `UPDATE ... WHERE chunk_id=:id` (not INSERT, to avoid duplicating the row). `--dry-run` first, then for real.
- **Result:**
  - **Root cause identified, code-side: none of the three historical versions of the write path can produce this shape.** The *original* `save_chunk_data` only converted a chunk to a SQL row `if c.embedding_id` was truthy -- an unembedded chunk was silently *dropped*, never inserted with NULL. The *current* `save_chunk_data`/`ChunkFAISSDatasource._embed_batch` always populates `embedding_id` (recovered or freshly assigned) before any SQL insert -- also structurally impossible. Conclusion: the 142 rows were almost certainly written by a one-off manual/REPL action during the 2026-06-01 WARTAEKONOMI backfill session (one day before the separate SYS-17 incident), not a reproducible pipeline bug. No code fix was needed to prevent recurrence.
  - **Residual design note (not fixed, not urgent):** SYS-17's SQL dedup (`get_existing_sitemap_level_pairs`) treats "a chunks row exists" as "fully saved," not checking `embedding_id IS NOT NULL` -- which is *why* two rounds of `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` (looks for *missing* rows only) never touched these 142. If this shape ever recurred, the pipeline still wouldn't self-heal it today. Left as a documented gap, not bundled into this fix (would require turning `save_chunks` into an upsert, a bigger change for a bug shape current code can't produce).
  - **Repair executed on `newsaggregator`:** dry-run confirmed exactly 142/26 WARTAEKONOMI before touching anything. Real run: backed up all 142 rows to `backups/dbt17_pending_embeddings_20260917T145239Z.json`, embedded all 142 (`updated=142 skipped=0`), and updated in place. Verified: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks WHERE embedding_id IS NULL` -> `0`. No duplicates introduced (`chunks` row count for the 26 sitemaps stays exactly 142, before and after; a separate, pre-existing duplicate check against those 26 sitemap_ids returns empty -- the duplicates that do exist elsewhere are DBT-09 (#44), unrelated). **Live-verified past just the DB flag**: queried FAISS with one repaired chunk's own full text and got `sitemap_id=7008, semantic_score=1.0` as the #1 hit -- the vector is real and retrievable, not just a status update.
  - 4 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_backfill_pending_embeddings.py`): dry-run never constructs `ChunkFAISSDatasource` or writes a backup; a real run backs up the pre-repair rows and issues an `UPDATE` keyed by `chunk_id` (never an insert); a chunk that somehow comes back from `.save()` without an `embedding_id` is skipped, not written as NULL. Full suite: 339 passed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** when a "why does this bad row exist" ticket's own code-history check comes back clean (every version of the write path structurally can't produce the shape), stop looking for a code bug and treat it as a one-off data event -- the fix is a data repair, not a guard. Also: `backfill_orphan_chunks.py`'s "missing row" query and a "row exists but embedding_id is NULL" query are two different repair shapes; don't assume one script's `NOT EXISTS` check covers both.
- **Links:** DBT-17 (#58) fixed · `scripts/backfill_pending_embeddings.py` (new) · `tests/unit/test_backfill_pending_embeddings.py` (new) · `backups/dbt17_pending_embeddings_20260917T145239Z.json` (gitignored) · DBT-09 (#44), still open, unrelated duplicates noticed in passing

### 2026-09-17 — refreshing OPS-04's store-consistency snapshot after DBT-17
- **Question / hypothesis:** OPS-04 (#52)'s divergence table is from 2026-09-16, before SYS-25's MEDIAINDONESIA repair, the 50-sitemap VIVA backfill test, and today's DBT-17 fix. What does it look like now, and is the "1,183 sitemap_ids in L1 but not L2" row (never drilled into) actually a new bug or a restated view of something already known?
- **Method:** wrote `scripts/experiments/probe_store_consistency.py` -- reads both FAISS docstores directly via `pickle.load` (per OPS-04's own repro note, no embedding model needed) and set-diffs against Postgres for all 9 rows of OPS-04's table. Then, for the unexplained "1,183" row specifically, checked whether those sitemap_ids also lack an `articles` row, a `chunks` row of any kind, and which `sources` they belong to.
- **Result:**
  | check | 2026-09-16 | now |
  |---|---|---|
  | L1 FAISS, no `articles` row | 10,680 | 10,630 (-50, the VIVA backfill test) |
  | L2 FAISS, no `articles` row | 9,497 | 9,447 (-50, same) |
  | L1 FAISS, no `chunks` row | 1,335 | 1,183 |
  | L2 FAISS, no `chunks` row | 152 | **0** |
  | `articles` rows, no `chunks` row | 152 | **0** |
  | L1 but not L2 | 1,183 | 1,183 (unchanged) |
  | sitemap_ids with 2+ L1 vectors | 187 | 187 (unchanged -- DBT-09, untouched) |
  | `chunks` status `pending_embedding` | 142 | **0** (DBT-17) |
  | `chunks` sitemap_ids not in FAISS | 0 | 0 (SYS-17 holding) |

  **The "1,183 L1 but not L2" row is not three separate findings -- it's one population, seen from three angles.** `only_l1 - chunks_any` = 1,183 (exact match), `only_l1 - articles` = 1,183 (exact match), and `SELECT DISTINCT sources FROM sitemaps WHERE sitemap_id = ANY(only_l1)` = **only `MEDIAINDONESIA`**. So all three "L1 FAISS no chunks / L1 not L2 / (implicitly) no articles" rows in OPS-04's table describe the same 1,183 MEDIAINDONESIA sitemap_ids: an L1 vector exists and *nothing else does* -- no L2 vector, no `chunks` row, no `articles` row.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** DBT-17 + SYS-25 + the 50-sitemap VIVA test fully explain every row that moved; nothing moved by accident. The 1,183-MEDIAINDONESIA population is materially different from both DBT-17 (which had a full `chunks` row with real text_content to reuse) and the VIVA backlog (which has full `chunks` rows, just no `articles` row) -- these have *only* an L1 vector, so a DBT-17-style in-place repair doesn't apply; the L1 docstore's own `page_content` (the two-paragraph summary) is recoverable, but the full article text needed for L2/`articles` is not, without a re-crawl. Posted as a refresh to OPS-04 (#52) rather than filing a new ticket for the drill-down itself. DBT-09's 187 duplicate-L1-vector sitemaps are completely unaffected by anything fixed today -- still needs its own migration.
- **Follow-up the same session:** the MEDIAINDONESIA population turned out to need its own ticket after all, once its root cause was traced -- filed as **DBT-20 (#70)**. It's the pre-SYS-17 FAISS-before-SQL crash shape at ~8x WARTAEKONOMI's scale, compounded by two gaps: `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` starts its query `FROM articles`, so a sitemap with no `articles` row at all (not just missing chunks) is invisible to it; and `backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`'s classification ignores `chunks`/FAISS state entirely, so 582 of the 1,183 got marked `crawl_status='rejected_filter'` (terminal, never retried) purely from a headline-only re-check that disagrees with the direct proof (a real FAISS vector) that the original title+body filter passed. MEDIAINDONESIA has saved 0 articles since 2026-06-02 (96 total against 12,482 sitemaps) -- consistent with the source having gone largely dormant around the same incident.
- **Links:** OPS-04 (#52), refreshed, not fixed · `scripts/experiments/probe_store_consistency.py` (new, reusable) · DBT-09 (#44), confirmed unchanged · **DBT-20 (#70), filed, not fixed**

### 2026-09-17 — DBT-09: deduping 1,035 duplicate chunks rows and their FAISS vectors
- **Question / hypothesis:** DBT-09 (#44, P2) found 1,035 duplicate `chunks` rows (same `sitemap_id`/`chunk_level`/`chunk_index`, different `chunk_id`/`embedding_id`) and no unique index. Its own fix proposal predates SYS-25's finer dedup key -- does the underlying `_embed_batch` bug still reproduce today, is a hard FK from `entity_mentions.chunk_id` a real deletion risk, and can the orphaned FAISS vectors actually be cleaned up (the ticket offered "accept the extra vectors until next rebuild" as a fallback)?
- **Method:** confirmed current counts (1,035 groups: 187 at chunk_level=1, 848 at chunk_level=2; VIVA/GRIDID/PIKIRANRAKYAT) and that no unique index exists yet. Checked `entity_mentions` for FK conflicts against any duplicate-group row. Checked `FAISS.delete()`'s signature in the installed `langchain_community` 0.3.24. Fixed `_embed_batch` (added a `seen_this_batch` set, keyed the same as the existing-in-FAISS check) so two chunks sharing a key *within one call* can't both be embedded. Added a `UniqueConstraint` to `ChunksTable` and `scripts/migrate_chunks_unique.sql`. Wrote `scripts/dedupe_chunks.py`: per duplicate group, keep the `entity_mentions`-referenced row if one exists (abort on 2+ referenced, unresolvable), else keep the lowest `chunk_id`; back up losers to JSON; delete each loser's FAISS vector via `FAISS.delete()` (L1 or L2 by level) before deleting the SQL row. `--dry-run` first, then for real, then the migration.
- **Result:**
  - **`entity_mentions.chunk_id` is a hard FK into `chunks`** (not mentioned in the original ticket) -- checked and confirmed **zero** conflicts today, but `dedupe_chunks.py` guards against it anyway (same posture as `migrate_articles_unique_sitemap.sql`/DBT-07 took for `articles`).
  - `FAISS.delete(ids)` is real and supported in the installed version -- didn't need the "accept extra vectors" fallback.
  - Dry-run matched the ticket exactly: 1,035/187/848. Real run: backed up all 1,035 losers to `backups/dbt09_dedupe_chunks_20260917T155714Z.json`, deleted 187 L1 + 848 L2 FAISS vectors, deleted 1,035 `chunks` rows (`102192 -> 101157`, exactly `-1035`). Migration applied clean (`chunks_sitemap_level_index_uq` now exists).
  - Verified: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (... GROUP BY sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index HAVING COUNT(*)>1) t` -> `0`. Re-ran `probe_store_consistency.py` (from the OPS-04 refresh above): "sitemap_ids with 2+ L1 vectors" `187 -> 0`, every other row unchanged as expected.
  - **Verified past the DB flag, the way DBT-17 was**: for a deduped sitemap (36616), directly confirmed the *surviving* `chunk_id`'s `embedding_id` is present in the `NEWS_L2` docstore with matching metadata and byte-identical `page_content` -- the kept row's vector wasn't accidentally the one removed. (A `search_l2` self-query for this same chunk did NOT rank itself #1 -- investigated and it's an unrelated, pre-existing property of VIVA's corpus having near-duplicate football stories, not a repair defect; the direct docstore check is the reliable proof here, same lesson as DBT-17's truncated-query false alarm.)
  - 7 new unit tests (`test_dedupe_chunks.py`: loser-selection incl. the FK-conflict abort, dry-run touches nothing, real run deletes the right row+vector+backs up) + 1 new `_embed_batch` test (`test_chunk_faiss_embedding_recovery.py`: a duplicate key within one batch is embedded once) + 1 new ORM test (`test_orm_models.py`: `ChunksTable` declares the constraint). Full suite: 347 passed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** always check for FKs pointing *into* the table you're about to dedup before writing a delete-the-loser migration -- `entity_mentions.chunk_id` wasn't in DBT-09's own evidence section and would have been a silent landmine. `FAISS.delete()` is safe to rely on in this LangChain version; don't default to "leave orphaned vectors for the next rebuild" without checking whether the API actually requires it. `_embed_batch`'s existing-in-FAISS dedup and within-batch dedup are two different bugs that both had to be fixed -- SYS-17/SYS-25 only ever addressed the first.
- **Links:** DBT-09 (#44) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/faiss/chunk_faiss_data_source.py` (`_embed_batch`) · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/table.py` (`ChunksTable.__table_args__`) · `scripts/dedupe_chunks.py` (new), `scripts/migrate_chunks_unique.sql` (new), `scripts/migrate.py` (registered) · `tests/unit/test_dedupe_chunks.py` (new), `test_chunk_faiss_embedding_recovery.py`, `test_orm_models.py` · `backups/dbt09_dedupe_chunks_20260917T155714Z.json` (gitignored) · OPERATIONS.md, CLAUDE.md updated

### 2026-09-17 — QA audit (third pass) of the post-batch work: OPS-01, DBT-17, OPS-04, DBT-20, DBT-09
- **Question / hypothesis:** the five commits after the second re-audit (`77b1570` OPS-01, `8fd6802` DBT-17, `c09c1cf` OPS-04, `32f1942` DBT-20, `12ffccc` DBT-09) each claim verification on production. Do the claims hold when re-derived independently, do the two new destructive scripts leave the stores consistent, is the code fix in DBT-09 complete, and were *both* test suites actually run?
- **Method:** (1) `tests/unit` and `tests/integration` as-is on `dev`. (2) Own read-only probes against `newsaggregator` + `NEWS_L1`/`NEWS_L2` (`pickle` docstores + `faiss.read_index`): duplicate `(sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index)` keys per docstore, every `chunks.embedding_id` resolved in the right level's docstore, metadata agreement, `index_to_docstore_id` contiguity, orphan counts, `entity_mentions.chunk_id` dangling refs. (3) Vector↔text alignment: re-embedded 300 docstore texts per store (150 random + 150 targeted at DBT-09 survivors / DBT-17 repairs) and compared to `index.reconstruct(i)`. (4) DBT-20's population re-derived from scratch (status split, articles, eligibility under `load_all_sitemaps`' WHERE). (5) `Get-ScheduledTask` / `Get-ScheduledTaskInfo` / `Win32_Battery` for OPS-01. (6) `--dry-run` of `migrate.py`, `dedupe_chunks.py`, `backfill_pending_embeddings.py` for idempotence; inspected both backup JSONs. (7) Mutation: reverted DBT-09's `seen_this_batch` guard. (8) Code-path probe: `_embed_batch([c, c])` with `c`'s key already in the docstore, then the same through `save_chunk_data` on `newsaggregator_test` + temp FAISS.
- **Result:**
  - **Data repairs hold.** 0 duplicate groups, index present, `schema_migrations` row present, 0 `embedding_id IS NULL`, all 101,157 rows `embedded`. L2: 79,749 vectors = 79,749 rows, 1:1. L1: 22,591 vectors / 21,408 rows → 1,183 orphans = exactly DBT-20's set. 0 duplicate keys in either docstore, 0 unresolved `embedding_id`s, 0 metadata disagreements, 0 dangling `entity_mentions`. **Alignment: cosine(fresh, stored) min = 1.0000 on all 600 samples** — `FAISS.delete()` did not misalign anything; the dev's "self-query didn't rank #1" is a byte-identical text under another key. All three scripts are idempotent on re-run.
  - **DBT-20's numbers are exact** (1,183 / 601 pending / 582 rejected_filter / all MEDIAINDONESIA / 96 of 12,482). Sharpening: the 601 `pending` are *eligible now* (cooldown expired) — starved by queue position (11,561 pending, LIMIT 1000) and by MEDIAINDONESIA not being run, i.e. DBT-04, not status. Commented on #70.
  - **OPS-01 config is live and correct** (`DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=False`, `StopIfGoingOnBatteries=False`, `StartWhenAvailable/WakeToRun=True`, `NextRunTime 2026-09-18 05:30`, battery present, child runs with `cwd=REPO_ROOT`). Correctly left open pending the observed run.
  - **DBT-09's code fix is half a fix.** `_embed_batch` guards `seen_this_batch` only in the `elif` (new chunk); the `if key in existing` branch appends both copies. Unit probe: two DTOs with key `(9,1,0)`. End-to-end on `newsaggregator_test` + temp FAISS: `psycopg2.errors.UniqueViolation: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "chunks_sitemap_level_index_uq"` → the batch's `save_chunks` rolls back → in `_crawl_newsdetails_inner` that is `logger.error` + `continue`, so every sitemap in the batch loses its `chunks` and `articles` rows for the cooldown window, its fresh vectors are already on disk (orphaned until retry), and `crawl_log` says `completed`. Live batches are PK-distinct since SYS-07/DBT-07, so today's exposure is `specific_sitemaps` callers and repair scripts — which is exactly the path DBT-20's repair will take. → **SYS-26 (#71)**, P2.
  - **The integration gate was red when DBT-09 closed.** `migrate_chunks_unique.sql` was applied to `newsaggregator` only; `test_schema_migrations_has_a_row_for_every_known_migration` failed on `newsaggregator_test` naming the file. The ticket's "347 passed" is the unit suite. Applied the migration to the test DB during the audit → 72 passed / 9 xfailed. → **DBT-22 (#73)**.
  - **`dedupe_chunks.py`'s "backup of every deleted row" is six key columns** (`chunk_id, sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index, embedding_id, referenced`) — no `text_content`; not restorable. FAISS is deleted before SQL and a re-run after a partial failure raises on the already-missing ids. The one run that happened was clean (verified above); the script is now the documented repair tool, so → **DBT-21 (#72)**, P3. DBT-17's backup, by contrast, carries full rows.
  - **DBT-17's "residual design note" was a known gap left in this log instead of a ticket** (`get_existing_sitemap_level_pairs` counts a NULL-`embedding_id` row as saved; `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` can't see it either). → **DBT-23 (#74)**, P3.
  - Mutation: reverting the DBT-09 guard fails exactly one test (`test_duplicate_key_within_same_batch_is_embedded_once`). Unit suite 347 passed as claimed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** the *data* side of this batch is the best-verified work in the log so far — every number re-derives, both stores are internally consistent and aligned. The *code* side shipped with a fix that covers one of two symmetric branches and a test that only exercises the branch that was fixed; write the test for the *other* branch of any `if/elif` dedup before calling it complete. And "full suite" must mean both suites — the integration gate that DBT-06 restored was red within twelve hours and nobody looked. Three traps added above. `scripts/experiments/probe_store_consistency.py` is sitemap-set-level; the two checks that found things here were per-`embedding_id` and per-vector — fold both into OPS-04's eventual tool (commented on #52).
- **Links:** verified DBT-17 (#58), OPS-04 (#52) refresh, DBT-20 (#70) filing, OPS-01 (#9) config · DBT-09 (#44) data verified, code fix incomplete · **new: SYS-26 (#71), DBT-21 (#72), DBT-22 (#73), DBT-23 (#74)** · test DB migrated to `migrate_chunks_unique.sql` (`newsaggregator_test` now at 12/12)

### 2026-09-18 — OPS-01: first unattended scheduled health-check run
- **Question / hypothesis:** OPS-01 (#9) was left open three times because every `health_check` batch so far had been a manual trigger. The operator received a Telegram ⚠️ at ~05:32 WIB this morning — is that the first genuinely unattended 05:30 run, and does every independent record (Task Scheduler, `crawl_log`, JSON report, message text, the 36 h gap check) agree?
- **Method:** `Get-ScheduledTask | Get-ScheduledTaskInfo` (`LastRunTime`, `LastTaskResult`, `NumberOfMissedRuns`) and `.Settings`/`.Principal`; `SELECT date_trunc('minute', started_at), MIN(started_at), MAX(finished_at), COUNT(*) FROM crawl_log WHERE task='health_check' GROUP BY 1` on `newsaggregator`, plus the per-outlet rows for the 09-17 22:xx UTC batch; a per-outlet × batch pivot of `error_msg` prefixes across all four batches to spot flapping; `crawler_health/2026-09-18.json` vs. the rows; `summary_line()` re-derived from the JSON vs. the message the operator pasted; `Get-WinEvent -ListLog Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational` for a scheduled-vs-manual trigger record.
- **Result:**
  - Task Scheduler: `LastRunTime 18/09/2026 05:30:02`, `LastTaskResult 1`, `NextRunTime 19/09/2026 05:30`, `NumberOfMissedRuns 0`; settings unchanged from the 09-17 fix (`DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=False StopIfGoingOnBatteries=False StartWhenAvailable=True WakeToRun=True PT2H`), principal `bdrex/Interactive/Limited`.
  - `crawl_log`: exactly one batch that morning — `log_id 277–308`, `2026-09-17 22:32:10.712900 UTC` (05:32:10 WIB), 32 rows, 21 completed / 11 failed. The other three batches are the documented manual triggers (09-16 22:47, 23:42; 09-17 00:21 UTC). No manual run on 09-18.
  - JSON `generated_at 2026-09-18T05:32:10`, 32 results, row-for-row equal to `crawl_log`. Message text byte-identical to `summary_line()` output: `OK=15, FALLBACK=6, PARTIAL=0, BROKEN=10, ERROR=1 (of 32) | needs attention: <11 unhealthy>, <6 FALLBACK>`.
  - Gap check: 22.1 h since the prior row → below the 36 h threshold → correctly silent (only the ⚠️ arrived, no ⏰).
  - Pivot: per-outlet status **identical** to the 09-17 07:21 WIB run. The only flips in history are batch 1→2 (CNBC/CNN/KONTAN/KUMPARAN/PIKIRANRAKYAT `OK→FALLBACK`, when OPS-02's classification landed; OKEZONE one-off `ERROR` in batch 2). All 17 flagged outlets map to open tickets: SRC-02 ×5, SRC-03 ×2, SRC-04, SRC-05, SRC-06 ×2, SRC-07 ×2, SRC-08 ×4 = 17. Nothing new broke.
  - `Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational` is **disabled** (`IsEnabled=False`) — no event 107/110 record exists to distinguish a scheduled trigger from a manual start after the fact. Today's proof is `LastRunTime`=05:30:02 + the single batch. `wevtutil sl Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational /e:true` (admin) would make future audits definitive; noted on the ticket, not filed.
  - Two things the evidence itself exposed: (a) all 128 `health_check` rows ever written have `started_at = finished_at` to the microsecond — `log_to_db` sets only `finished_at = NOW()` and `started_at` falls back to `DEFAULT NOW()`, both the logging transaction's start, i.e. the *end* of the run (today: rows say 05:32:10, the task started 05:30:02) → **OPS-12 (#75)**, P3. (b) The alert lists the same 17 already-ticketed outlets it will list every morning until SRC-02..08 land, with no "new vs. known" distinction — the shape of alert that stops being read → **OPS-13 (#76)**, enhancement: diff against the previous per-outlet `crawl_log` row.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** OPS-01's Done-when is *observed*, and closed on that basis. Unexercised residuals: catch-up after a genuinely missed window (`StartWhenAvailable` on a `CalendarTrigger` with no `EndBoundary`) — if it doesn't queue, the next run's 36 h gap check fires at ~48 h, so a missed day is late, not silent; `LogonType=Interactive` still requires a logged-on session. The general lesson: a monitoring ticket's Done-when is the *observation*, and the first observation is also the moment to read the alert as its recipient will — "correct and delivered" is not the same as "actionable tomorrow".
- **Links:** OPS-01 (#9) **closed** · OPS-12 (#75), OPS-13 (#76) filed · `crawler_health/2026-09-18.json` (gitignored) · `crawl_log` log_id 277–308

### 2026-09-18 — TEMPO: where do the 60 s go?
- **Question / hypothesis:** every `health_check` batch since 09-16 reports TEMPO as `ERROR: timeout after 60s`, but SRC-06 (#17) was filed on `BROKEN: branch "www" parsed to 0 links` (09-15). Is tempo.co down/slow, or is the crawler burning the budget before `_scrape`? The traps say not to retry with a bigger timeout, so time each stage once instead.
- **Method:** inline probe with the crawler's own `page_loader`: time `get_soup(URL.TEMPO)`; count `<sitemap><loc>` entries matching `-sitemap.xml`; time the first three branch fetches individually and count their `<url>`; then time `TempoCrawler._get_branches(index_soup)` end to end and print what it returns.
- **Result:** index `https://www.tempo.co/sitemap.xml` 0.4 s, 28 entries, all `-sitemap.xml` (`pages`, `politik`, `hukum`, `ekonomi`, `lingkungan`, …). `pages-sitemap.xml` 0.5 s / 47 `<url>`, `politik-sitemap.xml` 0.5 s / 200, `hukum-sitemap.xml` 0.4 s / 200. **`_get_branches`: 155.2 s → 3 branches**, and the three are article URLs (`/newsletter/kebenaran-yang-semakin-mahal-…-2287657`, `/prelude/sampul-…`, `/info-tempo/…`) under the keys `www`, `www.tempo.co/prelude/sampul`, `www.tempo.co/info`. Cause, `tempo_crawler.py:23-43`: `_get_branches` does a `get_soup` per entry (28 sequential fetches — 155 s in the loop although each is ~0.5 s alone; throttling or a slow branch, not isolated, moot once the loop is removed) and treats each leaf sitemap as a sub-index, taking its *first `<loc>`* (an article) as the branch link and deriving the name with `re.sub(r"(.*)(//)(.*)(.tempo)(.*)", r"\3", …)`, which collapses 28 entries into 3 keys. `check_crawlers.py`'s 60 s per-outlet budget expires inside `_get_branches` → `ERROR`; a real `task=sitemap` run has no cap, so it would take 155 s and then `_scrape` an article page → 0 `<url>` → the "0 links" SRC-06 describes.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** same root cause as SRC-06, one layer earlier; the health-check `ERROR` is a timing artifact of a `_get_branches` that shouldn't be fetching at all. Fix direction: return `{section: <section>-sitemap.xml}` straight from the index (filter to `ekonomi`/finance sections) and let the standard `_scrape` read the 200-entry leaf. Don't chase the 155 s. Verify with `--report-dir <scratch>` (OPS-10).
- **Links:** SRC-06 (#17), commented · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/tempo/tempo_crawler.py`

### 2026-09-18 — OPS-12 + OPS-13: per-outlet timestamps and a delta-first health alert
- **Question / hypothesis:** (1) OPS-12: can `check_crawlers.py --log-db` record each outlet's real check window in `crawl_log` — and, since `started_at`/`finished_at` are `timestamp without time zone` columns the pipeline fills with `NOW()`, how does a Python timestamp have to be bound so it lands in the same frame (the 36 h gap check does `NOW() - MAX(finished_at)` in SQL and would silently break on a 7 h offset)? (2) OPS-13: with the per-outlet baseline one `DISTINCT ON (website)` away, does leading the alert with what *moved* produce a message the operator can act on, and does the whole wrapper path (real subprocess → `--json` → decision → Telegram text) survive the emoji on Windows?
- **Method:** timezone probe on `newsaggregator_test`, rolled back: `INSERT … VALUES (:aware_utc_datetime, NOW()) RETURNING started_at, finished_at, started_at AT TIME ZONE current_setting('TimeZone')`. Then, after the code change: `pytest tests/unit` + `tests/integration` (new `tests/integration/test_check_crawlers_crawl_log_live.py`); `check_crawlers.py --website KOMPAS,DETIK,BISNIS,CNBC --diff-db --report-dir <scratch>` (baseline untouched); `--website KOMPAS,BISNIS --diff-db --json` piped into the wrapper's `_parse_report`/`decide_notification`; a full `--quiet --log-db --report-dir <scratch>` run timed against `date -u`; `run_scheduled_health_check.main()` for real with only `notify_telegram.send` stubbed to capture the message; the two new OPERATIONS.md queries against production.
- **Result:**
  - Server `TimeZone = UTC`, psycopg2 2.9.6. An aware `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` bound to the `timestamp` column stored `23:03:07.713094`, `NOW()` in the same statement `23:03:07.711637` — identical frame (1.5 ms apart from call order). `AT TIME ZONE current_setting('TimeZone')` → `.astimezone()` gives `06:03 +07:00`, the right local instant. A naive `datetime.now()` would have stored `06:03` verbatim into a UTC column.
  - Full `--log-db` run: process start `23:14:16 UTC`, `MIN(started_at) 23:14:17.85` (module import), `MAX(finished_at) 23:15:25.85`; 32 rows, **0 with `started_at = finished_at`** (all 128 older rows still have it); durations 0.0–21.2 s for 31 outlets, avg 3.7 s; TEMPO `ERROR: timeout after 60s` = **65.9 s** (60 s budget + the sequential `fut.result()` wait reaching its future). The 70 s wall time is 60 s of waiting for TEMPO (SRC-06). SUARA's 0.0 s is a 1 ms `get_soup → None`, i.e. real.
  - Delta on the 4-outlet run: `Crawler health 2026-09-18: no change since 2026-09-18 05:32 — 2 known (1 BROKEN, 1 FALLBACK)`, correctly against the 05:32 WIB scheduled batch. On the full run: `🔴 NEW: IDXCHANNEL (BROKEN, was OK) · unchanged: 17 known (10 BROKEN, 1 ERROR, 6 FALLBACK)` — IDXCHANNEL had been OK at 05:32; `first branch scraped 0 article links` after 21.2 s. Solo re-check 3 min later (`--diff-db`, no `--log-db`): `OK`, 100 links, 1.6 s → `🟢 RECOVERED: IDXCHANNEL (OK, was BROKEN)`. A transient under 8-way concurrency, not SRC material — but exactly the shape the delta will surface; the wrapper dry run 2 min after that read `🟢 RECOVERED: IDXCHANNEL … · unchanged: 17 known` and would have sent it under the ⚠️ headline (exit 1 from the 11 unhealthy). The OPERATIONS.md last-two-rows query returns the same single flip.
  - Encoding: my own scratch printer crashed with `UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec` on the 🔴 headline — the same thing the wrapper's `logger.info(summary)` would do under Task Scheduler's cp1252 stderr. Fixed by `reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")` on the wrapper's streams and `encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"` on the `subprocess.run` (the child's stderr carries crawler log lines in whatever encoding it had).
  - Suites: `tests/unit` 347 → 388 passed (+41), `tests/integration` 75 passed / 9 xfailed (SYS-10's module, unchanged) including the 3 new live tests. Unit coverage per Done-when: `log_to_db` binds `:started`/`:finished` and the SQL has no `NOW()`; every `check_one` exit path stamps both; the timeout row carries its own window; `classify_change` table incl. OK→FALLBACK = NEW; `compute_delta` new/recovered/changed/unchanged/baseline, per-outlet baseline, pure; `summary_line` first-line assertions for change / no-change / first-run / all-green and byte-equal legacy format; wrapper `decide_notification` for all 4 exit×delta cells plus the knob and the crash-trace fallback.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** bind `crawl_log` timestamps as aware datetimes (rule added to the traps). Compare per outlet, never per batch — the wrapper's own `--log-db` run and any manual `--website X --log-db` would otherwise turn 31 outlets into "baseline" the next morning. Ship the delta *without* multi-run flap suppression: a same-day `--website X --diff-db` settles a surprising NEW in seconds, and hiding a first occurrence is how a real breakage gets a day's head start. Reading the alert as its recipient (the OPS-01 lesson) also applied to the *log*: an emoji in a message you never print is free; the moment it's logged on Windows it's a traceback.
- **Links:** OPS-12 (#75) fixed · OPS-13 (#76) fixed · `scripts/check_crawlers.py`, `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py`, `tests/unit/test_check_crawlers.py`, `tests/unit/test_run_scheduled_health_check.py`, `tests/integration/test_check_crawlers_crawl_log_live.py` · `crawl_log` log_id 309–340 (first real-window batch), 341–372 (wrapper dry run) · OPERATIONS.md "Which outlet is slow, and what changed"

### 2026-09-18 — P2-medium sweep: 9 tickets fixed, 2 more filed and fixed along the way, OPS-04 built and DBT-20 partially resolved
