- **Question / hypothesis:** work through the P2-medium backlog (19 open) rather than the per-outlet SRC-02..08 scraper tickets, which need live network probing per site and don't fit in the same pass. Two (SYS-14 #33, DBT-07 #34) were already marked "Fixed" in this file from a prior session but still showed as open GitHub issues — real, or just unclosed?
- **Method:** `git log --oneline --all | grep -iE "SYS-14|DBT-07"` found both commits, each with its own `Fixes #N` footer, already on `dev` — GitHub only auto-closes via commit footer on the *default* branch, and `dev` has never been merged to `main`, so the closes never fired. Re-verified both live (`tests/unit/test_soft404_detection.py` 6 passed; production `count(*)-count(distinct sitemap_id) from articles` = 0, `articles_sitemap_id_uq` present) and closed them directly — not "fixed today," just unblocked from the ticket tracker. For each of the remaining 9: read the ticket in full, implement, write/extend unit tests, mutation-test the fix (revert the code, confirm the new test goes red, restore), run the full `tests/unit`+`tests/integration` suites, and where the ticket named a live DB check, run it for real against `newsaggregator`/`newsaggregator_test` and quote the output in the closing comment.
- **Result (chronological, each already has its own commit + closing comment with full evidence):**
  - **SYS-26 (#71) fixed** (`31ce455`): DBT-09's `seen_this_batch` dedup guard only covered the new-chunk branch of `_embed_batch`; a key already in FAISS (the SYS-17 recovery path — exactly DBT-20's orphan shape) skipped the guard entirely, so two chunks sharing that key both reached `result`, and `save_chunks`' own unique index then aborted the whole batch. Reproduced the exact `UniqueViolation` on the pre-fix code against `newsaggregator_test`, fixed by moving the guard before the branch split.
  - **SYS-10 (#28), SYS-27 (#77), SYS-28 (#78) fixed** (`bb93a9e`): `backfill_chunks.py` (target of SYS-10) had *three* independent bugs stacked on each other, each hidden behind the last — fixing the `nomic-embed-text` 401 (SYS-10) exposed `HierarchicalChunker.chunk()`'s interface having moved on to DTOs while the script still called the old `(list, dict)` shape (SYS-27, filed mid-fix), and fixing *that* exposed `--dry-run` looping forever because it never writes anything to shrink its own `WHERE chunk_id IS NULL` fetch query (SYS-28, filed mid-fix — caught by a genuinely hung `pytest` process, 20+ minutes, killed via `TaskStop` and reproduced standalone outside pytest to confirm it wasn't a fluke). All three needed fixing before `tests/integration/test_backfill_pipeline.py` (`xfail` since DBT-06) could actually pass. **Lesson: an `xfail(strict=False)` module can hide more than one bug behind the first exception** — don't assume the ticket that unblocks it is the only fix needed; keep going until the suite is green, filing what you find as you go.
  - **SYS-11 (#30) fixed** (`2379549`): `Crawler._get_timestamp` re-expressed `DateTimeReader.convert_date()`'s already-correct `+07:00` (WIB) instant in UTC before returning it, shifting any 00:00–06:59 WIB article onto the previous stored calendar day. `emitennews_crawler.py`'s own override already had a comment naming this exact bug and working around it — the fix was to make the base class match. `test_date_time_reader.py::DT07` had been asserting the *buggy* conversion as if it were expected crawler behavior; renamed and re-scoped rather than left contradicting the new regression guard.
  - **SYS-21 (#42) fixed** (`52f68b4`): `crawl_website`'s if/elif chain had no `else`, and `crawl_websites_in_batch` treated "returned without exception" as success — a typo'd `CRAWL_TASK` or `task=None` ran the whole website list, called zero service methods, wrote no `crawl_log` row, exited 0. Added `VALID_TASKS`/`CRAWLER_DICT` validation up front in `crawl_websites_in_batch` (before any site is touched) plus an `else: raise` backstop in `crawl_website` itself.
  - **SYS-09 (#27) fixed** (`3aaa506`): a sitemap whose fetch/extraction failed was never stamped `last_crawl_attempt` (only `mark_sitemaps_attempted`'s successes are), so it re-occupied a `load_all_sitemaps` slot every run forever. Added `sitemaps.fetch_failures`/`last_fetch_error` (migration, applied to both DBs), computed the failed set as `batch` minus what `batch_crawling_details` actually returned (no change needed to `_get_news_details`'s return type), and excluded `fetch_failures >= SITEMAP_MAX_FETCH_FAILURES` (default 3) from the query. `increment_fetch_failures` also stamps the cooldown, not just the counter — otherwise "one fewer failure" would still look ineligible in a same-second test (caught this while writing the live test, fixed the test's backdating rather than the code).
  - **OPS-04 (#52) partially built** (`c1e14ff`), **left open**: `scripts/check_store_consistency.py` (detection only — table/JSON/exit-code, same contract as `check_crawlers.py`, reads both FAISS docstores via pickle so it needs no embedding model) plus `/api/health`'s new `faiss_docs` field, read the same lightweight way specifically so it can't become another DBT-15 blocking hazard. Live: 4/9 checks nonzero, all at-or-below the 2026-09-16 audit baseline, none growing. The ticket's `--repair` mode (delete/rewrite FAISS + Postgres data) deliberately not built this pass — that's a dry-run+backup+decision session, not a first-pass addition to a detection tool.
  - **DBT-20 (#70) fixed** (`f732861`), using OPS-04's own tooling to find the population: 582 of MEDIAINDONESIA's 1,183 orphaned-L1 sitemaps had been marked `rejected_filter` by `backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`'s headline-only re-check, contradicting direct evidence (a real L1 vector only exists if the original filter passed). `scripts/fix_rejected_filter_with_faiss_evidence.py` flips any `rejected_filter` row with FAISS evidence back to `pending` — general, not MEDIAINDONESIA-specific. Decision on the harder question (re-crawl vs. accept the lower-fidelity FAISS text vs. write off): re-crawl, matching the VIVA/SYS-08 precedent, executed via *nothing but the status correction* — a `pending` sitemap already re-enters the same queue every other pending sitemap uses, so the next scheduled `task=all` run does the actual re-crawl with no dedicated script needed.
  - **OPS-03 (#11) fixed** (`13decfe`): `check_one` always sampled `next(iter(branches.items()))` — whichever branch a sitemap index lists first, often not finance (GRIDID's "Bobo", PIKIRANRAKYAT's "AksaraJabar", DETIK's "suara-pembaca"). `pick_branch()` prefers a name matching `ekonomi|bisnis|market|finance|keuangan|money|uang`. Live-verified against the exact two outlets the ticket named: PIKIRANRAKYAT now samples "PotensiBisnis", DETIK now samples "berita-ekonomi".
  - Suites, cumulative across the whole sweep: `tests/unit` 347 → 436 passed (+89), `tests/integration` 75 passed/9 xfailed → 88 passed/0 xfailed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** a commit with `Fixes #N` in its footer does not close the issue unless the branch it's on is (or gets merged to) the repo's default branch — check `git log` before assuming a ticket needs work; this repo's convention keeps `dev` as the long-lived work branch, so this will keep happening until/unless that changes. Fixing the *named* bug in a ticket is not the same as making its Done-when true when the Done-when is "the test suite passes" — a stale/broken script can have several independent bugs stacked where only the outermost one is visible until the one under it is fixed too (SYS-10/27/28). A repair tool's *detection* half and its *repair* half are different risk classes and don't need to ship together — OPS-04's read-only script shipped today; its data-mutating `--repair` mode did not, on purpose.
- **Links:** SYS-14 (#33), DBT-07 (#34) closed (already fixed, tracker gap) · SYS-26 (#71), SYS-10 (#28), SYS-27 (#77), SYS-28 (#78), SYS-11 (#30), SYS-21 (#42), SYS-09 (#27), DBT-20 (#70), OPS-03 (#11) fixed and closed · OPS-04 (#52) open, partial · commits `31ce455`, `bb93a9e`, `2379549`, `52f68b4`, `3aaa506`, `c1e14ff`, `f732861`, `13decfe`
### 2026-09-18 — QA audit of the P2-medium sweep (SYS-26, SYS-10/27/28, SYS-11, SYS-21, SYS-09, OPS-04, DBT-20, OPS-03, OPS-12/13): 1 P1 filed, 1 reopened, 5 more tickets
- **Question / hypothesis:** the dev handed over 11 closed tickets plus OPS-04's detection half, all committed on `dev` between `b5ba6fc` and `e060d3f`, with "tests/unit 436, tests/integration 88 passed, 0 xfailed". Does each fix hold under mutation, against the production DB, and against the way the pipeline is actually run — not just against the ticket's own test?
- **Method:** both suites as-is; every commit diff read; a byte-level mutation harness (scratch `mutate.py`) re-introducing 19 faithful mutants and running the named guard suite for each, restoring via `git checkout`; production `newsaggregator` read-only via psycopg2 (`crawl_log` cadence, `sitemaps` schema/indexes/`crawl_status`/`fetch_failures`, `chunks.article_id`, duplicate links); `Get-ScheduledTask` for what actually runs unattended; the team's own tools run for real (`check_store_consistency.py`, `fix_rejected_filter_with_faiss_evidence.py --dry-run`, `backfill_chunks.py --dry-run`); a `backfill_chunks.py` real-mode repro on `newsaggregator_test` with a scratch `VECTOR_DB_DIR`; a network probe (`scripts/experiments/probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py`) fetching all 21 HEALTHY sources' live sitemaps through the real crawlers and comparing today's `(link, posted_at)` to the stored rows; `tracemalloc` timing of `web.app._faiss_doc_counts()`.
- **Result:**
  - **Suites:** unit 436 passed, integration 88 passed — matches the claim. `git status` clean after every mutation.
  - **Mutation: 17/19 caught.** SYS-26 (guard back on the `elif`), SYS-09 ×3 (drop the SQL clause — unit-green but the live integration test catches it, as the dev said; never call `increment_fetch_failures`; drop the cooldown stamp — same, integration-only), SYS-11 (`.astimezone(UTC)` back), SYS-21 ×3, OPS-03, OPS-12, OPS-13 ×2, SYS-10 (`nomic-embed-text` back). **Survivors:** SYS-28 — removing the keyset cursor makes `test_backfill_pipeline.py` *hang* (killed at 240 s; no `pytest-timeout` in the venv), i.e. the guard reproduces the bug instead of failing; DBT-20 — removing `AND sitemap_id = ANY(:ids)` from `find_misclassified` (the script would flip all 24,413 `rejected_filter` rows) leaves 436 green. → **DBT-25 (#82)**.
  - **Nothing in this batch has run for real.** `crawl_log` has no `sitemap`/`full_text` row since 2026-09-17 12:32 UTC; the last full-list `task=all` is 2026-06-02; the only registered scheduled job is the 05:30 health check. `fetch_failures` = 0 on all 101,492 rows.
  - **SYS-11 (#30) — code correct, consequence missed → SYS-29 (#84), P1.** `save_sitemaps` dedups on `(link, int(posted_at))` in a Python map with no unique index. The fix moves `posted_at` forward one day for every 00:00–06:59 WIB article, so every such article already stored and still listed dedup-misses and inserts a second row. Live probe across all 21 HEALTHY sources: **4,024 would-insert duplicates on the next crawl, 3,817 of them exactly the +1-day shift** (VIVA 2,511, JPNN 816, TVONENEWS 161, PIKIRANRAKYAT 150, IDXCHANNEL 83, TRIBUN 35, WARTAEKONOMI 26, CNBC 19, GRIDID 12, SINDONEWS 4); 207 (OKEZONE 206, GRIDID 1) are sites re-stamping `news:publication_date`, a pre-existing hole in the same key. The chain is already visible once in production: sindonews `sitemaps` 8478/85673 (same URL, 20260513/20260602) → `articles` 3182/11990, identical 2,688 chars, 5 chunk rows each. Fix direction: dedup on `link`, `UNIQUE(sitemaps.link)` after deduping the 2 existing links.
  - **SYS-10/27/28 (#28/#77/#78) — Done-when met, tool still unusable → DBT-24 (#79), P2.** `_FETCH_SQL` joins on `chunks.article_id`, which the live pipeline never sets (0 of 101,157 non-NULL), so `--dry-run` on production selects **`11961 articles → 27432 chunks`** while articles genuinely without chunks (by `sitemap_id`) = 0. Real mode on `newsaggregator_test` against one live-style chunked article: loads the model, embeds, then `UniqueViolation … chunks_sitemap_level_index_uq` — nothing written only because DBT-09's index exists. Vectors would go to `VECTOR_DB_DIR/faiss_l1.index`, which nothing reads (the commit's own docstring says so and defers to DBT-10, which doesn't track it). `_parse_paragraphs` treats post-SYS-18 prose as one paragraph → ~1.3 L2 chunks/article vs. the live ~8.5; the integration test seeds `json.dumps(list)`, never the production format.
  - **OPS-04 (#52) — detection tool works, `/api/health` regressed → OPS-14 (#80), P2.** `check_store_consistency.py` reproduced the claimed 4/9 nonzero, all KNOWN, none GROWING. But `_faiss_doc_counts()` `pickle.load`s both docstores (37 MB + 83 MB) on every request, inside an `async def`: **2.06 / 2.13 / 2.09 s, 304 MB peak** ×3 — a per-request event-loop stall that grows with the corpus, introduced while citing DBT-15 as the thing being avoided.
  - **DBT-20 (#70) — reopened.** Status flip verified (`--dry-run` → "Nothing to fix"; MEDIAINDONESIA `rejected_filter` 825→243, `pending` 11,561→12,143). Done-when says "decision made **and executed**"; the closing comment executes it via "the next scheduled task=all run (06:00/18:00 daily)", which is not registered anywhere, and the 582 rows (all ≤ 2026-06-02) sit in a 12,143-row `LIMIT 1000` `posted_at DESC` queue for the one serially-crawled source. Enabled, not executed.
  - **SYS-09 (#27) — correct, incomplete → OPS-15 (#81), P3.** The cap is permanent with no reset script (OPS-09 built one for the identical Gemini shape one day earlier), the failed set is "batch minus DTOs" so a source-wide block or a `_get_news_details` regression retires 1,000 rows/run/source after 3 runs ≥ 7 days apart, and `last_fetch_error` is always the same literal. Process: CLAUDE.md's migration list ("must match `scripts/migrate.py`") and OPERATIONS.md's env table were not updated; the comment at `crawler_service_impl.py:116-118` now contradicts the code below it.
  - **SYS-21 (#42) — verified; one residual → SYS-30 (#83), P3.** `crawl_websites_in_batch([], 'all')` (what `process_event({'task': 'all'})` produces) is still `ok=0 failed=0`, zero service calls, exit 0.
  - **SYS-26 (#71), OPS-03 (#11), OPS-12/13 (#75/#76): verified, stay closed.** SYS-26's guard placement is right and the live orphan-repair test exercises it. OPS-03 only changes the *sampled* branch; expect one morning of NEW/RECOVERED noise as PIKIRANRAKYAT/DETIK diff against their old-branch baseline. OPS-12/13's wrapper handles the no-JSON crash path (`report=None` → raw text sent).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **"Verified live" without a `task=all` since the fix means verified in a REPL** — every fix in this batch was tested against the test DB or a hand-run; the pipeline that exercises them last ran on 2026-06-02 and is not scheduled. (2) **When a fix changes how a stored value is derived, grep for every dedup/lookup key that contains that value before closing** — SYS-11's one-line fix is a 3,817-row duplication event waiting for the next crawl. (3) **A script's Done-when of "its test module passes" proves nothing about production when the test seeds its own data format** — run `--dry-run` against `newsaggregator` and check the count is plausible. (4) **Mutation-testing an infinite-loop fix needs `pytest-timeout`** or the guard is the bug. (5) **A closing comment that cites a schedule must name the registered job**, not the runbook's recommendation.
- **Links:** filed SYS-29 (#84, P1), DBT-24 (#79), OPS-14 (#80), OPS-15 (#81), DBT-25 (#82), SYS-30 (#83) · reopened DBT-20 (#70) · comments on SYS-10 (#28), SYS-11 (#30), SYS-21 (#42) · verified closed: SYS-26 (#71), SYS-21 (#42), SYS-11 (#30), SYS-09 (#27), OPS-03 (#11), OPS-12 (#75), OPS-13 (#76), SYS-10/27/28 (#28/#77/#78) · `scripts/experiments/probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py` (new, reusable — SYS-29's Done-when check; exit 1 while any source would duplicate); the mutation harness, `backfill_chunks.py` repro and `/api/health` timing were throwaway scratch, reproduced inline above
### 2026-09-18 — SYS-29: make the sitemap dedup key the link alone and enforce it
- **Question / hypothesis:** the QA batch of 2026-09-18 (SYS-29 P1, OPS-14, DBT-24, DBT-20 reopened, SYS-30, DBT-25, OPS-15) — fix each properly: read the ticket, fix, write the guard, mutate the fix and watch the guard go red, run both suites, run the ticket's own live check against `newsaggregator`, and only then close. SYS-29 first because it blocks every `task=sitemap`/`task=all`. (Heading kept on the first ticket so the trap-line anchors resolve; this entry covers the whole batch. Work ran 2026-09-18 evening into 2026-09-19 morning.)
- **Method:** per ticket, in order —
  - **SYS-29 (#84):** `save_sitemaps` keyed on `(link, int(posted_at))` and pre-fetched by a `posted_at >= earliest-in-batch` window. Changed both: key is `link`, lookup is `link IN (...)` chunked by 1,000 (`find_existing_by_link`, public so the probe uses the real code), `posted_at` on an existing row is left as first seen (decision, documented in the code: `articles.meta_data.posted_at` was derived the same way, and re-stamping sites would churn it). New `scripts/dedupe_sitemap_links.py` (keeper rule = entity_mentions-referenced → has article/knowledge_extracted → has chunks → lowest id; full-row JSON backup; SQL in one transaction *then* FAISS, `--faiss-only` resume — DBT-21's critique applied) and `scripts/migrate_sitemaps_unique_link.sql` (`sitemaps_link_uq`), registered in `migrate.py`/CLAUDE.md; ORM `Index("sitemaps_link_uq", ..., unique=True)`. `scripts/experiments/probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py` rewritten to ask `find_existing_by_link` instead of re-deriving the key.
  - **OPS-14 (#80):** `/api/health`'s count now reads `faiss.read_index(index.faiss, IO_FLAG_MMAP | IO_FLAG_READ_ONLY).ntotal`, cached on the file's `(mtime_ns, size)`; the payload runs via `run_in_threadpool`. Timed the alternatives first (scratch `time_faiss_count.py`).
  - **DBT-24 (#79):** deleted `scripts/backfill_chunks.py`, `tests/integration/test_backfill_pipeline.py`, `tests/unit/test_embedding_id.py`, `tests/unit/test_parse_paragraphs.py`; `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` gets `main(argv) -> summary` and `tests/integration/test_backfill_orphan_chunks_live.py` (production prose format, no-op on a chunked article, level-aware selection, `search_l2()` finds the vectors); PRD §7.5 marked superseded.

SYS-28's mutant is moot (script deleted under DBT-24).

Docs: CLAUDE.md migration list (SYS-09's file was missing), OPERATIONS.md env table (`SITEMAP_RECRAWL_COOLDOWN_DAYS`, `SITEMAP_MAX_FETCH_FAILURES`, `SITEMAP_SOURCE_FAILURE_RATIO`), the stale `crawler_service_impl.py` comment.
