- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Every Phase 0 Done-when holds on the live DB and the regression guards are real** (23/29 + 1) -- the closures stand. (2) The gaps are all *downstream of the writes*, in what the gold set now makes measurable: a stem collision (SYS-42), a fixture built before the metadata it carries was corrected (SYS-44), a sampler that never dedups (SYS-43), and a gate whose precision nobody could measure before 09-20 (SYS-45). **SYS-37 must not train or gate on `filter_gold.jsonl` until SYS-43/SYS-44 are fixed** -- the leak alone inflates its test metrics. (3) Four traps added above. (4) The SYS-34 vocabulary-sampling method was right for the *names it added* and blind to the *names those stems collide with*; the gate-rule audit (5 s, read-only) is the missing second half -- run it after any change to the two keyword lists.
- **Links:** SYS-42 (#137) · SYS-43 (#138) · SYS-44 (#139) · SYS-45 (#140) · DBT-32 (#141) filed · no production writes · `crawler_health/store-consistency-2026-09-21.json`

### 2026-09-21 — Phase 0.5: SYS-42, SYS-43/SYS-44, DBT-32 worked; SYS-45 proposed on SYS-37
- **Question / hypothesis:** the five QA tickets from the Phase 0 audit, in the HANDOFF's order. The open design question was SYS-43/SYS-44's: the HANDOFF said "fix the sampler, `sample` → `label`", but does a re-draw even reproduce the strata the set exists for?
- **Method:** SYS-42 -- `celeb` moved to the word-bounded stem list, whole word `celebrity` onto the substring list, gate-rule audit re-run over all 569 live `(source, category)` pairs, the 452 not-denied pairs frozen to `tests/fixtures/category_vocabulary_not_denied.json` and swept by a test. SYS-43/44 -- a `refresh --dry-run` of the committed sample against the live DB before deciding; then `refresh`. DBT-32 -- the QA mutants re-run after each guard. Mutation harness converts `\n`→`\r\n` for CRLF files (three of the target files are CRLF).
- **Result:**
  - **A re-draw cannot reproduce the 2026-09-20 strata.** `finance_reject` = finance category AND `rejected_filter` AND no body: after SYS-35's re-queue there are **0** such rows in the DB (2,105 → `pending`, 252 → `saved`); `soft_save` = soft category with a saved article: **0** after SYS-34's cleanup. A fresh `sample` today produces a gold set with the SYS-35 recall population missing. The frozen sample is the only record of it, so it was repaired in place (`refresh`) and the sampler fixed for the next deliberately-new set. Documented in the `sample` docstring.
  - **`refresh` on the committed files** (dry-run counts identical to the real run): categories refreshed 336 (JPNN 293, CNN 43) · strata reassigned 141 · duplicate rows dropped 156 (218 rows / 61 groups → one per (source, headline); 1 cross-source wire-copy group kept, on one side) · split rows moved 0 (every one of QA's 46 leaks was an INEWS within-source copy and left with the duplicates) · conflicting-label groups un-labelled 8 → sample 3,844, gold 3,836. INEWS 319 rows / 164 headlines → 164 / 164. Per-stratum test share 18–20% (hard_news gained 130 rows carrying their old split). Six fixture invariants in `TestCommittedGoldFixture`, all red on the old file, green now.
  - **Labeller inter-batch disagreement (SYS-43 Done-when):** of the 61 headlines that appeared more than once in the 2026-09-20 set, **8 (13%) were labelled both `is_news=true` and `false`** by `deepseek-v4-flash` at temperature 0 -- `Moment Hangat Presiden Prabowo … Gontor` ×12 → 7 news / 5 soft. That is the noise floor on *borderline regional features*, not on the set at large (the hand check measured 96%); it is the reason a row that survives dedup out of such a group is re-labelled rather than kept.
  - **SYS-42 live gate audit after the change:** 117 of 569 pairs denied, `celebes` the only change; `celebrity` (OKEZONE) still denied. Mutants: `celeb` back on the substring list → 1 red; gate order swapped → 1 red (the old order test used `bali/sport`, which never matched the allow-set).
  - **DBT-32:** 8/8 re-run mutants red (M08, M18 unit+live, M28, four on `repair_placeholder_headlines.py`, whose write loop is now `apply_titles()` so the live test can drive it without the network). `tests/unit` 657 → 687, `tests/integration` 104 → 106.
  - **SYS-45:** proposed on SYS-37 as an added gate -- allow-set precision ≥ 0.90 on the `test` split with the existing recall gates unchanged -- and the post-probe cleanup of the soft allow-set saves since 2026-09-20 15:20 UTC. Owner decision (stop point 8), nothing shipped.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Before re-drawing a labelled sample, check that its strata still exist in the DB** -- a stratum defined by a state a later repair script clears is only recoverable from the frozen file (or the repair's backup). (2) `refresh` is the right tool for metadata drift (category rewrites: SRC-33 for VIVA and SRC-29 for INEWS will need it again); a re-draw is for a new population. (3) The next stem on either deny keyword list is checked against the frozen not-denied vocabulary by a test; when a source's vocabulary changes, regenerate the fixture from the DB in the same commit as the deny-list change.
- **Links:** SYS-42 (#137) closed `0120f3f` · SYS-43 (#138) / SYS-44 (#139) `811e909`, open for the owner's rubric decision → 8-row `label` → redone hand check (SYS-44 also for SYS-37's first evaluation) · DBT-32 (#141) closed `6250fd3` · SYS-45 (#140) proposed on SYS-37 (#121) · no production writes

### 2026-09-21 — SYS-46: the rubric moves on two boundaries; what a full re-label of the gold set does to the labels
- **Question / hypothesis:** the owner overruled the rubric on the two hand-check disagreements (school/campus rules → soft; a celebrity's own criminal case → soft). A rubric change re-derives every label. How much of the 4k set actually moves, and how much of the movement is the boundary vs the labeller re-rolling borderline rows?
- **Method:** `filter_rubric.py` amended (worked negatives = the two hand-check rows), `PRD.md` one sentence, prompt pinned by a test. Old labels snapshotted (`811e909`), gold cleared, `label --max-calls 1` smoke, then `label --workers 4`: 193 calls, 3,844 rows, 0 rejected, 0 failed, ~2 min. Diff by `sitemap_id` against the snapshot; boundary attribution by headline keywords (school: `sekolah|siswa|kampus|ujian|seragam|guru|...`; celebrity crime: old `law_crime` + `tersangka|kdrt|narkoba|sidang|...`).
- **Result:**
  - **`is_news` agreement old vs new: 3,636 / 3,836 = 94.8%** — 160 news→soft, 40 soft→news. `soft` 1,261 → 1,388 (33% → 36%).
  - **On the two moved boundaries: ~24 of the 160** (17 school-shaped, 7 celebrity-crime). Both motivating rows are soft now; the whole Rizky Billar/Lesti Kejora group (11 KlikSeleb rows) is soft; `Kemenag Batasi Penggunaan HP di Sekolah` is soft.
  - **The other ~136 news→soft and all 40 soft→news are the labeller re-rolling borderline rows** — the same text under a rubric whose relevant sentences did not change: `Unilever Indonesia Raih HR Asia Awards` news→soft (right), `Vaksin Ebola, antara Harapan dan Tantangan` news→soft (an op-ed; right), `Synology Hadirkan Teknologi Penyimpanan Data Baru` soft→news (product PR; wrong). This is the third measurement of the same number: Gemini-vs-DeepSeek 94.8% on 400 rows (SYS-36), 13% disagreement on repeated borderline headlines (SYS-43), now 94.8% run-to-run on the full set. **The gold set's `is_news` noise floor is ~5%; SYS-37's 0.95 recall gate sits on it.** A recall of 0.95 measured against these labels is indistinguishable from perfect; anything below ~0.93 is a real miss.
  - By old topic, news→soft: health_education 30, corporate 26, other_news 24, law_crime 20, politics_government 19. `health_education` 109 → 74 and `other_news` 123 → 91 — the two classes the boundaries touch shrank most; `tech_science` 77 → 69 (already the weak class). Per stratum `is_news`: mixed_bucket 49.6%, finance_reject 77.7%, hard_news 87.9%, viva_general 42.8%, finance_saved 87.8%, econ_politics 81.8%, soft_category 10.3%, religion 60.1%, finance_pending 76.3%, soft_save 16.1%.
  - Fresh hand check written (100 `is_news` across 10 strata + 48 `topic`), old filled CSV in `e69396e`. The Persib-seizure row (`Jelang Laga Persib, Ratusan Barang Berbahaya Disita Polda Jabar`) is still soft — the model error the owner caught last time, unchanged by the rubric.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A rubric change costs a full re-label and a full hand check, and moves ~5% of labels that have nothing to do with the change** — batch the boundary decisions, do not re-label per decision. (2) **Quote the labeller's noise floor next to any gate measured on the gold set**: SYS-37 should report recall/precision with a ±0.05 band, and a majority-of-3 label on the `test` split (3 calls per batch, cents) would halve it if the gate ever has to be argued. Filed as a consideration on SYS-37, not done here. (3) The two worked negatives in the prompt did their job (the exact rows flipped, and the group around them); the heuristic attribution is approximate — the hand check is the arbiter.
- **Links:** SYS-46 (#142) `cd642fa` · SYS-43 (#138) / SYS-44 (#139) close on the hand check · SYS-37 (#121) trains on the result · no production writes

### 2026-09-21 — SYS-37: the linear probe on the MiniLM embedding is below every precision/topic gate, and the gates conflict — stopped, not shipped
- **Question / hypothesis:** the ticket's design — a multinomial LogisticRegression over the `paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2` embedding of `title + body[:500]`, trained on the SYS-36 gold set — meets: finance-strata gate recall ≥ 0.95, general-strata recall ≥ 0.90, mixed-bucket precision ≥ 0.90, allow-set precision ≥ 0.90 (SYS-45), topic accuracy ≥ 0.80. Threshold chosen on 5-fold CV of `train`, everything reported on `test` (763 rows) through the *full* live rule (category `deny` before the probe).
- **Method:** `scripts/train_importance_probe.py --no-write` (the trainer refuses to write the artifact below any gate; asserts its npz-softmax == sklearn `predict_proba` to 1e-6), then `scripts/experiments/probe_design_sweep.py` on cached embeddings: (A) one 13-class multinomial LR, balanced and unweighted; (B) two heads — binary soft-vs-news gate + 12-class topic LR on news rows — gate C ∈ {0.5..16} × class_weight ∈ {None, balanced}; (C) topic C ∈ {0.5..32}; (D) MLP(256) heads as the non-linear ceiling. For each: the τ with the widest minimum gate slack on CV, the five gates on test, and the recall/precision curve as τ moves. Gold at `cd642fa` (3,844 rows, sha256 `59fdd4eca5d2…`), seed 20260921.
- **Result — the ticket's design (A, balanced), test at τ=0.50:** recall_finance **0.994** · recall_general **0.964** · precision_mixed **0.567** · precision_allow **0.827** · topic_accuracy **0.602** (macro-F1 0.581) · uncertain band [0.35, 0.65] holds 11.7% of decisions. It keeps 162 of 174 mixed-bucket rows: `class_weight="balanced"` gives `soft` (36% of rows) a weight of ~0.2, so P(soft) rarely clears any threshold — a "keep everything" gate. Per stratum (n / news / kept / recall / precision): mixed_bucket 174/92/162/1.000/0.568 · finance_reject 119/88/118/1.000/0.746 · hard_news 106/92/102/0.978/0.882 · viva_general 100/45/71/0.889/0.563 · finance_saved 80/70/80/1.000/0.875 · econ_politics 64/54/58/0.944/0.879 · religion 32/19/27/0.947/0.667 · finance_pending 30/22/27/0.955/0.778 · soft_category 40/3/0 · soft_save 18/3/0.
- **Result — the sweep (test, each at its own best τ):** every configuration FAILs ≥ 3 gates. Two heads fix the calibration but sit on the *same* curve: e.g. gate LR C=1 unweighted, τ=0.32 → rec_fin 0.839 · rec_gen 0.745 · prec_mixed 0.830 · prec_allow 0.931 · topic 0.639. MLP(256) heads → 0.822 · 0.800 · 0.827 · 0.932 · 0.633 — non-linearity buys nothing. **The trade-off on the best linear gate (test):**

  | τ | recall_finance | recall_general | precision_mixed | precision_allow | kept |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0.10 | 0.417 | 0.303 | **0.970** | 0.982 | 21% |
  | 0.20 | 0.661 | 0.539 | 0.877 | 0.949 | 39% |
  | 0.30 | 0.822 | 0.709 | 0.847 | 0.928 | 51% |
  | 0.40 | 0.878 | 0.812 | 0.783 | 0.912 | 59% |
  | 0.50 | 0.939 | 0.897 | 0.726 | 0.889 | 68% |
  | 0.60 | **0.967** | 0.921 | 0.683 | 0.878 | 73% |
  | 0.70 | 0.983 | 0.945 | 0.645 | 0.859 | 77% |

  Holding recall_finance ≥ 0.95 → best precision_mixed **0.713** (prec_allow 0.881, rec_general 0.903). ≥ 0.90 → 0.778. ≥ 0.85 → 0.818. Precision_mixed 0.90 needs τ≈0.18 → recall_finance ≈ 0.63. **The recall and precision gates are ~0.2 apart on this signal; no threshold satisfies both.** CV-train shows the same curve (no test-set accident).
- **Result — topic:** 0.60–0.65 across every design; macro-F1 0.58. Confusion is where the labeller's own was: `corporate` → consumer 9 / macro_policy 7 / commodities 6 of 46; `politics_government` → other_news 12 / law_crime 7; `macro_policy` → markets 7 / corporate 5. The two-labeller topic agreement on SYS-36 was **71%**; a 0.80 gate is above what the labels themselves agree on.
- **Why (three causes, in order of weight):** (1) **73% of gold rows are headline-only** — a 384-d MiniLM vector of an Indonesian headline carries "what is it about", not "did something happen"; the soft-vs-news boundary the rubric draws (`Kadin Chairman Optimistic on Infrastructure Investment` soft; `Akali Barcode MyPertamina, Dua Warga Bobol BBM` news) is not linearly present, and the MLP result says it is not non-linearly present either. (2) **Label noise ~5% on `is_news`, ~29% on topic** (SYS-46, SYS-36): an oracle would show ~0.95 precision on a 53%-news bucket, so 0.90 is *theoretically* reachable but only by a near-perfect classifier. (3) The mixed buckets are by construction the rows where the labeller itself flipped 13% of the time.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Stopped per the ticket's rule: below the gate → report, do not ship, do not lower the gate.** No artifact written; `importance_filter.py` keeps the cosine score; `newscrawler/core/importance_probe.py` (weights+sidecar loader, softmax, parity helpers) and the trainer are committed and tested so the next attempt starts from a working harness. (2) **Do not try more linear/MLP heads on this embedding** — 30 configurations, one curve. What can move it is the *input*: a stronger encoder for the probe only (the FAISS embedding stays MiniLM), bodies for the headline-only rows, or a different gate contract. (3) Owner decision needed (HANDOFF stop point 3): options on the ticket.
- **Links:** SYS-37 (#121) — below gate, options posted · SYS-45 (#140) gate included · SYS-44 (#139) fourth Done-when item: the evaluation ran on `filter_gold.jsonl` at `cd642fa` · `scripts/train_importance_probe.py` · `scripts/experiments/probe_design_sweep.py` · no production writes

### 2026-09-21 — SYS-47: changing the probe's input (encoder + bodies) moves every gate to its floor; the last 0.004–0.01 is below what the labels can resolve
- **Question / hypothesis:** SYS-37 was 0.2 below its precision gate on the MiniLM embedding (previous entry). Owner picked option 1: a stronger encoder for the probe only, and bodies for the 73% of gold rows that were headline-only. Does either — or both — reach the five gates?
- **Method:** (1) `scripts/experiments/fetch_gold_bodies.py`: every headline-only gold row fetched through its own crawler's extractor chain (read-only on the DB; nothing persisted) → `tests/fixtures/filter_gold_bodies.jsonl`; `label_filter_gold.py merge-bodies` folded them in (`had_body_at_freeze` keeps the strata frozen) and the rows that gained a body were **re-labelled on title+body** (132 calls). (2) `merge-votes`: the 763 test rows carry the **majority of three** labelling passes (different batch shuffles; +78 calls) — the gates are measured against that. (3) Encoders: MiniLM (384-d), `intfloat/multilingual-e5-base` (768-d, `"query: "` prefix), `intfloat/multilingual-e5-large` (1024-d). (4) The trainer became two heads — a binary soft-vs-news gate and a 13-class balanced topic head — each selected by 5-fold CV on train (gate C and τ over the four τ-dependent gates, least-bad point when infeasible; topic C by CV accuracy), one strict pass on test. (5) Learning curve: CV mixed-bucket precision at finance recall ≥ 0.95 on 25/50/75/100% of train.
- **Result — bodies:** 2,634 / 2,825 fetched (93%; INEWS dead mirrors 117 + PIKIRANRAKYAT 55 missing) → **95% of the set has a body**. Re-labelled on title+body: `is_news` agreement with the headline-only label **93.6%** (74 news→soft, 94 soft→news), topic 88.4% — at the labeller's own noise floor; headline-only labels were not systematically wrong, the body mostly adds signal for the *probe*, not the label. BISNIS article pages fetched 5/5 (only its sitemap is Cloudflare-blocked — SRC-03 note).
- **Result — majority-of-3 on test:** pairwise agreement between passes 96.3%, unanimous 94.5%; the majority overturned 12 `is_news` (1.6%) and 28 topic labels. Non-unanimous rows sit where expected: mixed_bucket 13/174, finance_reject 8/119, hard_news 7/106.
- **Result — the cells** (test split, CV-selected τ; recall_finance / recall_general / precision_mixed / precision_allow / topic; gates 0.95 / 0.90 / 0.90 / 0.90 / 0.80):

  | encoder × input | rec_fin | rec_gen | prec_mixed | prec_allow | topic | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | MiniLM × frozen (27% body), ticket's 13-class LR | 0.994 | 0.964 | **0.567** | **0.827** | **0.602** | 3 fail |
  | MiniLM × frozen, two heads | 0.839 | 0.745 | 0.830 | 0.931 | 0.639 | 4 fail |
  | MiniLM × bodies, two heads | 0.835 | 0.849 | 0.919 | 0.956 | 0.742 | 3 fail |
  | e5-base × frozen, two heads (C=4) | 0.889 | 0.812 | 0.935 | 0.959 | 0.756 | 3 fail |
  | e5-base × bodies, two heads, single-pass test labels, τ=0.50 | 0.967 | 0.940 | **0.887** | 0.915 | **0.788** | 2 fail |
  | e5-base × bodies, majority test labels, least-bad τ=0.38 | 0.951 | **0.898** | **0.889** | 0.950 | 0.808 | 2 fail (−0.002, −0.011) |
  | **e5-large × bodies, majority test labels, least-bad τ=0.39** | **0.946** | 0.916 | 0.923 | 0.947 | 0.824 | **1 fail (−0.004)** |

  CV on train for the e5-large cell: rec_fin 0.938, rec_gen 0.921, prec_mixed 0.898, prec_allow 0.961, topic 0.794 — the four τ-gates are jointly infeasible by ~0.01 at every C. Topic macro-F1 0.809.
- **Result — the trade-off on test for e5-large × bodies** (τ chosen on CV is 0.39; the rest of the curve is information for the operating-point decision, not a selection):

  | τ | rec_fin | rec_gen | prec_mixed | prec_allow | kept |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0.35 | 0.930 | 0.892 | 0.928 | 0.950 | 61% |
  | **0.39 (CV)** | **0.946** | 0.916 | 0.923 | 0.947 | 62% |
  | 0.42 | 0.962 | 0.922 | 0.910 | 0.948 | 64% |
  | 0.45 | 0.962 | 0.928 | 0.898 | 0.939 | 65% |
  | 0.50 | 0.978 | 0.928 | 0.880 | 0.940 | 66% |

  At τ=0.42 every gate passes *on this test split*; on CV the same τ has prec_mixed ≈ 0.89. The 0.03 between the two columns is the resolution of the set, not a property of the probe.
- **Result — learning curve (e5-base × bodies, CV, prec_mixed at rec_fin ≥ 0.95):** 25% of train 0.842 · 50% 0.847 · 75% 0.863 · 100% 0.864; keeping all mixed rows while halving the rest 0.853. **Flat: more labels of the same kind will not move the mixed-bucket gate.** Topic still rises (0.726 → 0.790).
- **Result — the measurement floor:** the finance strata hold ~200 news rows in test; the standard error of a recall of 0.95 on 200 rows is ±0.015, and the labels themselves disagree ~4% run-to-run. A shortfall of 0.004 (one row) is not resolvable by any test split this project can label. The same is true, with the opposite sign, of the precision "passes": 0.923 on 274 mixed rows is 0.90 ± 0.02.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **The input was the lever, and it is now spent.** Encoder: +0.35 on mixed precision at fixed recall (0.57 → 0.92 across MiniLM → e5-large); bodies: +0.05–0.09; cleaner test labels: ±0.02 either way. Nothing further on this axis is cheap: e5-large is already 2.2 GB / ~10 min per 3.8k texts on CPU; fine-tuning the encoder is a GPU-and-days effort. (2) **The five gates as written cannot be certified by this gold set** — not because the probe is weak but because the gates sit at the resolution limit of 763 single-labeller test rows. What can be said: with e5-large every gate is met to within one row on the test split and within 0.01 on cross-validation. (3) **Decision back to the owner (stop point 3, second time):** accept e5-large at its CV threshold with the finance-recall gate recorded as "0.946 ± 0.015, met within the resolution of the set" — an owner decision, not a lowered gate — or hold SYS-37 for a labelled set an order of magnitude larger, or pick another operating point on the curve on the ticket. (4) Runtime note for whichever is chosen: the probe's encoder is loaded *in addition to* MiniLM (which the chunk/FAISS store keeps); e5-large is 2.2 GB RAM and ~0.3 s/article on CPU — measure on the crawl host before a scheduled run (SYS-41 territory).
- **Links:** SYS-47 (#143) · SYS-37 (#121) · SYS-45 (#140, its gate is the `precision_allow` column) · `scripts/experiments/fetch_gold_bodies.py` · `scripts/experiments/probe_input_matrix.py` · `tests/fixtures/filter_gold_bodies.jsonl`, `filter_gold_test_votes.jsonl` · no production writes

### 2026-09-22 — SYS-37 landed: the probe replaces the cosine score; migration, backfill and the CNBC live run
- **Question / hypothesis:** with the owner's decision on the gate tolerance (±0.02, the resolution of the gold set), does the e5-large probe hold up end to end — through the real service, the real schema, the existing corpus and a live mixed-bucket crawl — and what does it say about what the old filter had saved?
- **Method:** artifact written by `scripts/train_importance_probe.py` (e5-large, two CV-selected heads, τ=0.39; gates within tolerance asserted at write time and again by `TestCommittedProbeArtifact`) · `importance_filter.py` rewritten around `ImportanceProbe` (`classify_batch` the one decision, `deny` before embedding, `allow` decided by the probe per SYS-45, hard failure on a missing/mismatched artifact) · service carries `topic`/`topic_confidence` on the DTO, logs the probe keep rate per news category and the uncertain-band share · `scripts/migrate_articles_topic.sql` via `scripts/migrate.py` on `newsaggregator_test` (integration 107) then production (owner ask) · `--backfill --dry-run` then `--backfill` over 11,700 articles (owner ask) · `scripts/experiments/run_mixed_bucket_verification.py --website CNBC --category news --limit 200` through the real `crawl_newsdetails` (owner ask) · `tests/unit` 711.
- **Result — live run, CNBC `news` × 200 (Sept 18–19 rows):** `attempted=200 extracted=200 passed_filter=193 saved=193`, uncertain band 6/200. **Every one of the 7 rejects is soft** (BRI Private PR ×2, bank bjb award PR, BYD Atto 2 product piece, "China Fans Mi Instan" video feature, STAN/IPDN SKD-minimum how-to — the SYS-46 boundary — TASPEN campus PR). **No recipe/prayer/horoscope/gossip/sport/listicle in the 193 saves**; 2–3 borderline features/PR (`LiuGong Perkenalkan Excavator F-Series`, `Guru Bahasa Mandarin Makin Dicari`) ≈ 1.5%. Topics: macro_policy 70 (APBN/DJP/upah minimum), commodities_energy 32 (BBM/migas/Bahlil), international 31, disaster_accident 15, corporate 14, politics_government 13, law_crime 7 — plausible on a full read; the arguable ones are inherently ambiguous (`DJBC Gagalkan Penyelundupan Emas` → macro_policy). Caveat: this bucket turned out news-heavy (96.5% kept), so it demonstrates precision and topic; the recall evidence remains the gold-set number (0.946 ± 0.015).
- **Result — backfill dry-run over the 11,700 saved articles:** topic distribution politics_government 2,395 · macro_policy 2,022 · corporate 1,393 · markets 1,365 · law_crime 1,041 · consumer 908 · commodities_energy 825 · other_news 645 · health_education 427 · disaster_accident 275 · tech_science 256 · international 148. **The probe calls 2,858 of them (24.4%) soft** — the cosine score's accumulated noise (`Klaim Link DANA Kaget Hari Ini`, `Motor Baru 2026 … Skutik Paling Worth It`, CSR reprints). Not touched by the backfill; SYS-45's `cleanup_soft_by_probe.py` reports it by scope (allow-set saves since 2026-09-20 15:20 UTC vs the whole corpus) for the owner.
- **Result — backfill:** 11,700 rows updated in one transaction (backup `backups/sys37_topic_backfill_20260922T021044Z.json`); all 11,893 articles carry a topic, 0 NULL. **SYS-45 cleanup (owner ask):** its own scope — allow-set saves since 2026-09-20 15:20 — held 252 articles, **46 soft (18.3%)**: LIPUTAN6 `bisnis` 26, IDNTIMES `business` 19, TIRTO `ekonomi` 1, every sampled title a tip/quiz/listicle/PR; deleted with backup (236 chunks, 46 L1 + 190 L2 vectors), articles 11,893 → 11,847, stores consistent. The corpus-wide 2,858 (with ~1 in 10 likely real news at 0.92 precision — `Hason Kembali Diusulkan Pimpin PKB Purwakarta`, `i3L Resmi Menjadi Universitas`) went to SYS-48 (#144) for a P(soft)-banded decision, not deleted.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) The probe is live: `INTERESTS`/threshold/margin are gone, the decision is one batch function, and the service cannot start without a matching artifact. (2) **A live run on a news-heavy bucket is a precision test, not a recall test** — pick the bucket for the property you need to show; CNBC `news` was the only allowed ≥200-row mixed bucket today. (3) The corpus the old filter built is ~24% soft by the probe; deleting it is a product decision (SYS-45), not a side effect of the backfill. (4) Runtime: the crawler now loads e5-large (2.2 GB) next to MiniLM; measured fine on this host (31 GB), unmeasured on any other (SYS-41).
- **Links:** SYS-37 (#121) closed · SYS-45 (#140) cleanup pending owner · commits `1701acd`, `e44de43`, `0ac9acd` · `backups/sys37_topic_backfill_*.json` · `crawl_log` CNBC full_text 2026-09-22 article_count=193 · no production writes beyond the three the owner approved (migration, backfill, live run)

### 2026-09-22/23 — Phase 1 finished: SRC-35, OPS-11, DBT-26, DBT-11, DBT-23, DBT-13, DBT-14
- **Question / hypothesis:** the seven tickets left in Phase 1 after SYS-37 landed. Two of them (SRC-35, DBT-11) are only answerable from live evidence — which branches are actually soft, and what the real schema actually holds — so neither was decided from its name or from the ticket's own description.
- **Method:** per ticket, code + unit tests + a live test on `newsaggregator_test` where a DB is involved, mutation checks on the guards, and for SRC-35 a 3-title sample of every ambiguous branch (new `scripts/experiments/sample_branch_titles.py`, DB first, live fetch where we have no rows) plus a before/after fleet probe.
- **Result — SRC-35 (#128): 1,077 → 911 branches fleet-wide, 166 dropped.** One word-bounded vocabulary in the base `Crawler` (`SOFT_BRANCH_WORDS` + `is_soft_branch`/`drop_soft_branches`), applied in `get_news_in_bulk`. DETIK 242→180 · GRIDID 43→22 · JPNN 90→64 · OKEZONE 34→19 · VIVA 53→43 · KUMPARAN 10→4 · TRIBUN 14→10 · SINDONEWS 12→9 · TVONENEWS 7→3 · CNN 9→6 · IDXCHANNEL 17→15 · KONTAN 43→41 · BERITASATU 19→17 · CNBC 10→9 · PIKIRANRAKYAT 127→125 · WARTAEKONOMI 39→36. MEDIAINDONESIA (283) unchanged: every key is a URL (SRC-14 shape).
  **What the sampling changed versus the ticket's proposed regex** — the probe's own `SOFT` pattern over-flags, and this is the whole reason SRC-35 says "verify per source, not per regex":

  | branch | sample (2026-09-22) | call |
  |---|---|---|
  | `teknologi` (CNN/JPNN), `tekno` (SINDONEWS), `ototekno` (OKEZONE/BERITASATU), `tekno-sains` (KUMPARAN) | NASA meteor analysis · Apple foldable · Nvidia humanoid robots · Snowflake–OpenAI deal | **keep** |
  | `kesehatan` (TRIBUN, WARTAEKONOMI) | MBG poisoning victims · Ebola in Africa · BPJS premium rumour | **keep** |
  | `otomotif` (SINDONEWS/JPNN/WARTAEKONOMI/VIVA/CNN) | Toyota global sales down · EV sales in 37 countries · dealer expansion · SIM digital rules | **keep** (SYS-31 made the same call) |
  | `transportasi` (WARTAEKONOMI) | toll traffic, ganjil-genap | **keep** (and the `sport` trap) |
  | `video`/`foto` (IDXCHANNEL) | IHSG analysis · the Menkeu handover | **keep** |
  | `travel` (WARTAEKONOMI) | "Rupiah Melemah, Turis Asing Makin Ramai Serbu Indonesia" | **keep — per-crawler exception** |
  | `english` (KONTAN) | "GLOBAL MARKETS-Tech Rally Boosts Asian Stocks" | **keep — per-crawler exception** |
  | `english` (JPNN) | "4 Sifat Pria Ini Bikin Wanita Bertekuk Lutut" | drop |
  | `lifestyle` (BERITASATU/CNBC/SINDONEWS/KONTAN), `gaya-hidup` (CNN/VIVA) | Andre Taulany · savings tips · stretching routine · Java Jazz | drop |
  | `KlikSeleb` (PIKIRANRAKYAT) | gadget promo listicles | drop |
  | `gadget`, `fotostop_*`, `games-news` (DETIK) | FBe discounts · camera gear · GTA 6 | drop |

  **One finding filed rather than fixed: SRC-42 (#145)** — SINDONEWS `kalam` (haji operations: Dam Haji distribution, corridor gate, kloter arrivals) and JPNN `ramadan` (mudik logistics, a Menhub decision) are **news by the rubric** but are denied at both branch and category level. Dropping the branch loses nothing today (SYS-31's category deny already rejected every row they produce), so SRC-35 kept the names for consistency and SRC-42 proposes handing both to SYS-37's probe. `religi` sampled correctly soft (`Doa Pelunas Utang`) and stays.
  `check_crawlers.py` now prints `<branch> <listed>-<soft>` per source (live: CNN `ekonomi 9-3`, TVONENEWS `ekonomi 7-4`, WARTAEKONOMI `agribisn 39-3`).
- **Result — DBT-11 (#46):** the drift was identical on both databases: `chunks.article_id` and `sitemaps.cluster_id` in the DB only; `articles.sitemap_id` Integer vs bigint; `chunks.chunk_level` Integer vs smallint; `crawl_log` and `newsletter_digests` with no model at all. All mirrored, and **`chunks.article_id` is now written** — chunks are saved before the articles row exists, so `save_newsdetails` links the batch in one `UPDATE ... FROM articles` after the insert; `migrate_chunks_article_id.sql` backfilled **44,968 of 99,221** rows (0 left; the other 54,253 are orphan chunks with no article at all — OPS-04/DBT-16 territory). `tests/integration/test_schema_matches_orm.py` reflects every table both ways with an explicit allow-list (`chunks.fts_vector`, `schema_migrations`); four drift mutants go red. `is_embedded=True` was left alone with its reason recorded: only articles whose chunks were just embedded ever reach that mapper.
- **Result — OPS-11 (#67):** `extract_all` returns an `ExtractionResult` (an int carrying attempted / transient_failures / first error); both website-agnostic tasks are bracketed with `crawl_log` under `website='ALL'`, and a run where every call failed without a response is `status='failed'` with the first error instead of 50 unread warnings. Live test on the test DB proves the row and that `knowledge_extract_attempts` stays 0 (OPS-09's semantics). The runbook now says to read that row *before* the extraction counts.
- **Result — DBT-23 (#74):** a level counts as saved only when no row at it lacks an `embedding_id` (`HAVING COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE embedding_id IS NULL) = 0`), and `save_chunks` upserts on the DBT-09 index, so the DBT-17 shape is repaired by the normal save path. Live: a level with one NULL row is offered again; `save_chunks` fills that row (same `chunk_id`, no duplicate).
- **Result — DBT-26 (#85):** `_FETCH_SQL` excludes any sitemap with a `chunks` row or an L1 vector and logs how many were skipped; dropping either exclusion goes red against a live fixture (plain / with-chunks / with-L1).
- **Result — DBT-13 (#48) / DBT-14 (#49):** the `CHUNK_*` env vars reach `HierarchicalChunker` at every call site (defaults read from `constants` at call time), and `overlap >= max` / `min > max` now raise at construction instead of looping forever in `_split_with_overlap`; the dead `full_text_scraper_lambda_handler.py` is deleted (nothing referenced it).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Sample the branch, never the name** — five of the names the ticket's own regex flagged are news desks, and one of them (`transportasi`) is the trap that started the rule. (2) A drop that "changes nothing observable" because a later stage already rejects the rows is still worth a ticket when the two stages agree for the *wrong reason* (SRC-42). (3) An integration test that seeds rows in a shared DB must neutralise other modules' rows when the code under test selects globally (`get_articles_pending_extraction` took four articles instead of two until the OPS-11 fixture parked the rest). (4) `scripts/migrate.py` is the only thing keeping the two databases equal, and the new reflection test only sees the test one — apply migrations through it, both places.
- **Links:** SRC-35 (#128) `c0a5acd` · OPS-11 (#67) `edb47a1` · DBT-23 (#74) `d06c9d9` · DBT-26 (#85) `79cb527` · DBT-11 (#46) `70c48ca` · DBT-13/DBT-14 `3b21ec3` · SRC-42 (#145) filed · production writes: `migrate_chunks_article_id.sql` (owner-approved, 44,968 rows)

### 2026-09-23 — QA audit of Phase 0.5 and Phase 1: independent re-measurement, 24 mutations, a live fleet sweep and a read-only filter dry run
- **Question / hypothesis:** do the thirteen Phase 0.5 / Phase 1 closures hold when the evidence is re-derived from scratch rather than read off the tickets? Three things were worth checking independently: (a) the probe artifact's sidecar reports its own metrics, so nothing in CI proves the committed weights achieve them; (b) SRC-35's fleet numbers came from the team's own probe script, which uses a private regex rather than the shipped `is_soft_branch`; (c) the SYS-37 live verification ran on CNBC `news`, which turned out 96.5% news — precision evidence, not recall.
- **Method:** (1) recompute the five gates from the committed `importance_probe.npz` + `filter_gold.jsonl`, re-encoding 24 test rows with a freshly loaded `multilingual-e5-large` to prove the cached embedding matrix is genuine; (2) 24 mutations — one per closed fix — each applied to the working tree, `tests/unit` (or the matching integration file) run, file restored; (3) fleet sweep of all 30 registered crawlers with the **shipped** `Crawler.is_soft_branch`/`drop_soft_branches`, counting URL-keyed branches; (4) `scripts/check_crawlers.py` over 10 outlets, `--report-dir` to a scratch dir, no `--log-db`; (5) a read-only dry run in the VIVA-audit shape (real `batch_crawling_details` + real `classify_batch`, nothing written) on SINDONEWS `scope` x25 and TRIBUN `nasional` x20 — two buckets the CNBC run did not cover; (6) live-DB integrity queries; (7) filter latency and RSS on 100 real bodies.
- **Result — the probe reproduces exactly.** Recomputed from the committed weights on the 763-row test split at tau=0.39: recall_finance **0.9459459459**, recall_general **0.9156626506**, precision_mixed **0.9230769231**, precision_allow **0.9473684211**, topic_accuracy **0.824**, uncertain band **0.1180** — identical to the sidecar to every printed digit, per-stratum table included; `gold_sha256` matches the committed fixture; re-encoded rows are bit-identical to the cached matrix. A useful trap surfaced while doing it: `gate_metrics(rows, probs, ...)` reads P(soft) from `probs[:, soft_idx]`, so handing it the **topic** head's matrix instead of the gate head's silently returns a different, better-looking recall (0.994 / 0.976) and a worse precision (0.831). The trainer passes the right one; the API makes it easy not to.
- **Result — 24 of 24 mutations caught.** Every Phase 0.5 / Phase 1 fix is guarded: SYS-42 (both the word-list move and re-adding `celeb` as a substring), SYS-45's allow-gate short-circuit, `category_gate` deny-before-allow, the probe's strict `<` threshold, a missing artifact, an encoder/sidecar mismatch, an encoder/probe dim mismatch, the topic dropping off the DTO, `drop_soft_branches` removed from `get_news_in_bulk`, `is_soft_branch` degraded to substring matching, `SOFT_BRANCH_EXCEPTIONS` ignored, all three OPS-11 `crawl_log` paths, DBT-23's `HAVING` clause and its upsert, DBT-13's env defaults and its overlap validation, DBT-32's requeue `NOT EXISTS` and NULL-category normalisation. Two are caught only by `tests/integration` — DBT-26's evidence exclusion and DBT-11's ORM drift + `chunks.article_id` link — so a unit-only run signs off neither.
- **Result — live DB matches the closures.** `articles` 11,847 rows, **0 without a topic or confidence**; `chunks` 99,221 with 44,968 linked and **0 chunks that have an article but no `article_id`, 0 mismatched** (the 54,253 unlinked all belong to the 9,447 orphan-chunk sitemaps with no article at all); `schema_migrations` carries both new migrations; `migrate.py`'s `MIGRATIONS` and CLAUDE.md's list agree on all 16 files, same order. Gold set: 3,844 rows, 1 duplicate-headline group left (was 218), **0 train/test leakage, 0 conflicting labels, 0 JPNN/CNN URL categories**, every test row carries `label_votes`.
- **Result — SRC-35's 166/1,077 is exactly right, and 340 branches are keyed by URL.** The shipped helper reproduces the ticket's fleet numbers per source. But 31.6% of the fleet's branch keys are URLs, which the word-bounded vocabulary can never match: MEDIAINDONESIA **283 of 283**, VIVA 29 (SRC-33), **OKEZONE 23**, **DETIK 5**. Eleven of OKEZONE's are unambiguous soft verticals still listed today (`women.okezone.com/beauty|fashion|food|travel|momandkids|life|health`, `celebrity.okezone.com/hotgossip|musik|movie|tvscoop`) — the exact hosts SRC-35's last Done-when item claims are "no longer listed". Filed as **SRC-43 (#146)**. Cross-check against the corpus was otherwise clean: of 569 distinct live `(source, category)` pairs, 105 are dropped by the helper and **not one of them has ever produced a saved article**, and none is in the filter's allow-set — the drop costs no observed recall.
- **Result — the filter behaves on buckets nobody had run it on, except one class.** TRIBUN `nasional` (allow desk): 17/20 kept, the three rejects a "what day is it" listicle, a TASPEN campus PR piece and "50 Ucapan HUT ke-81 PMI" — SYS-45's "a news desk can still be rejected" working as designed. SINDONEWS `scope` (a geopolitics **analysis column**): 18/25 kept, 15 tagged `international`, and near-duplicate op-eds land on both sides of tau — `Perang Iran Ubah Tatanan Dunia` 0.34 keep vs `Perang Iran Berakhir, Timur Tengah Akan Berubah Total` 0.47 reject. The rubric never rules on opinion/analysis/editorial content, and the gold set labels those buckets 8 news / 11 soft, so the boundary is arbitrary by construction. 1,505 sitemap rows sit in opinion buckets today and 124 are already saved as news. Filed as **SYS-49 (#147)**.
- **Result — runtime cost, measured.** `SemanticImportanceFilter()` constructs in 5.8 s; scoring 100 real articles takes **353 ms/article** one at a time (what the crawl loop does) vs **290 ms** batched — batching is worth only 1.2x, so the per-article `classify()` call in `crawl_newsdetails` is not the thing to change. Process RSS reaches **3.9 GB** with e5-large loaded and scoring, before `DataFlowRepositoryImpl` adds MiniLM and both FAISS indexes. ~30 min of filter time per 5,000 articles (SYS-41's target volume). Health check over 10 outlets after the SRC-35 change: 7 OK, 3 FALLBACK, 0 BROKEN.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A sidecar that reports its own metrics is not a test of them** — recomputing from the committed weights took 90 s with the embedding cache and is the only thing that makes `gates_met` mean anything; the CI test asserts the sidecar's numbers against the floors, which a hand-edited sidecar would pass. (2) **A fix applied at the one enforcement point is still only as good as its input**: SRC-35 is correctly placed and correctly word-bounded, and it still does nothing for a third of the fleet because three subclasses hand it a URL instead of a name. Check the *inputs* of a single-choke-point fix, not just the choke point. (3) **Verify a fleet claim with the shipped function, not the probe script** — `probe_branch_scope.py`'s private regex and `Crawler.is_soft_branch` are different predicates, and only one of them ships. (4) The read-only dry run cost ~45 fetches and found the one content class the rubric never ruled on; run it on a bucket the last verification did *not* cover, and pick the bucket for the property you need (CNBC `news` at 96.5% news could not have shown this).
- **Links:** SRC-43 (#146) · SYS-49 (#147) · OPS-21 (#148) · DBT-33 (#149) · DBT-34 (#150) · DBT-35 (#151) and DBT-36 (#152), filed 2026-09-23 on the owner's decision after the audit report: the three "guards that do not guard" the audit found but had judged below the ticket bar — the circular sidecar gate assertion, `gate_metrics`'s ambiguous matrix, and `classify_batch`'s silently-shortened return · audited closures: SYS-42 (#137), SYS-43 (#138), SYS-44 (#139), SYS-45 (#140), DBT-32 (#141), SYS-46 (#142), SYS-47 (#143), SYS-37 (#121), SRC-35 (#128), OPS-11 (#67), DBT-11 (#46), DBT-13 (#48), DBT-14 (#49), DBT-23 (#74), DBT-26 (#85)

### 2026-09-23 — SYS-49: the opinion boundary, and what a rubric change costs when it is enforced at the category instead of the probe
- **Question / hypothesis:** the owner ruled (2026-09-23) that opinion/analysis/editorial columns are **soft**. Two things to establish: does enforcing that at the *category/branch* level actually give near-identical op-eds one verdict (the probe demonstrably cannot), and what does the re-label + retrain do to the five gates?
- **Method:** (1) live DB sweep for opinion-shaped categories, 4 titles sampled per bucket before any name was denied (CLAUDE.md rule). (2) `filter_rubric.py` amended with the ruling + two worked negatives; `OFFTOPIC_CATEGORIES_EXACT` and `SOFT_BRANCH_WORDS` gained the five live names plus `tajuk`/`opinion`/`editorial`, word-bounded; the six pairs removed from `category_vocabulary_not_denied.json` in the same commit. (3) **Targeted** re-label: the 20 gold rows in opinion buckets stripped and re-labelled by `deepseek-v4-flash` (2 calls) — *not* a full re-label, per SYS-46's measured conclusion that a full pass moves ~5% of labels unrelated to the change. (4) `label_filter_gold.py refresh` to re-run stratum assignment. (5) retrain on e5-large, strict test-split pass.
- **Result — the samples were unambiguous.** All five buckets are commentary, not events: CNBC `opini` "Ekonomi Pancasila dan Strategi Rantai Pasok Hijau", MEDIAINDONESIA `editorials` "Pastikan IKN bukan Proyek Mangkrak", `kolom-pakar` "Aroma Politik dalam Putusan MK", `surat-pembaca` "Kemacetan di Jalan Raya Lenteng Agung", SINDONEWS `scope` "Standar Ganda Amerika...". `surat-pembaca` (500 rows) was **not** in SYS-49's original count of 1,505 — the live total is **2,005 rows** (1,813 pending, 124 saved, 68 already rejected).
- **Result — the labels were incoherent exactly as predicted, and the fix is not the classifier.** The 20 gold rows were 8 news / 12 soft; re-labelled under the amended rubric they are **0 news / 20 soft, 8 flips**, and all three `test`-split rows kept their existing label, so the majority-of-3 property on the test split is untouched. The four near-duplicate Iran analyses now get **one verdict** (`deny`) — while the probe, asked the same four with the category hidden, *still* splits them: 0.22 keep vs 0.44 / 0.45 / 0.55 reject. **A classifier cannot separate near-identical texts; only the boundary can.**
- **Result — the retrain costs ~0.005 on every gate, and moves the uncertain band a lot.** Test split, before -> after: recall_finance 0.9459 -> **0.9405**, recall_general 0.9157 -> 0.9096, precision_mixed 0.9231 -> 0.9206, precision_allow 0.9474 -> 0.9441, topic_accuracy 0.8240 -> 0.8220, macro-F1 0.8093 -> 0.8076. All five inside floor − `GATE_TOLERANCE` (0.02) and far inside the labels' ~5% noise floor. **The uncertain band jumped 0.118 -> 0.2045**, because 5-fold CV re-selected the gate head at `C=1.0` (was 4.0) and the topic head at `C=16.0` (was 8.0): more regularisation, probabilities closer to the middle. 15 sample rows also changed stratum (`mixed_bucket`/`econ_politics_boundary` -> `soft_category`/`soft_save`) because `assign_stratum` reads the category gate — all 15 were opinion rows, nothing collateral.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **When two texts are near-identical and the labels disagree, move the boundary, not the model.** Three passes were spent tuning a signal (SYS-31/34/35) before SYS-37 replaced it; SYS-49 is the same lesson one level up — the category is free, reliable and auditable, the classifier is none of those on this class. (2) **Re-label the affected rows, not the whole set**: 2 calls and zero unrelated churn, against SYS-46's measured ~5% churn for a full pass. (3) **A rubric change is not a labels-only change** — `assign_stratum` reads `category_gate`, so denying a category silently re-stratifies the gold set and therefore moves which rows each gate is measured on; run `refresh` and read the strata diff before trusting a before/after comparison. (4) **Watch the uncertain band across a retrain**: the gates can all hold while calibration shifts enough to double the band, which is what an OPS-18 tie-break would act on.
- **Links:** SYS-49 (#147) · SYS-37 (#121) · SYS-46 (#142) · SYS-48 (#144, inherits the 124 saved op-eds) · SYS-40 (#126) · `newscrawler/core/models/importance_probe.{npz,json}` · no production writes

### 2026-09-23 — SRC-43: three crawlers keyed branches by URL, and one of them was losing 38 of 72 sections
- **Question / hypothesis:** SRC-43 says OKEZONE (23), DETIK (5) and MEDIAINDONESIA (283) key branches by their URL so SRC-35's drop cannot see them. Name them properly and the soft verticals should drop — but what *else* does the naming change?
- **Method:** fetched each source's live sitemap index, printed the raw `<loc>` shapes and the keys the shipped `_get_branches` produces, then rewrote each with `re.match` + skip. Verified by key-by-key diff against the pre-change logic, and by a fleet sweep with `probe_branch_scope.py` (now using the shipped `Crawler.is_soft_branch`, not its own regex).
- **Result — OKEZONE was far worse than the ticket described.** Its regex captured the **subdomain only**, so every section under a subdomain collapsed onto one dict key and all but the last was silently discarded: `news.okezone.com`'s twelve national/regional desks (`nasional`, `megapolitan`, `international`, `nusantara`, `bali`, `jabar`, `jateng`, `jatim`, `yogya`, `sulsel`, `sumsel`, `sumut`) were the single key `news`, and eleven of them have never been crawled. **72 `<loc>` produced 34 keys.** Host+path naming gives **49 sections, 0 URL-keyed, 22 dropped soft** (7 `women/*`, 4 `celebrity/*`, 6 `bola/*`, 5 `sports/*`). Filed as **SRC-44 (#154)**. Also settled: every section publishes both `sitemap.xml` and `sitemap-web.xml`, and the `-web` one carries the same URLs **without** `news:title`/`news:publication_date` (100 urls, checked on `news/nasional`) — it is the lastmod-only duplicate SYS-32 exists to drop. 49 sections, **0 web-only**, so listing only `sitemap.xml` loses nothing.
- **Result — DETIK's fix is provably a no-op on its existing branches.** Key-by-key diff: 237 named keys before, **0 lost**, exactly 5 gained (`indonesianow/*`), and every shared key points at the same link. `indonesianow.co` is DETIK's English edition — sampled: "Influenza Cases Rise in Indonesia", "Update on KM Virgo SAR: 18 Dead", "Anak Krakatau Status Downgraded" — real news, in English, duplicating desks already listed, so `indonesianow` joins the vocabulary on the same ground as `english`.
- **Result — MEDIAINDONESIA is nameable after all, and its index is mostly not news.** The keys are `news`, `category`, `video`, `article-YYYY-MM`, `gallery-YYYY-MM` (~130 of each date partition). Naming them changes **nothing** about what is listed — none of those words is in the vocabulary — but it ends the false `soft_branches=0`. Only `sitemap-news.xml` (904 urls) carries `news:title`; `sitemap-article-2026-09.xml` is 8,374 urls with none, `sitemap-gallery-2026-09.xml` is 476 `detail_galleries` photo pages. The scope/volume decision is filed separately as **SRC-45 (#155)** with those numbers rather than smuggled into a naming fix.
- **Result — the fleet.** 1,092 branches listed, 182 dropped as soft, **29 keyed by URL — all VIVA** (SRC-33, the one ticketed whitelist), down from 340. An offline sweep handing every registered crawler a `<loc>` no crawler can name found **2 of 30** that still key it by URL: IDXCHANNEL and SINDONEWS, both clean on today's live index, filed as **SRC-46 (#156)** and pinned as xfail in `tests/unit/test_soft_branch_drop.py`.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A `re.sub` branch name has two failure modes, not one** — the known one is "no match, key is the URL"; the silent one is "matched too little, several sections share a key and the dict drops all but the last". The second leaves no trace: the count looks plausible and `check_crawlers` says OK. Count `<loc>` entries against keys. (2) **Prove a refactor is a no-op with a key-by-key diff**, not by reading the regex — it took one command and it is the whole evidence that DETIK's 237 branches were untouched. (3) A source that cannot be named at branch level is usually a source nobody has *looked* at: MEDIAINDONESIA's index was legible all along, and the "filtered at category level only" escape hatch was not needed.
- **Links:** SRC-43 (#146) · SRC-44 (#154) · SRC-45 (#155) · SRC-46 (#156) · SRC-35 (#128) · SRC-39 (#133) · SRC-33 (#119) · `scripts/experiments/probe_branch_scope.py --fail-on-url-keys` · no production writes

### 2026-09-23 — DBT-35: what the circular gate test was hiding, measured by putting a bad artifact in front of it
- **Question / hypothesis:** the gate test asserted the sidecar's own `metrics_test` against the floors, so nothing executed the committed weights. Does a recomputation actually catch anything the existing suite did not, and what does it cost?
- **Method:** committed the test-split embedding matrix (`tests/fixtures/probe_test_embeddings.npz`, 763 x 1024 float32, 2.8 MiB) keyed by encoder + gold sha256 + a sha256 of the exact texts; added a test that loads the committed `.npz`, applies the live rule (category deny, then the gate head at tau) and asserts the five recomputed gates against their floors **and** against the sidecar to 1e-9. Then mutated the artifact the way a bad merge would — `gate_coef *= 0.85`, shapes, class list and gold hash all untouched — and ran the whole suite.
- **Result — the mutation is invisible to everything except the new test.** `tests/unit` under the mutated artifact: **2 failed, 897 passed**. The two failures are the recomputation and the weights-vs-sidecar comparison; `test_sidecar_records_metrics_meeting_the_gates` **passed**, as did every shape/class/gold-hash check, which is exactly the blind spot the ticket described. Recomputed recall_finance read 0.9027 against the sidecar's 0.9405.
- **Result — cost.** The recomputation is ~2 s with the committed matrix (it is a matrix multiply); the whole `test_importance_filter.py` module runs in **28 s**, of which ~20 s is the separate slow test that re-encodes 24 sampled rows through e5-large to prove the committed matrix is genuine encoder output (max abs delta 0.0 vs a fresh encode). Full `tests/unit`: 46 s. The owner accepted a slower suite for an honest one; it cost 14 s.
- **Result — `gate_metrics` can no longer be handed the wrong matrix.** Its signature is now `(rows, p_soft, topic_pred, tau)` — the two quantities it actually uses — so passing a 13-column probability matrix is a `ValueError` naming the mistake instead of a silent 0.994 recall. `assemble()` is deleted; it existed only to build the ambiguous matrix, and scaling the news columns by `1 - P(soft)` never changed the argmax, so no number moved.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Commit the embedding matrix, not the trust.** Keying it by (encoder, gold sha256, text sha256) makes the fast path honest and makes a fixture edit fail loudly; a separate re-encode test keeps the matrix itself from becoming the next unverified artifact. Without that second test the cache is just the same problem moved. (2) **A guard's worth is measured by mutating what it guards** — asserting the new test goes red is the only evidence that distinguishes it from the one it replaced.
- **Links:** DBT-35 (#151) · SYS-37 (#121) · OPS-18 (#123, the next caller of `gate_metrics`) · DBT-25 (#82) · `tests/fixtures/probe_test_embeddings.npz` · no production writes

### 2026-09-23 — SRC-45: excluding archives and galleries fleet-wide, and why the branch name was the wrong test
- **Question / hypothesis:** the owner widened SRC-45 from MEDIAINDONESIA to the whole fleet — *"exclude archives, galleries, photos, something like that, not just from MEDIAINDONESIA, but also other sources."* Can that be done by adding `video`/`foto`/`gallery`/`archive` to `SOFT_BRANCH_WORDS`, i.e. by name?
- **Method:** dumped every registered crawler's full live branch key set (1,092 keys, 30 sources) and matched them against a wide archive/gallery/photo/video/multimedia/infografis/tv pattern. For every candidate leaf: sampled 3 live titles, **then fetched 2 of its articles and measured the extracted body through that crawler's own `_get_whole_text`** — the step a title sample alone does not cover.
- **Result — 297 candidates across 8 of 30 sources**, and the name predicts the substance badly:

| section | body chars | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| MEDIAINDONESIA `article-YYYY-MM` (140 partitions, 2015-02 → 2026-09, ~1.18M URLs) | 2333, 3179 | real articles — the back-catalogue |
| MEDIAINDONESIA `gallery-YYYY-MM` (140, ~67k URLs) | 365, 370 | `detail_galleries` photo pages |
| MEDIAINDONESIA `video` | 1054, 1118 | `detail_video` pages |
| IDXCHANNEL `video` | **205, 198** | captions on a player (`MIN_ARTICLE_CHARS` is 200) |
| IDXCHANNEL `foto` | **597, 842** | full reporting: "Komisi V DPR Setujui Anggaran Kemendes PDT Rp2,785 Triliun" |
| BERITASATU `multimedia` | **759, 899** | full reporting: "Babak Akhir BLBI: Utang Jumbo Rp 640 Triliun Lunas" |
| VIVA `viva-visual` | **1157, 2514** | full articles |
| IDXCHANNEL `infografis` | (10 urls) | macro-policy news: "Utang Baru Rp781,86 Triliun Masuk RAPBN 2026" |
| KONTAN `infografik`, OKEZONE `foto`/`video` | 0 urls | empty or dead |

  **Four sections are named after a medium and publish ordinary journalism.** A blanket name rule would have dropped them — the `transportasi`/`sport` and `celebes`/`celeb` false positive for the third time.
- **Result — the split that ships.** `gallery`/`galleries`/`galeri`/`video`/`foto`/`photo` join `SOFT_BRANCH_WORDS`; `multimedia`, `visual`, `infografis` and `tv` deliberately do **not**, because on this corpus their default meaning is news. The keeps are per-crawler `SOFT_BRANCH_EXCEPTIONS` carrying their char counts (IDXCHANNEL `foto` + `infografis`); BERITASATU and VIVA need no exception because their words were never added. **This reverses half of SRC-35's recorded decision**: `video`/`foto` were kept on 2026-09-22 from titles alone ("IDXCHANNEL: IHSG analysis, the Menkeu handover"), and measuring bodies splits them — `foto` holds up, `video` does not. Its titles are still informative ("Analisis Saham Pilihan ANTM, MDKA, AMMN") which is exactly why a title sample could not catch it.
- **Result — archives are a shape, not a word.** `Crawler.is_archive_branch` matches a `YYYY-MM` calendar partition in the key, bounded so a bare year cannot match (`piala-dunia-2026`, `euro-2024` are tournament desks, not archives). Writing `article` into the vocabulary instead would have denied the most ordinary section name there is on every other source. The rule is fleet-general, so the next source to publish month partitions is covered without another ticket.
- **Result — fleet, live, with the shipped helpers:** **1,092 branches listed → 327 dropped as soft + 140 dropped as archive → 625 crawled**, 29 keyed by URL (VIVA, SRC-33). Before this change: 182 dropped, 910 crawled. MEDIAINDONESIA goes from 283 listed to **2 crawled** (`news`, `category`) — its `news` sitemap is 904 URLs of current Google-news-format articles and is the only one of its 283 that carries `news:title` at all, so ongoing coverage is unaffected and roughly **1.24M URLs of back-catalogue and galleries** stop being listed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A 3-title sample decides what a section is *about*; it does not decide whether there is an article there.** For any section named after a medium, measure the extracted body too — IDXCHANNEL `video` passes a title sample and fails at 200 characters. Sampling titles was the rule; this is the amendment. (2) **"Archive" is a key shape, not a vocabulary word** — express it as a shape, or you end up denying `article`. (3) A rule that needs three per-source exceptions to avoid dropping hard news is a rule aimed at the wrong property; the exceptions here are evidence, not workarounds, and each carries its measurement.
- **Links:** SRC-45 (#155) · SRC-35 (#128, the `video`/`foto` half now corrected) · SRC-39 (#133) · SRC-37 (#130, GRIDID) · OPS-19 (#132, sizing) · `scripts/experiments/probe_branch_scope.py` (now reports an `arch` column) · no production writes

### 2026-09-23 — SRC-46: the last two `re.sub` crawlers, and proving a regex swap changes nothing
- **Question / hypothesis:** IDXCHANNEL and SINDONEWS are the last two crawlers that key an unnameable `<loc>` by its URL. Converting them to `re.match` + skip is the fourth time this fix has been applied — can it be shown to be a no-op on the live index rather than argued from the regex?
- **Method:** dumped both live sitemap indexes; mapped every `<loc>` to the key the **old** `re.sub` produced and to the key the **new** `re.match` produces; compared key sets and the link each key points at. Then removed both from `TestFleetNeverKeysABranchByItsUrl.KNOWN_URL_KEYERS` and re-ran the suite, and separately re-added one to confirm the companion guard fires.
- **Result — both are exact no-ops.**
```
IDXCHANNEL  old 17 new 17 | lost [] | gained []   links identical: True
SINDONEWS   old 12 new 12 | lost [] | gained []   links identical: True
```
- **Result — IDXCHANNEL's "22 locs, 17 branches" is not section loss.** Five of its 22 `<loc>` entries are **literal duplicates** (`/economia/`, `/foto/`, `/news/`, `/technology/`, `/video/` each listed twice in the index). That is worth recording because the SRC-44 lesson — a key count below the `<loc>` count means sections are being dropped by dict collision — has a benign cause too. Check *which* keys collide before concluding anything.
- **Result — SINDONEWS's eight `sitemap-*.xml` entries are site-wide indexes, not desks** (`sitemap-channel`, `-news`, `-web`, `-subkanal`, `-images`, `-video`, `-topic`, `-redaksi`); the 12 real desks are subdomains. The existing "skip anything containing `sitemap-`" filter is therefore correct and was kept. The branch name is now taken from the **original** `<loc>` and the `-news` variant built afterwards, so the name no longer depends on a string rewrite happening first.
- **Result — the fleet guard is now unconditional.** `KNOWN_URL_KEYERS` is empty; `tests/unit` 926 passed, **0 xfailed** (was 924 passed / 2 xfailed). Re-adding IDXCHANNEL to the whitelist fails the companion test with *"IDXCHANNEL no longer keys branches by URL -- remove it from KNOWN_URL_KEYERS"*, so a fixed crawler cannot be left on the list quietly unguarded.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A key-by-key diff turns "this refactor is safe" from a claim into a fact**, and it costs one command. This is the second time today it was the whole evidence for a `_get_branches` rewrite (DETIK was the first). (2) A branch count lower than the `<loc>` count has two causes — silent collision (SRC-44, real loss) and duplicate entries in the publisher's own index (here, no loss). Print the colliding keys before filing. (3) The xfail-plus-companion-test pattern is worth reusing: the xfail keeps a known defect visible without going red, and the companion test makes the whitelist self-cleaning.
- **Links:** SRC-46 (#156) · SRC-43 (#146) · SRC-44 (#154) · SRC-36 (#129) · SRC-41 (#135) · SRC-39 (#133) · no production writes

### 2026-09-25 — Verifying the branch-scope work three ways, and why only the third found the P1
- **Question / hypothesis:** SRC-43/SRC-45/SRC-46/SYS-49 changed what six sources list. Unit tests pass and `probe_branch_scope.py` reports the expected branch sets. Is that enough to sign the work off, or does a real `task=sitemap` see something the offline checks cannot?
- **Method, in increasing cost:** (1) offline unit tests on the predicates and an unnameable-`<loc>` fleet sweep; (2) `scripts/experiments/verify_branch_scope_live.py` — new, read-only: runs the **shipped** `get_news_in_bulk()` per source and inspects the URLs a real run would insert, checking them two independent ways (branch attribution from `_scrape`'s `category` stamp, and URL shape against the shipped predicates); (3) one real `task=sitemap` on MEDIAINDONESIA, chosen as the source whose branch set changed most drastically (283 → 2) for the smallest volume, with before/after DB counts.
- **Result — (1) passed and proved little.** 955 unit tests green; the fleet sweep reported 1,092 listed → 327 soft + 140 archive → 625 crawled, 29 URL-keyed (VIVA only). All true, and all about branch *keys*.
- **Result — (2) found SRC-47 (#157): the drop is leaky by design.** **1,598 of 22,007 listed URLs (7.3%)** come from sections already rejected, every one of them through a **kept** branch: DETIK 1,185 (7.7%, largely `wolipop` via kept sections), MEDIAINDONESIA 183, OKEZONE 101 (`www/news`, `ototekno/*`, `www/headline`), IDXCHANNEL 100 (`idxc-live` lists the same `video-1` player pages whose branch SRC-45 dropped), SINDONEWS 27 (`media` lists `/foto/`), BERITASATU 2. `drop_soft_branches` is correctly placed and simply cannot see inside a kept aggregate feed.
- **Result — (3) found SRC-48 (#158), a P1 the other two could not see.** `_scrape` chose the stored category with `branch_name == link` — a proxy for "this crawler could not name its branch". SRC-43 made that permanently false for MEDIAINDONESIA, so its per-article section was never consulted and **every row became `category='news'`**, a mixed bucket the deny-list cannot reject. Measured on the live DB: `/olahraga/` URLs crawled 04-17→06-02 carry `olahraga` (gate **deny**), those crawled 09-23→25 carry `news` (gate **None**). The bug is in what gets **stored**, not what gets **listed**, so neither the unit tests nor the read-only listing check could reach it. Fixed; 179 of 973 entries now deny where 0 did; 931 rows repaired (all `pending`, owner-approved, backed up).
- **Result — two wrong fixes stopped by checking first.** (a) The obvious SRC-47 fix — apply the vocabulary to listed URLs — would drop 100 hard-news articles per run from IDXCHANNEL's kept `foto` desk, whose articles live at `/foto-1/foto/…`, because `SOFT_BRANCH_EXCEPTIONS` matches whole keys (`"foto-1" != "foto"`). (b) SRC-48's first drafted fix ("prefer the override wherever a crawler implements it") would have fixed nothing — MEDIAINDONESIA's override is itself `re.sub`-returns-its-input and yields a URL — while switching on eight unaudited overrides. An audit of all nine against live URLs settled it: GRIDID→`idea`, MERDEKA→`uang`, PIKIRANRAKYAT→`olahraga` are sections; three do not actually override; two have no rows; MEDIAINDONESIA returns a URL.
- **Result — the verification script's own first draft was wrong**, which is worth recording: matching the vocabulary against whole URLs reported **512 false leaks**, all article slugs (`...-podcast-bola-mnc-university` is an education-desk article; `...-berapa-liter-air-untuk-satu-foto-ai` is tech). Restricting it to section-like segments — never the final path segment, never a purely numeric one, ≤ 20 chars — removed every one.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Offline tests verify predicates, a read-only listing check verifies what would be listed, and only a real write verifies what gets stored.** They are three different questions and the third is where the P1 was. Run one real `task=sitemap` per changed source before OPS-19 schedules anything — it cost 931 rows and found a P1. (2) **`re.sub` for extraction is a defect pattern in this codebase** — three instances in two days, in three different functions, each returning its input unchanged on no match and becoming a URL-shaped key, a URL-shaped category, or a silently collapsed section. Treat every remaining occurrence as suspect. (3) When a fix looks like one line, check the exception path against a real URL before writing it; twice here that turned a "small fix" into a recall loss.
- **Links:** SRC-47 (#157) · SRC-48 (#158) · DBT-38 (#159) · SRC-43 (#146) · SRC-45 (#155) · SRC-46 (#156) · OPS-19 (#132) · `scripts/experiments/verify_branch_scope_live.py` · `scripts/repair_stale_branch_categories.py` · production writes: 11,847 topic rows, +931 sitemaps, 931 category repairs (all owner-approved, all backed up)

### 2026-09-25 — OPS-19 item 4: the first-ever crawl of six sources, and the two things it found
- **Question / hypothesis:** OPS-19 (#132) requires one manual `task=sitemap` per never-crawled source, with the row delta, before any of them enters a schedule. Its stated reason is sizing (DETIK "must drop its 66 soft branches first or the first run lists tens of thousands of sports/games URLs"). Now that SRC-35/43/45 have landed, what do the first runs actually produce — and does anything leak that the offline and read-only checks missed?
- **Method:** (1) `scripts/experiments/verify_branch_scope_live.py` read-only on the four unknown sources to learn their size before writing; (2) `task=sitemap` per source, **smallest first** so a problem surfaces cheaply — ERAID (16) → INVESTORID (100) → EMITENNEWS (2,000) → KONTAN (2,076) → BERITASATU (1,700) → OKEZONE (1,484) → DETIK (15,301); (3) after each batch, a per-source breakdown of what `sitemaps.category` actually received, gated through `category_gate` — the SRC-48 lesson that listing and storing are different questions.
- **Result — sizing, and the read-only pass was accurate to the dedup.** +22,093 rows; `sitemaps` 130,831 → 152,924.

| source | delta | predicted | difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| ERAID | +16 | 16 | — |
| INVESTORID | +100 | 100 | — |
| BERITASATU | +1,700 | 1,700 | — |
| EMITENNEWS | +2,000 | 2,000 | — |
| KONTAN | +2,072 | 2,076 | 4 dupes |
| OKEZONE | +1,279 | 1,484 | 205 already present from June |
| DETIK | **+14,926** | 15,301 | 375 dupes / no usable date |

  **DETIK is 14,926 URLs in one `task=sitemap`**, an order of magnitude above anything else. Against the 1,000-rows-per-source `load_all_sitemaps` queue that is a 15-run `task=full_text` backlog from a single listing.
- **Result — SRC-49 (#160): the word-boundary fix has a blind spot for compound names.** KONTAN listed 100 rows under `sportsetup` (`sportsetup.kontan.co.id`) — "Manchester United vs Manchester City", "Prediksi Borneo FC vs Persija", "Jadwal Timnas Bulu Tangkis di Asian Games 2026". `is_soft_branch('sportsetup')` was False and `category_gate` returned None, because SYS-42 word-bounded the vocabulary so `sport` could not match `transportasi` — which also makes `sport`+`setup` unreachable. A fleet sweep for the shape found 8 candidates: 4 genuine (KONTAN `sportsetup`, DETIK `sportstyle` = Tennis Cup/Chair Pilates/Gokart, DETIK `pop/korean-wave` = Kim Woo Bin/Lee Min Ho, GRIDID `Sportfeat`) and 4 that are the boundary and exception system working correctly (IDXCHANNEL `foto`, KONTAN `english`, WARTAEKONOMI `travel` and `transportasi`). Fixed with an **anchored** `^sport` prefix — measured first: 15 live branch keys match and every one is sports; 0 collisions against the 446-pair not-denied vocabulary fixture; `transportasi` does not start with `sport`, so the SYS-42 false positive is untouched.
- **Result — the category half needed a second commit, and that is the transferable finding.** Adding the exact value `korean-wave` to the category deny-list **did not work**: DETIK stores the *branch key* as the category, so the rows are `pop/korean-wave`, and `is_offtopic_category` matches the whole stored value, not its last segment. Word-bounded `korea` stops at the "n"; `korean` matches at the `-`. **A name added to `SOFT_BRANCH_WORDS` is not thereby denied at category level** — the two vocabularies are separate, and every source that does not override `_get_branch_name_from_url` (SRC-48) stores its branch key, so a compound name needs both.
- **Result — everything else held.** **0 URL-shaped categories across all seven first runs** (SRC-43/SRC-46/SRC-48 holding). A fleet-wide check for stored categories a source's *own* `is_soft_branch` would drop but the gate does not leaves exactly two known pairs: VIVA's URL-shaped category (507 rows, SRC-33 open) and IDXCHANNEL `video` (129 rows, SRC-45's deliberate choice). A first pass of that check used the base `Crawler.is_soft_branch` and wrongly flagged IDXCHANNEL's kept `foto` — per-crawler exceptions have to be honoured in the checker too.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A never-crawled source is an unmeasured source, and its first run is where the vocabulary gaps are.** Two of the last three defects found in this programme (SRC-48, SRC-49) came from real crawls, not from tests or read-only probes. Keep OPS-19 item 4 as a hard prerequisite per source, and read the stored-category breakdown afterwards, not just the row delta. (2) **Size read-only before writing.** The prediction matched the insert to the dedup on all seven, so the read-only pass is a reliable sizing tool and costs no rows — use it before any first run on an untested source. (3) **Crawl smallest-first.** ERAID's 16 rows validated the whole path in seconds; DETIK's 14,926 went last. (4) The branch vocabulary and the category deny-list are **two** lists; a fix to one is not a fix to the other.
- **Links:** OPS-19 (#132) · SRC-49 (#160) · SYS-42 (#137) · SRC-48 (#158) · SRC-43 (#146) · SRC-45 (#155) · SRC-33 (#119) · `scripts/experiments/verify_branch_scope_live.py` · production writes: +22,093 `sitemaps` rows across 7 sources (new rows, no existing row modified)

### 2026-09-25 — QA audit of the 2026-09-25 handoff and the six post-handoff closures: 8 new tickets, 1 reopened, 1 false mutation claim
- **Question / hypothesis:** do the 15 handoff closures and the 6 closed after it (DBT-12, OPS-06/07/10/16/17), plus OPS-19's wrapper, actually run and are they actually guarded?
- **Method:** (1) `tests/unit` + `tests/integration` on a clean DB; (2) 25 mutations: the handoff's own 6 table rows, plus 15 on the post-handoff commits, each re-introducing the bug and running `tests/unit -x` (harness was throwaway: replace one exact string, run, restore from a byte copy); (3) live `check_crawlers.py --website SUARA,KOMPAS,IDXCHANNEL,SINDONEWS,DETIK,BISNIS --report-dir <scratch>`; (4) a fleet branch sweep, calling each crawler's live `_get_branches` → `drop_soft_branches` and grepping kept keys against a broad soft-word list; (5) read-only DB queries on `newsaggregator`; (6) OPS-06's Done-when on `newsaggregator_test` (rename `newsletter_digests`, call `api_health`, rename back); (7) a stub test of `run_one` with a 6 s sleep and `timeout=1`.
- **Result — suites:** unit **1039 passed**; integration **119 passed**. (A first integration run failed 8 tests on leftover fixed-link rows only because the DB container came up mid-run and the session TRUNCATE was skipped. Not a defect.)
- **Result — mutations:** handoff's table, **5 of 6 hold** (the `gate_coef *= 0.85` row fails exactly the 2 named tests). **The SRC-48 row is false**: restoring `branch_name == link` in `_scrape` passes all 1039, because the "end to end" test never calls `_scrape` → DBT-39 (#166). Post-handoff commits: **14 of 15 caught**. The survivor is `error_msg = COALESCE(:note, error_msg)` → `error_msg = error_msg`, which passes unit **and** integration → DBT-40 (#168). `classify_batch`'s backstop alone survives removal, which is fine: it is documented as unreachable while the predict-length guard stands, and removing both is caught.
- **Result — OPS-19 wrapper:** `run_one(Api(sleep 6), timeout=1)` → `('timeout', 'exceeded 1s')` **after 6.0 s**. `with ThreadPoolExecutor(...)` exits through `shutdown(wait=True)`, so the timeout never frees the run → OPS-22 (#161, P1). The existing test passes because it checks the status and not the elapsed time.
- **Result — DETIK scope:** live DETIK listed 242, kept 173, and **25 kept leaves are soft by a 3-title sample**: `f1`, `moto-gp`, `raket`, `basket` (football), `relationship` (all *Ramalan Zodiak*), `hikmah/doa-dan-hadits|khazanah|kisah|muslimah`, `tempat-makan`, `*destination`, `cerita-perjalanan`, `jogja/plesir`, `music`, `pop/music|pensi|trending`, `wedding`, `hijab`, `diet`, `sexual-health`, `sale-and-shop`, `modifikasi` → SRC-50 (#162). No other crawler has a comparable gap.
- **Result — OKEZONE dates:** 2,299 rows, **2 distinct `posted_at`** (the two crawl days). The live `news.okezone.com/nasional/sitemap.xml` has 100 `<url>` with 1 distinct `publication_date` (render time), while the URLs carry `/read/2026/09/22..24/` → SRC-51 (#163).
- **Result — queue sizing:** `category_gate` over every eligible pending row gives **10,967 of 75,321 (15 %) deny**, which are fetched before being rejected (MEDIAINDONESIA 5,794 / 48 %) → SYS-50 (#165).
- **Result — infrastructure:** the prod Postgres container had exited (255) and stayed down: `RestartPolicy=no`, compose file in a deleted `.claude/worktrees/phase1-migration/`, anonymous volume, no `pg_dump` → OPS-23 (#164, P1). Started by hand for this audit.
- **Result — held:** OPS-06 on a real DB (table missing → **503 `degraded`** naming `digests_generated`; restored → 200 `ok`). OPS-17 live (BISNIS `sitemap fetch failed: HTTP 403`; freshness fields populated). OPS-10 (partial run wrote `2026-09-25-bisnis-and-5-more.json`). OPS-16's note persists on the test DB. `crawl_log.started_at` is naive **UTC** and the DB timezone is UTC, so the uncommitted gap check's arithmetic is right. Handoff counts reconfirmed: `sitemaps` 152,928 (+4 from the INVESTORID smoke test), `articles` 11,847 with 124 NULL `topic`, OKEZONE 1,020 June rows unattempted, probe `recall_finance` 0.9405.
- **Result — process:** OPS-16 was closed with a Done-when item unmet (its EXPERIMENTS trap line) and with `OPERATIONS.md:264` still describing the old behaviour → reopened. OPS-24 (#167): the wrapper bypasses DBT-12's validation.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A regression test must call the function that regressed.** "End to end" that skips the broken layer is a helper test; mutate the exact pre-fix line before believing it. (2) **Never use `ThreadPoolExecutor` as a context manager when you want a timeout to free the caller.** (3) **A vocabulary sweep that searches for vocabulary words cannot find soft leaves with no vocabulary word in them.** SRC-49's "fleet sweep for the same shape" had that blind spot. Sweep kept keys against a broad independent list, then sample titles. (4) **A source's `news:publication_date` is not evidence of the publication date** until it has been checked against the URL or page for at least one sitemap per source.
- **Links:** OPS-22 (#161) · SRC-50 (#162) · SRC-51 (#163) · OPS-23 (#164) · SYS-50 (#165) · DBT-39 (#166) · OPS-24 (#167) · DBT-40 (#168) · reopened OPS-16 (#87) · comments on SRC-48 (#158), OPS-17 (#89), DBT-12 (#47), OPS-19 (#132)

### 2026-09-25 — OPS-23: the first pg_dump of production, a restore that proves it, and the container's missing definition
- **Question / hypothesis:** there has never been a dump of `newsaggregator`; does a `pg_dump -Fc` taken from inside the container restore to an identical database, and where did the container's definition go?
- **Method:** `docker exec <c> pg_dump -U postgres -d newsaggregator -Fc -Z 6` → `backups/pg_dump/`; restore into a throwaway `newsaggregator_restore_check` (`createdb` + `pg_restore --no-owner`), `count(*)` of sitemaps/articles/chunks/entities/entity_mentions/crawl_log in both, `dropdb`. Then `scripts/backup_db.py` (streams the dump over `docker exec -i` stdout instead of `docker cp`) run by hand and from Task Scheduler. The deleted definition: `git show 2cd7be0^:docker/docker-compose.yaml` and `docker history --no-trunc` on the image.
- **Result:** 60,126,191 bytes. `pg_restore` rc 0, all six counts equal (152,928 / 11,847 / 99,221 / 54 / 32 / 627). The streamed dump is byte-for-byte the same size as the `docker cp` one, so `docker exec -i` without `-t` is binary-safe on Windows. The Task Scheduler run (`LastTaskResult 0`) wrote and verified a third. The container was built from `docker/postgresql_tables/Dockerfile` = `FROM postgres:12.2-alpine` + an `init.sql` that predates every migration (`posted_at timestamp`, no unique keys). `2cd7be0` deleted both from the repo as "legacy deployment files", so the container was re-created from a worktree copy. `docker update --restart unless-stopped` applied to the live container. **Two traps:** in Git Bash, `docker exec … -f /tmp/x` fails with `could not open output file "C:/Users/…/Temp/x"` (MSYS rewrites the path; `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`). A restore "into `newsaggregator_test`" (the ticket's wording) would overwrite the integration fixtures with production data, so the check uses a scratch DB it drops afterwards.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** a dump is only a backup once `pg_restore --list` shows TABLE DATA for every table that matters. `backup_db.py` enforces that before it prunes anything. The FAISS indexes are not in the dump. The live container is still on its anonymous volume: the move onto `docker/docker-compose.yaml` (named volume `newsaggregator_pgdata`) is an owner-approved write, and its reboot test belongs to that step. Until then, never `docker rm` it and never `docker volume prune`.
- **Links:** OPS-23 (#164) · `docker/docker-compose.yaml` · `scripts/backup_db.py` · `tests/unit/test_backup_db.py` · OPERATIONS.md "Database: container, backup, restore" · Task Scheduler "NewsAggregator DB Backup"

### 2026-09-25 — OPS-22: a per-source timeout that frees the caller AND stops the source
- **Question / hypothesis:** Can the scheduled crawl keep its one-model-load design and still have a per-source timeout that (a) returns on time and (b) guarantees the timed-out source is not writing while the next runs? Plan: one long-lived child process builds the crawler and serves sources over a Pipe. On timeout the parent kills it and joins it before returning, and the next source gets a fresh child.
- **Method:** `newscrawler/tools/source_worker.py` (`SourceWorker`, spawn context). Unit tests against real child processes with stub crawlers (`tests/unit/sched_worker_stubs.py`): a stub that never returns and appends to a file every 50 ms, with `timeout=1`. Mutation harness over six mutants. Then two real runs of `scripts/run_scheduled_crawl.py` as `__main__` with the real `init_crawler()`, pointed at `newsaggregator_test` (`POSTGRES_DB_NAME=newsaggregator_test`, Telegram vars blank), `--phase sitemap` only, because `full_text` would write the real `NEWS_L1/L2` whatever the DB is.
- **Result:** `run()` returns in ~1.1 s for the never-ending stub (QA's repro took 6.0 s for `sleep(6)`). The stub's file stops growing the moment `run()` returns, and the next source runs in a new pid (`spawned == 2`).
