Mutants: abandon-instead-of-kill KILLED, wait-for-the-worker KILLED (as a pytest-timeout, loud by design), no crawl_log close-out KILLED, no run budget KILLED, no `worker.close()` KILLED, gap-check threshold KILLED. Real run 1 (`--timeout 20`): child built in 28 s, KOMPAS 7 s + CNBC 6 s in the same child, +2,000 sitemaps. Real run 2 (`--timeout 2`): DETIK killed at 2 s mid-listing, the child rebuilt (22 s), CNBC killed at 2 s. `crawl_log` rows 21/22 read `failed, 2 s, killed by run_scheduled_crawl: exceeded 2s; worker process killed`. Nothing was left in `started`. Test-DB rows removed afterwards.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** A kill is safe for the stores here only because of three earlier fixes: atomic FAISS swaps (DBT-10), SQL-keyed chunk dedup (SYS-17/25), and a queue that keeps any sitemap with no `articles` row. Do not weaken any of them without revisiting this. The one thing a kill leaves is a `started` crawl_log row, which the parent closes, windowed on the DB's own clock (`SELECT NOW()::timestamp`). Do not use the host clock: the rows are stamped by the DB's `NOW()`. A killed child that is not joined before returning would bring the concurrency back, which is what the file-growth test guards. The run budget (`SCHEDULE_RUN_BUDGET`, 3 h 30 min) exists because Task Scheduler's 4 h kill of the *parent* sends no summary. If the parent is killed anyway, the child's parent-watchdog exits it within 5 s.
- **Links:** OPS-22 (#161) · OPS-19 (#132) · `newscrawler/tools/source_worker.py` · `scripts/run_scheduled_crawl.py` · `tests/unit/test_source_worker.py`, `tests/unit/test_run_scheduled_crawl.py`

### 2026-09-25 — SRC-50: every kept DETIK leaf read, 59 denied per source, and why the deny had to be source-scoped
- **Question / hypothesis:** QA named 25 DETIK soft leaves no vocabulary word reaches. Are there more among the 173 kept, and can their denial be added without colliding with another source?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/sample_branch_titles.py --website DETIK --all-kept` (new mode: live index once → shipped `drop_soft_branches` → 3 titles per kept leaf, DB first, live fallback). 173 leaves: 158 from DB, 15 live (11 of those empty). Rule: deny only when all 3 titles are soft. A mixed leaf stays for the probe, and an empty leaf is not decided by name. Collision checks: (1) stored `sitemaps.category` of every other source, (2) `tests/fixtures/category_vocabulary_not_denied.json`, (3) gold sets (`filter_gold*.jsonl`, handcheck csv), (4) `NEWS_CATEGORIES`, (5) **live** kept branch keys of every crawler (`scripts/experiments/probe_branch_category_mismatch.py`, new).
- **Result:** 58 all-soft leaves: QA's 25 plus `ask-the-expert` (zodiak/jodoh), `hikmah/dakwah|haji-dan-umrah|pesantren`, `pop/awards|culture`, `culture`, 7 regional `*/budaya` (film synopses, song lyrics, Javanese calendar), `jabar/jabar-gaskeun`, `makanan-anak`, `sehat`, `bank-obat-dan-penyakit`, `konsultasi`, `true-story`, `work-and-money`, 4 `properti/*`, `road-warriors`, `editors-choice` (Daihatsu advertorial), `tips-and-tricks`, `insight`, `kamus`, `story` (press releases), `suara-pembaca`, `prokontra`. Kept as mixed: `living`, `hikmah/ziswaf`, `properti/kepemilikan-rumah`, `sumut/budaya`, `bali/budaya`, `kendaraan-niaga`, `perencanaan-keuangan`. Checks 1–4 were clean. **Check 5 was not.** KONTAN lists a `sehat` branch with no stored rows (its sitemap is empty, so it cannot be sampled), and a fleet-wide exact `sehat` would have denied it by name alone. Check 5 also found DETIK `edu/edutainment` kept as a branch but category-denied through the `tainment` stem (100 rows fetched to be rejected; now in the set), plus 8 pre-existing mismatches filed as SRC-52 (#169). After: DETIK 242 listed → 128 soft → 114 crawled (was 173). `reject_denied_categories.py --dry-run --website DETIK`: 14,926 queued rows, **5,187 in a denied category**, all `pending`, 0 saved articles.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) A vocabulary sweep cannot find soft leaves that contain no vocabulary word. Read every kept leaf (`--all-kept`). (2) **A deny decided on one source's titles must be scoped to that source.** `category_gate(category, source)` / `classify_batch(texts, categories, sources)` now take the source, and an AST test fails if a production call site omits it. (3) A collision check against stored rows cannot see a branch that has never been stored. Run the live mismatch probe too. (4) One definition for both lists: `DetikCrawler.SOFT_BRANCH_DENY is SOURCE_SOFT_LEAVES["DETIK"]`, and a test enforces the mirror for every crawler.
- **Links:** SRC-50 (#162) · SRC-52 (#169) · SRC-49 (#160) · `newscrawler/core/importance_filter.py` (`SOURCE_SOFT_LEAVES`) · `scripts/reject_denied_categories.py` · `tests/unit/test_soft_branch_drop.py::TestSourceSoftLeaves`, `::TestEveryCallerPassesTheSource`, `tests/unit/test_reject_denied_categories.py`

### 2026-09-25 — SRC-51: OKEZONE's publication date is its render time on every branch; the URL date is on every article
- **Question / hypothesis:** Is OKEZONE's render-time `news:publication_date` limited to `news/nasional`, as in QA's sample, or is it every branch? Does every article URL carry its own date?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_okezone_dates.py` (new, read-only): every kept OKEZONE branch through the real `_get_branches` + soft drop, distinct publication stamps per file (to the minute), URL dates through the crawler's own `date_from_url`, then the stored `sitemaps.link` shapes. `probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py --website OKEZONE` for the SYS-29 trap. `scripts/repair_posted_at_from_url.py --dry-run`.
- **Result:** 25 kept branches, 1,486 `<url>`. **Every branch with content has exactly 1 distinct publication stamp** (100–120 URLs each). URL dates span `ototekno/techno` 2023-08-03..2026-09-25, `news/nasional` 09-22..09-25, and `www/news` is a stale 2025-08 file. **100.0% of live URLs carry a date** (`/read/`, `/play/` video, `/view/` foto). Nine regional `news/*` sitemaps are empty, and `muslim/tausyiah` failed to fetch. Stored: 2,299 rows, 2 distinct `posted_at`, 0 undatable, 14 already agree. Dedup drift: `would_insert_duplicate=0`, `date_drift=1383` (the wrong dates being corrected). Repair dry run: 2,285 rows to rewrite, **distinct `posted_at` 2 → 251**, 0 with an `articles` row.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** OKEZONE's sitemap dates are worthless: read the URL. `date_from_url` accepts only the three verified path shapes and returns None otherwise (the SYS-32 drop), never the render time. The repair rewrites `sitemaps` only and refuses once an article exists, because `articles.meta_data.posted_at` and the FAISS metadata copy the date at save time. So it must run before OKEZONE's first `task=full_text`.
- **Links:** SRC-51 (#163) · SYS-29 (#84) · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/okezone/okezone_crawler.py` · `scripts/repair_posted_at_from_url.py` · `tests/unit/test_okezone_dates.py`

### 2026-09-25 — SYS-50: category deny before the fetch, and the ratio it must not dilute
- **Question / hypothesis:** Can `category_gate`'s `deny` move in front of `batch_crawling_details` without changing any decision? What does the scheduled queue look like net of denied rows?
- **Method:** `CrawlerServiceImpl._split_denied_by_category` (the same `category_gate(category, source)` the live filter calls first). Denied rows are marked `rejected_filter` and counted in `gate_counts["deny"]`, and only the rest are fetched. The post-fetch gate stays as a backstop. Unit tests in `tests/unit/test_sitemap_terminal_state.py::TestCategoryDenyBeforeFetch`, and 4 mutants. Queue sizing: `scripts/experiments/probe_queue_net_of_denied.py` (new, read-only).
- **Result:** a batch of only denied rows makes **0** `batch_crawling_details` calls and ends `rejected_filter`. Mutants: no pre-deny, **denied rows counted in `attempted`**, source dropped, rows not marked: all KILLED. Queue: 28 scheduled sources, **73,292 eligible, 15,846 denied (22%), 57,446 net**. MEDIAINDONESIA 48%, TVONENEWS 55%, SINDONEWS 53%, KUMPARAN 47%, DETIK 35%, GRIDID 100% (SRC-52). QA's 75,321 / 10,967 (15%) was measured without the cooldown term and before SRC-50's DETIK leaves.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** **`attempted` is the denominator of OPS-16's source-wide ratio and must count only FETCHED rows.** With the denied rows in it, a 100% fetch outage on MEDIAINDONESIA reads as ~52%: the guard never fires, and every `HTTP 403` counts toward SYS-09's cap and retires live URLs (`test_pre_denied_rows_do_not_dilute_the_source_wide_ratio`). Two SYS-34 tests used `hype` as their example of a first save under an unseen category. That is now unreachable (it is denied before the fetch), so they use `ragam`, a mixed bucket.
- **Links:** SYS-50 (#165) · OPS-16 (#87) · SRC-50 (#162) · SRC-52 (#169) · `newscrawler/domain/services/crawler_service_impl.py` · OPERATIONS.md OPS-19 "Queue size, net of category-denied rows"

### 2026-09-25 — OPS-23: production moved onto the checked-in container and a named volume
- **Question / hypothesis:** Can production move from the worktree-defined container (anonymous volume, `restart: no`) to `docker/docker-compose.yaml` with no data change, and does it then survive a Docker restart?
- **Method:** Checked no sessions or pipeline processes; fresh verified dump (`backup_db.py`); `newsaggregator_test` dumped too (it lives in the same container); FAISS dirs copied to `backups/faiss_<stamp>/` (515 MB); baseline counts of 10 tables. `docker update --restart no` + `docker stop` on the old container (not removed), `docker compose up -d`, `pg_restore --exit-on-error` of both DBs, then counts diffed, extensions, sequences, `migrate.py`. Restart test 1: `kill -9` of the postmaster from inside. Test 2: `docker desktop restart`. Then the app's `SQLAlchemyClient`, `backup_db.py` auto-discovery, and `tests/integration`.
- **Result:** both restores rc 0. **All 10 tables identical** (sitemaps 152,928, articles 11,847, chunks 99,221, …), `pg_trgm` present, `sitemaps` sequence = max id, `migrate.py`: nothing to do. **Test 1 proved nothing**: RestartCount 0 and the same StartedAt, because PID 1 ignores a signal sent from inside its own container. **Test 2 passed**: after `docker desktop restart` the container was up again 2 s after the engine returned, with a new StartedAt and no manual start. The old container stayed Exited, and `ihsg_postgres` came back too. App reads 11,847 articles. `backup_db.py` found `newsaggregator-postgres` by port (60.3 MB). Integration 124 passed. Downtime ≈ 1 minute.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** Test a restart policy by restarting the daemon, not by signalling PID 1 from inside. The old container and volume are the rollback until OPERATIONS.md step 6 (remove them after a week of clean runs).
- **Links:** OPS-23 (#164) · OPERATIONS.md "Database: container, backup, restore"

- **Question / hypothesis:** The owner decided to drop the 8 kept branches whose rows the category gate always rejects (KONTAN `english`, WARTAEKONOMI `travel`, GRIDID ×6). What remains of GRIDID afterwards?
- **Method:** `sample_branch_titles.py --website GRIDID --all-kept` before retirement, plus KONTAN `english` / WARTAEKONOMI `travel` samples for the record. `probe_branch_category_mismatch.py` after. `reject_denied_categories.py --website KONTAN,WARTAEKONOMI,GRIDID` (dry run, then write).

Both were dropped on the owner's decision, because the category gate denied them anyway (300 + 24 rows, 0 saved). GRIDID: 43 listed, 20 kept. After the 6 brand branches, the rest are gossip (`GridPop`/`Kids`: "Doktif Tantang Richard Lee") or did not fetch, so GRIDID was **retired** (SRC-37's proposal: removed from `WebsiteName`, `URL`, `CRAWLER_DICT`, `WEB_URL_DICT`, `HEALTHY` and `backfill_missing_articles`; `scrapers/grid_id/` deleted; SYS-31's brand deny entries and the 2,165 rows kept). ### 2026-09-25 — SRC-29: INEWS's dead backlog is 199 articles listed on 112 mirror hosts, not 113 dead hosts
- **Question / hypothesis:** SRC-29 says the 22,406 regional-subdomain rows are dead because their 113 hosts are dead mirrors. Is that true host by host, today, before anything is marked terminal? SRC-34 plans to crawl INEWS `daerah`.
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_inews_hosts.py` (3 newest stored links per host through INEWS's page loader), a random 60 of the 1,976 rows that had recorded 404s, bare-vs-`?page=all` comparison, a SQL count of `/read/<id>/` article ids per host. Then `scripts/retire_inews_mirror_duplicates.py --dry-run` (per id: slug-named host first, stop at the first live copy) and a trafilatura spot check of 6 keepers.
- **Result:** the probe's **first run said 342/342 "alive". That was wrong**: it used `get_url_with_reason`, which returns the response for a 404 too. The corrected probe (`get_soup_with_reason`): 333/342 HTTP 404. 109 hosts dead in the sample, `bogor`/`celebes`/`jatenginfo` mixed, `ramadan` (section pages) alive. The previously-404 rows are still 404 (60/60), and `?page=all` makes no difference. **The unit is the article, not the host:** 22,288 `/read/` rows = **199 article ids × 112 hosts**, and each id is served only by its home host (716996 is live on `bogor`, 404 on the other 111). Dry run: 183 live copies found, 1 already saved, 15 with no live copy anywhere, **0 unresolved** (no timeout or 403 anywhere), **22,104 rows to retire, 184 kept**, 10,369 fetches spent once (median 57 per id; the slug named the home host for 39 of 183). Keepers spot-checked: 6/6 real articles, bodies 1,338–8,746 chars. Side notes: INEWS's own selector extracts 0 chars on live pages (already SRC-19 #99), and the health check's 09-24 `BROKEN` for INEWS was a one-off (the index fetches, 177 entries).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** do not retire INEWS regional rows by host. Dedup by article id and keep the live copy. A 404 is evidence and a timeout is not. Probe liveness with `get_soup_with_reason`. SRC-34's `daerah` widening will re-create this duplication unless it dedups by article id at listing time.
- **Applied 2026-09-26 00:14 (owner-approved):** 22,104 rows retired in one transaction (backup `backups/src29_inews_mirror_retire_20260925T171417Z.json`). Real `task=full_text` INEWS right after: 1,000 queued, 254 denied by category before fetching (SYS-50), **746 fetched, 648 extracted, 98 HTTP 404 (13%, was 99.5%), 450 articles saved** (INEWS had saved 2 in its history). The 98 are the one-per-host section pages outside `/read/`, left to SYS-09's counter.
- **Links:** SRC-29 (#111) · SRC-34 (#124) · SRC-19 (#99) · `scripts/retire_inews_mirror_duplicates.py` · `tests/unit/test_retire_inews_mirror_duplicates.py` · `backups/src29_inews_mirror_plan.json`

### 2026-09-26 — SRC-38: KOMPAS's three sitemap indexes; its finance desk listed for the first time
- **Question / hypothesis:** KOMPAS's `URL` points at `news.kompas.com/news/sitemap.xml` (10 news/regional hosts). Do `money.kompas.com/news/sitemap.xml` and `www.kompas.com/sitemap.xml` add the finance desk and the www sections as Google-news leaves?
- **Method:** fetched all three indexes. New base hook `Crawler.EXTRA_INDEX_URLS` + `list_branches()` (primary index + extras, the primary winning a key collision; a failing extra is skipped), used by `get_news_in_bulk`, `check_crawlers.py` and the branch probes. `sample_branch_titles.py --website KOMPAS --all-kept` (32 leaves). Then one real `task=sitemap` (`process_event`).
- **Result:** indexes: news 10, money 3 (`sitemap-news-money.xml` = 235 `<url>` with `news:title` + real publication time; `sitemap-basic`/`sitemap-archive-*` skipped), www 51 (25 `sitemap-news-*` sections + archives). Listed 35. The vocabulary drops `hype`/`food`/`sport`. **11 more denied per source** after sampling: `badminton`, `motogp`, `homey`, `skola` (exam question banks), `sains` (science curiosities, not developments), and 6 provincial SEO/service desks (`jawa-barat`/`kalimantan-barat` "Tanggal X Memperingati Hari Apa?", `jawa-tengah` KRL timetables, `jawa-timur`/`kalimantan-timur`/`banten` weather forecasts). Kept as mixed: `tren`, `cekfakta`, `edu`, `lampung`, `riau`, `sumatera-utara`, `sumatera-barat`, `sulawesi-selatan`. `probe_branch_scope`: 35 listed, 14 soft, 21 crawled, fin=1 (`money`; the probe's regex lacked the word), 0 URL keys. Mismatch probe: 0. Real `task=sitemap`: **+1,470 rows**, `money` 235, `properti` 48, `global` 32, `tren` 89, `edu` 29, `cekfakta` 22, 0 soft categories stored.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** a source can have more than one sitemap index. Every tool must ask `crawler.list_branches()`, not `_get_branches(get_soup(website_url))`, or it silently sees only the primary one.
- **Links:** SRC-38 (#131) · SRC-50 (#162, the same source-scoped leaves) · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/kompas/kompas_crawler.py` · `tests/unit/test_kompas_crawler.py`

### 2026-09-26 — DBT-10: FAISS saved once per source instead of once per batch, kill-safe
- **Question / hypothesis:** The crawl rewrote both whole indexes after every batch: L1 ~68 MB + L2 ~204 MB, plus the same again for the `.bak` copy, ~10 batches per source. Can the save move to once per source without giving up crash consistency now that OPS-22 kills timed-out sources on purpose?
- **Method:** `ChunkFAISSDatasource.begin_deferred/end_deferred/deferred()` (in-memory adds, one atomic save per touched index at the outermost exit, also after an exception). A key cache replaces the per-batch full-docstore scan and is rebuilt whenever the docstore size changes. `DataFlowRepositoryImpl.deferred_embeddings(website)`, used by the service around each source's article crawl: chunk rows are written with `embedding_id` NULL until the flush succeeds, then assigned. On entry the block re-embeds that source's NULL rows, because their sitemaps have articles and never re-enter the queue. Scripts are unchanged (save per call). `tests/unit/test_deferred_embeddings.py`: real FAISS (FakeEmbeddings) in tmp_path with the index-path constants patched, and a fake chunks table on the DBT-09 key. Two real runs.
- **Result:** unit: 10 tests, including kill-inside-block (nothing on disk, NULL rows, next block repairs), kill-between-flush-and-assign (healed, no duplicate vectors), failed flush (no ids assigned) and exception in the body (flushed and assigned). 4 mutants killed: ids assigned before the flush, ids written immediately, no re-embed on entry, no cache invalidation. Integration 124 passed (one test double lacked the new repository method and was given the base contract). **Real TEMPO `full_text`: 10 batches, 20 embeds, 2 index saves** (was 20); 69 saved; 0 NULL `embedding_id` rows. Real CNN: 10 batches, 85 saved, both index files written once (same mtime), 0 NULL rows. Production had 0 unembedded rows beforehand, so the first run has no backlog to repair.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** do not defer the vector-store save without deferring the SQL claim that the vector exists. Written together, a kill turns into an invisible, permanent gap (SQL says embedded, disk does not have it). The repair has to run where the rows live: articled sitemaps never come back through the queue.
- **Links:** DBT-10 (#45) · OPS-22 (#161) · DBT-23 (#74) · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/faiss/chunk_faiss_data_source.py` · `newscrawler/infrastructure/repositories/dataflow/data_flow_repository_impl.py`

### 2026-09-26 — OPS-04: the repair mode, and the half-repair it did first
- **Question / hypothesis:** The last divergence was 9,447 VIVA sitemaps with vectors but no article. Which can still become articles, and can a repair remove the rest safely?
- **Method:** classified by current status: 6,529 queued, 2,918 `rejected_filter` (mostly VIVA soft categories denied by SRC-33). `scripts/repair_store_consistency.py`: pure `plan()` + `--apply` (FAISS backup, atomic save, refuses while a crawl is `started`). Checked with `check_store_consistency.py` before and after.
- **Result:** **first `--apply` was wrong.** It deleted the 2,918 terminal sitemaps' vectors (L1 2,918 + L2 12,998) but not their `chunks` rows. Those sitemaps had chunk rows too (June: SYS-08 failed their articles after the chunks were written), so `chunks_not_in_faiss` went **0 → 2,918**. The checker caught it immediately (`GROWING`). Fixed forward, not rolled back, because the chunk rows were the same debris and also surface in RAG's full-text search: the plan now removes a terminal sitemap's vectors and chunk rows together (a test pins the invariant "no deleted vector stays referenced by a surviving chunk row"). The second `--apply` deleted the 15,916 chunk rows (backed up as JSON). Result: 7/9 checks 0, `chunks_not_in_faiss` 0; the only divergence left is the 6,529 queued VIVA sitemaps, which heal on crawl. A third run: nothing to do. Baselines lowered to 6,529.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** "no article" is not enough to call a vector an orphan. Check what else references the key before deleting it: a repair that removes one store's half of a pair creates the divergence it exists to fix. Run the checker straight after any `--apply`.
- **Links:** OPS-04 (#52) · SRC-33 (#119) · SYS-08 (#26) · backups `faiss_20260925T182149Z`, `faiss_20260925T182305Z`

### 2026-09-26 — SRC-03: BISNIS re-tested; every page, articles included, is behind a Cloudflare managed challenge a real headless browser cannot pass
- **Question / hypothesis:** Is BISNIS still blocked, is it only the sitemaps (so an alternative listing could feed the crawl), and does a real browser get through?
- **Method:** `requests` with a current Chrome UA + `Accept-Language: id-ID` on `/sitemap-news.xml`, `/sitemap.xml`, `finansial.bisnis.com/`, `/rss`, and two stored article URLs (`market.bisnis.com/read/20260602/...`). Then the real Chrome binary: `chrome --headless=new --virtual-time-budget=25000 --dump-dom <sitemap>`, which runs the challenge's JavaScript. No new dependency installed.
- **Result:** every URL `403`, `cf-mitigated: challenge`, `server: cloudflare`, a ~5.7 KB challenge page, **article pages included**. Headless Chrome after 25 s of virtual time: `<title>Just a moment...</title>` (28 KB), no `<urlset>`.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** not a header, UA, path or "needs JavaScript" problem. The publisher blocks automated access to the whole site, so an alternative listing (Google News, RSS elsewhere) would only yield links whose pages are equally blocked. The remaining routes are bot-detection evasion (stealth drivers, CAPTCHA-solving services), deliberately not pursued. Options are the owner's: retire BISNIS like KAPANLAGI/BATAMPOS, or seek permitted access (a feed or licence from Bisnis.com). **Owner decision 2026-09-26: not retired.** BISNIS is too important for the niche; SRC-03 stays open to revisit, and BISNIS stays off `HEALTHY` meanwhile.
- **Links:** SRC-03 (#14)

### 2026-09-26 — QA audit of HANDOFF_2026-09-26, watching the first unattended scheduled crawl live
- **Question / hypothesis:** Do the 16 closures in `HANDOFF_2026-09-26.md` hold, and can the scheduled crawl run unattended as shipped (risks 1-3: DBT-10's crash contract, OPS-04's end state, the run budget)?
- **Method:** suites (`tests/unit`, `tests/integration`). 18 mutants (5 from the handoff's table, 13 new) run in a `git -c core.autocrlf=false archive` export, never the live tree, because the 07:00 run was about to spawn workers from it. Id-level FAISS vs `chunks` check (pickle docstores, every `embedding_id` against its level's docstore and metadata). `repair_store_consistency.py` dry run. OKEZONE date check against URL dates. Live re-sampling of KOMPAS/DETIK denied leaves. Then the 07:00 scheduled run observed from Task Scheduler + `crawl_log` + NULL-embedding counts every ~10 min, the saved titles of 7 sources read, a fleet scan of every stored body for paragraph cascades, and the Windows Application log for the crash. The lxml crash repro is inlined: 160 cached PIKIRANRAKYAT pages, `BeautifulSoup(raw,'html.parser')` → `Crawler._trafilatura_fallback` on a `ThreadPoolExecutor(min(32,cpu+4))`, each trial in its own process, threaded vs behind a `threading.Lock`.
- **Result:**
  - Unit 1259 passed / 5 skipped, integration 124 passed (as claimed).
  - Mutants: 16/18 KILLED. **Survived:** removing `repair_store_consistency`'s crawl-running guard (OPS-26 #175), and removing `gadget` from VIVA's soft leaves (harmless: all 507 rows already `rejected_filter`, and the branch is dropped by vocabulary).
  - Stores: exact at id level (L1 18,980 / L2 65,578, 0 NULL, 0 unreferenced, 0 metadata mismatch). **But 1,237 of the "6,529 queued" VIVA sitemaps are terminal**: SRC-33's pre-2024 retirement (06:30) came after OPS-04's apply (01:23), leaving 9,409 orphan vectors + chunk rows, hidden by the checker's KNOWN baseline (OPS-25 #174).
  - Handoff claim "ran unattended at 07:00 today" was false when written (06:35): the task had never run.
  - **Scheduled run:** 07:00:01-10:17, sitemap phase 7 min for 28/28, full_text 27 rows, **12,428 saved**, 0 timeout kills (MEDIAINDONESIA 880/900 s). **PIKIRANRAKYAT worker crashed in lxml** (`etree.cp39-win_amd64.pyd`, `0xc0000005`, offset `0x1fc7a9`): 269 articles saved without vectors, 666 NULL rows, failure counts lost, `crawl_log` says "killed by" (SYS-52 #178). **WARTAEKONOMI skipped** by the budget (last alphabetically, +6,235 new rows, queue 8,833; OPS-28 #179). The run wrote no log file (OPS-27 #176). The store checker flags the crash `GROWING` but is not scheduled (OPS-29 #180).
  - **EMITENNEWS: 935/935 saved bodies cascaded** (each paragraph repeats the rest, 4.7x). Nested unclosed `<p>`, 9 of 10 `<p>` contain another. Every other source: 0. SRC-54 (#177).
  - INEWS still holds 657 URL-keyed rows (inews-tv sections), 16 promo/segment pages saved this run (SRC-53 #173). Gold-set sha depends on checkout line endings (DBT-41 #171). OPERATIONS.md has a raw CR in the crawl command (DBT-42 #172).
  - lxml repro: threaded 2,880 + full-chain 1,920 extractions, **0 crashes**. Locked 33 s vs threaded 32 s per 480 extractions, so the lock costs nothing. The WER minidump was not retained (only `Report.wer`).
  - Holds: OKEZONE 2,299/2,299 `posted_at` = URL date, 762 saved articles likewise. KOMPAS denied provincial leaves re-sampled live (weather, KRL timetables, "hari apa"). DETIK denies mostly hold (`culture`, `edu/edutainment` closer to mixed, 5 rows each). BERITASATU/DETIK/IDNTIMES/IDXCHANNEL saves are hard news.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** DBT-10's contract held under a real crash, but the nearest thing to a detector (the store check) runs only by hand. The run budget does not fit a full fleet at 1,000 rows per source. Read a new source's saved bodies, not its save count. The lxml trigger is rarer than 1 in ~4,800 threaded extractions offline, so do not burn more time guessing it; `faulthandler` in the worker is the next step.
- **Follow-up (owner-approved, 11:35):** `repair_store_consistency.py --apply` removed the 1,237 terminal VIVA sitemaps' 9,409 vectors (L1 1,237 / L2 8,172) and 9,409 chunk rows together. Backup `backups/faiss_20260926T043511Z/` (+ `orphan_chunk_rows.json`). After: no-articles 6,529 → 5,292 (all queued), second dry run 0 to delete, id-level check exact (L1 30,171 / L2 102,858).

### 2026-09-26 — SRC-54 / OPS-30: EMITENNEWS's cascade fixed on four live pages, and why the health check never saw it
- **Question / hypothesis:** (1) Does taking each `<p>`'s own text (skipping nested `<p>` subtrees) give exactly the article, no more and no less? (2) Why did `check_crawlers.py` report EMITENNEWS `PARTIAL 0/3` while production saved 935?
- **Method:** 4 random saved EMITENNEWS URLs fetched through the shipped loader; old vs new `_get_whole_text` compared against every string in the page's `#post-content` container and against the site's own `data-total-words`. Then `check_crawlers.py --website EMITENNEWS --sample-size 3` with the fix, and again with the old extractor stashed. Sitemap head probed with `requests` (`allow_redirects=True`) and the loader's `get_soup_with_reason`.
- **Result:**
  - Nesting: 10 `<p>` / 9 nested, 10/9, 3/2, 8/7. Children of each `<p>`: a `<strong>` dateline (`EmitenNews.com -`), empty `div.home-ads`, a bold sub-heading, then the next `<p>`. Own text alone (`find_all(string=True, recursive=False)`, QA's first suggestion) **drops the dateline and the sub-headings**; the shipped helper `own_paragraph_text` keeps inline markup and skips only nested-`<p>` strings.
  - New extractor: 317/362/130/333 words = **exactly every word in `#post-content`** (no text sits outside `<p>`); the site's `data-total-words` says 309/445/131/331 (its own counting). Chars 2,396/2,706/971/2,462 vs old 12,711/14,580/1,816/10,241. Cascaded pairs new 0/0/0/0, old 9/9/2/7.
  - A side effect of the old code: an outer paragraph containing a nested "Baca juga" line was dropped whole, so some stored bodies lost text as well as repeating it. The first offline fixture hid the cascade this way (only 1 of 3 tests failed on the old extractor) until a variant without the "Baca juga" line was added.
  - Health check: the head of `sitemap-current-1.xml` is **scheduled posts dated today and tomorrow** (`lastmod 2026-09-27`), each `soft-404: redirected to /404` until it publishes; entries a day old resolve. With settled-first sampling (OPS-30): `EMITENNEWS OK 3/3 (2250ch)`. With the old extractor: `REPEATED 3/3 (11953ch) 3/3 sample bodies repeat their own text`.
  - `scripts/requeue_cascaded_articles.py --website EMITENNEWS` (dry run): 935 articles, 935 cascaded, 8,042 chunk rows = NEWS_L1 935 + NEWS_L2 7,107 vectors, 0 entity mentions.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** A health check that samples the first N listed entries samples whatever the site puts first, here unpublished posts. Sample entries at least a day old. Count repeated text, not just extracted characters: 11,953 chars looked healthier than 2,250. When a body extractor is suspected, compare its word count to every string in the article container, not to the site's own counter.
- **Links:** SRC-54 (#177) · OPS-30 (#181)

### 2026-09-26 — SYS-52 / OPS-25..29: what a native worker crash now leaves behind, and the lock window the old guard missed
- **Question / hypothesis:** (1) Does `faulthandler` in the worker give a Python stack for a native crash, which is what SYS-52 lacked? (2) Is the scheduled run's FAISS exposure really wider than the per-source `crawl_log` 'started' row (OPS-26)? (3) Do the new run log, starvation order and store check work in a real scheduled run, not just under stubs?
- **Method:**
  - Native crash: stub source `SEGV` calls `faulthandler._read_null()` (a NULL read in C) inside a real `SourceWorker` child with `log_path` set (`tests/unit/test_source_worker.py::TestLogFileAndCrashes`). **`ctypes.string_at(0)` does not work on Windows:** ctypes turns the access violation into `OSError: exception: access violation reading 0x0…`, so the process survives and nothing is tested.
  - Lock window: a real run, `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN= venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts\run_scheduled_crawl.py --website ERAID --log-dir <scratch>` (empty token = no Telegram), and during its model load `repair_store_consistency.py --apply` plus `SELECT … FROM crawl_log WHERE status='started'`.
  - Mutations, each reverted after: `running = _crawl_running(session)` → `running = 0` (QA's surviving mutant); the repair's lock guard removed; the `_atomic_save_local` chokepoint check removed; `dedupe_chunks.py`'s guard removed; `faulthandler.enable(file=…)` removed; `with LXML_PARSE_LOCK:` → `if True:`; the old EMITENNEWS extractor.
- **Result:**
  - Crash: `('failed', 'worker process crashed (exit code 3221225477 = 0xC0000005 access violation)')`, and the run log holds `Windows fatal exception: access violation` with every thread's stack, current thread first: `sched_worker_stubs.py, line 38 in crawl_website` ← `source_worker.py … in _worker_loop`. The next source runs in a fresh child.
  - Lock window: lock file present 3 s after start (`{"pid": 22968, …}`); `started` rows in the last 10 min: **none** (models still loading); repair `--apply` → **exit 2** `refusing to write: a scheduled crawl (pid 22968 …) holds …\.scheduled_crawl.lock`. The old guard would have let it through.
  - E2E run (ERAID, both phases, 27 s, models warm): one log file with the wrapper's lines and the worker's `[source-worker]` lines (`Full-text crawl finished for ERAID: pre_denied=0 attempted=0 …`), `full_text order … / projected 16s`, `store check: GROWING chunks_pending_embedding, chunks_not_in_faiss -> crawler_health\store-consistency-2026-09-26.json`, SUMMARY `… · vectors pending (re-embedded on the source's next full_text): PIKIRANRAKYAT 269 articles/666 chunk rows · log …`; lock released; exit 0.
  - Checker split, live after the OPS-25 and SRC-54 repairs: `terminal` L1/L2 **0/0**, `queued` 5,292/5,292 (info), every other check 0 except PIKIRANRAKYAT's 666/269. Every baseline is now 0.
  - Mutations: all 7 killed (1–3 tests fail each).
  - **Trap found while mutation-testing:** with `running = 0` the repair test ran on into the real code path, and `shutil.copytree` copied the **real** NEWS_L1/L2 into `backups/faiss_20260926T080715Z` (686 MB) before failing on the stub store's save. Live files were untouched (mtime 14:56, totals unchanged). The stray copy was deleted and the test now points `VECTOR_DB_NAME_L1/L2` and `BACKUP_DIR` at `tmp_path`. A test that guards a writer must isolate the writer's paths, because a mutant *will* run the write.
  - SYS-52 item 5 (flush FAISS every N batches) not done: a crashed source's NULL rows are re-embedded on its next `full_text` (DBT-10's re-embed on entry), OPS-29 now reports them per source, and a mid-source checkpoint doubles the per-source index writes DBT-10 cut.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** Use `faulthandler._read_null()` to test native-crash handling on Windows, not ctypes. A per-source signal is not a run-level lock. In a stub-driven test of a script that writes real paths, monkeypatch the paths first.

### 2026-09-26 — SRC-53: iNews TV's four URL-keyed sections, named and sampled
- **Question / hypothesis:** What are INEWS's 657 URL-keyed rows, which of their sections are soft, and can they be renamed with crawler code rather than a one-off mapping?
- **Method:** stored titles per section (`row_number() … ORDER BY random()`, 6 pending + every saved) with `category_gate(<url>, "INEWS")` and `category_gate(<name>, "INEWS")`; `scripts/fix_url_shaped_categories.py --dry-run --website INEWS` after adding `INewsCrawler.branch_name_from_url`.
- **Result:**
  - `inews-tv` (200: 188 saved, 12 rejected): hard news ("Prabowo Pastikan Kecelakaan KM Virgo Transport 8 Diusut", "NTT Diguncang 3.752 Gempa Susulan"). Rename only.
  - `inews-tv/talkshow` (200: 193 pending, 5 saved): programme promos ("Jangan Lewatkan Rakyat Bersuara Malam Ini: … Live di iNews", "Saksikan Malam Ini di Interupsi: …") and debate-show clips ("Panas! Firdaus Oiwobo Ribut hingga Nyaris Baku Hantam …"). The 5 saved went through the probe as politics/law. **Soft: denied for INEWS** (`SOURCE_SOFT_LEAVES["INEWS"]`).
  - `inews-tv/eksklusif` (140: 119 pending, 11 saved): mixed. Promos and "Food Diary" alongside the Pati PBB protest, a malpractice victim's account, and the Mbah Priok eviction (saved bodies 1.2–2.1k chars). **Left to the probe**, which also passed one promo ("Jangan Lewatkan Interupsi Malam Ini …"): promo titles are a gap the probe does not close.
  - `inews-tv/religi` (117): already `deny` by the substring rule, URL or name.
  - Rename (owner-approved, 15:28): 657 rows in one transaction, backup `backups/src36_url_shaped_categories_20260926T082823Z.json`; `SELECT count(*) FROM sitemaps WHERE category LIKE 'http%'` = **0** fleet-wide.
  - A side effect a test caught: once INEWS names its branches, `test_no_url_category_on_a_source_whose_crawler_names_its_branches` sees the gold set's 8 INEWS URL rows. Refreshing them changes the gold sha, so they join VIVA's 289 in SYS-51 (#170).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** Name stored rows with the crawler's own `branch_name_from_url`, even for sections the crawler no longer lists, so SRC-34's widening inherits the same keys. TV-programme sections carry promos under news-shaped titles; sample them before trusting the probe. A rename that touches a source in the gold set is a SYS-51 item, not a quick fix.

### 2026-09-26 — QA audit of HANDOFF_2026-09-26_evening: the lock guards "now", not "since I loaded"; four surviving mutants
- **Question / hypothesis:** Do the evening handoff's closures (OPS-25/26/27/29, SRC-53, DBT-41/42, OPS-30) hold, and do the three open ones (SYS-52, OPS-28, SRC-54) have the evidence claimed for run 1? Specifically: (1) does OPS-26's lock really stop every other FAISS writer (handoff risk 1); (2) how likely is risk 2's reused pid; (3) is risk 5's "a cascaded body gives the same probe input as a clean one" true; (4) the nine mutations the handoff asked for, plus mine.
- **Method:**
  - Suites: `venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest tests/unit -q` and `tests/integration -q`.
  - DB, read-only: `crawl_log` for the 19:00 run, `chunks.embedding_id IS NULL`, the EMITENNEWS cascade scan (independent copy of `cascaded_pairs`, fleet-wide), VIVA queued-with-vectors rows. Log scan of `logs/scheduled_crawl_2026-09-26_1900.log`.
  - Fresh clone: `git -c core.autocrlf=true clone`, then sha256 of `tests/fixtures/*.jsonl` against `importance_probe.json`.
  - Mutation: `scripts/experiments/qa_mutation_runner.py` (23 mutants, tests/unit `-x` each; FAISS/DB/Telegram pointed away; tree restored by `git checkout`, FAISS mtimes and `backups/` count checked before and after).
  - OPS-26 repro: two **sibling** processes (neither a child of the other) over a tmp FAISS (FakeEmbeddings) with `run_lock.LOCK_PATH` in tmp, using HEAD's code from a `git archive` snapshot (the mutants owned the tree):
    ```python
    # sched.py
    with run_lock.RunLock():
        w = load(); signal("locked"); wait("manual_loaded")
        w.add_texts(["run"]); ChunkFAISSDatasource._atomic_save_local(w, IDX)
        signal("sched_flushed"); wait("manual_tried")
    signal("released")

    # manual.py
    wait("locked"); m = load(); signal("manual_loaded"); wait("sched_flushed")
    m.add_texts(["manual"])
    try:
        ChunkFAISSDatasource._atomic_save_local(m, IDX)
    except RunLockHeld:
        pass
    signal("manual_tried"); wait("released")
    ChunkFAISSDatasource._atomic_save_local(m, IDX)
    ```
  - Risk 5: `build_check_text` + `truncate_for_probe` (title + body[:500], 200 words, whitespace-normalised) on the 935 backed-up cascaded bodies (`backups/src54_20260926T075610Z/rows.json`) against the same articles' clean re-crawled bodies.
  - Live: `check_crawlers.py --website EMITENNEWS,TIRTO,ERAID --sample-size 3 --report-dir <scratch>`.
- **Result:**
  - Suites: unit **1,335 passed, 5 skipped**; integration **124 passed**. Both match the handoff.
  - Fresh `autocrlf=true` clone: every gold fixture is LF, and `filter_gold.jsonl` sha is `01c038e6…` = the probe's recorded sha. **DBT-41 holds.**
  - **19:00 run:** 28/28 sitemap and 28/28 full_text `completed`, 9,646 saved, `chunks.embedding_id IS NULL` = **0**, no faulthandler output, no worker respawn, no interleaved or NUL lines in the 2.8 MB log (the handoff's "1.3 MB" was read mid-run), store check all 10 zero. PIKIRANRAKYAT 319 saved; MEDIAINDONESIA **947 s** under its 1200 s timeout. Every HEALTHY source has a completed full_text within 24 h.
  - EMITENNEWS: 996 articles, **0 cascaded**, median 1,904 chars. **Fleet-wide 0 of 33,859 cascaded**, 1 article with a single pair. 1,011 re-queued rows are still pending, never attempted. Live `OK 3/3 (2250ch)`, so **OPS-30 holds**. (The 05:30 `crawler_health/2026-09-26.json` still says PARTIAL; it predates the fix.)
  - **Risk 1, reproduced as a real hole:** the manual flush during the lock was refused as designed. After release it was allowed, and the final disk was `['seed', 'vector saved by a manual crawl']`: **the run's vector is gone.** Filed **SYS-53 (#182, P1)**. Separately, three scripts write FAISS indirectly with no start-time guard (`backfill_missing_articles`, `backfill_orphan_chunks`, `backfill_pending_embeddings`), and the guard test only greps `_atomic_save_local`: **DBT-43 (#183)**.
  - **Risk 2:** 465 live pids, 270 below 20,000, so a dead run's pid is live again **~5.4%** of the time. A lock whose pid is now `explorer.exe` gives `RunLockHeld ... Run it after the crawl ends.` Every later run fails until the file is deleted by hand, and the message says to wait. Filed **OPS-31 (#184)**.
  - **Risk 5 holds:** the probe input is identical or ≥ 90% word-identical for **934 of 935** (median 99%; usually only the 500-char cut's last word differs). Not re-queuing the 60 `rejected_filter` EMITENNEWS rows is sound.
  - **New:** 5,292 VIVA sitemaps (28,928 chunk rows) have vectors but no article and are `pending` (the June article-gap era). VIVA's probe keeps 65% (628 of 966), and the crawl settles rejections without removing vectors, so about 1,850 of them will become terminal orphans. The store check will then say `STORE DIVERGENT` from normal crawling. Filed **SYS-54 (#185)**.
  - **Mutants, 19 KILLED:** starvation_order by name · timeout_for with no max · timeout_for ignoring its table · store_note with no exemption · store_note exempting all · sample_order identity · abandon_reason always "killed by" · CASCADE_MIN_SUFFIX_CHARS 0 and 200 · is_cascaded ≥ 3 · SOURCE_SOFT_LEAVES["INEWS"] emptied · RunLock.release unconditional · own_paragraph_text = get_text · newspaper parse unlocked · INEWS branch_name returns the URL · worker pid not allowed · stale lock treated as live · log prune keeps all · NULL-embedding note ignored.
  - **Mutants, 4 SURVIVED:**
    - `timeout = args.timeout` at the run-loop call site: OPS-28's whole per-source timeout. **DBT-44 (#186)**.
    - the `sitemaps_url_category` SQL → `WHERE false`, and its value dropped before `compute_divergence`: **DBT-45 (#187)**.
    - `is_cascaded` `>= 1`: **DBT-46 (#188)**.
  - Not filed, by the settled rule at the top of this file: OKEZONE (703 of 703 "Site extractor raised") and KONTAN (all 630 articles `trafilatura`) are dead-selector FALLBACK, which is DBT-05's call. Both show FALLBACK in the daily health JSON already. KONTAN's `kiaton`/`caritahu`/`belanjaon` leaves were 430 of its 707 rejections on the run; the rejections are correct, but they are fetch waste.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A run lock answers "is a writer running now?", but a whole-index save needs "has the disk changed since I loaded it?"** A process that loads before or during the run and saves after it overwrites the run. Guard whole-file saves with a generation check, not only a lock (SYS-53). (2) A pid-file lock needs the process start time, not just `pid_exists`, on Windows. (3) A test named "…and the budget uses it" that calls only the pure helper does not guard the call site; mutate the call site. (4) A store check whose I/O half has no test can be `WHERE false` forever. (5) The mutation runner restores with `git checkout`, so run it on a clean tree, and run other repo-importing work from a `git archive` snapshot while it runs.

### 2026-09-27 — SYS-53 / OPS-31 / DBT-43..46 / SYS-54: the QA list fixed; 40 of 40 mutants killed, and the two-process repro keeps the run's vector
- **Question / hypothesis:** Does a generation check at the FAISS save chokepoint close SYS-53 (a stale writer overwriting the scheduled run after the lock is released) in real separate processes? Do the fixes for the whole 2026-09-26 QA list actually guard what they claim, i.e. would QA's surviving mutants now die, and would a mutant of each new guard die?
- **Method:**
  - `scripts/experiments/repro_stale_index_overwrite.py` (new): QA's sibling-process repro turned into a script. `sched` holds a real `RunLock` over a tmp FakeEmbeddings index, and `manual` (not its child) loads, tries to save under the lock, and again after release. Run from a `git archive c45486c` snapshot while the mutation runner owned the tree. The same script is the unit test `TestTwoSeparateProcesses`, also run against HEAD's pre-fix code by stashing the fix.
  - `scripts/experiments/qa_mutation_runner.py` on a clean tree after the three fix commits: QA's 23 mutants (with `stale_lock_is_live` re-pointed at OPS-31's owner check) plus 17 new ones, one per new guard. FAISS mtimes and `backups/` were counted before and after.
  - Suites: `tests/unit`, `tests/integration`.
- **Result:**
  - Repro, **before** (fix stashed): events `manual: save under the lock refused (RunLockHeld)` → `manual: save after release ALLOWED`, `final_disk ['manual', 'seed']`. The run's vector was gone. **After:** `manual: save after release refused (StaleIndexError)`, `final_disk ['run', 'seed']`, child rcs `[0, 0]`.
  - Mutation run 23:21–00:0x: **40 KILLED, 0 SURVIVED**, including QA's three survivors: `over_budget_uses_default_timeout` (DBT-44), `url_category_sql_never` and `url_category_not_passed` (DBT-45), `is_cascaded_ge1` (DBT-46). New guards killed: generation check removed, save lock removed, own save not re-tracked, stale compare disabled, no reload after refusal, refused block's ids kept, run-lock call removed from the chokepoint (named `sys53_run_lock_outside_save_lock`, but it deletes the call rather than moving it), `os.link`→`os.replace`, no create_time in the lock, pre-OPS-31 lock never stale, `backfill_pending_embeddings` unguarded, `process_event` unguarded, SYS-54's service call removed, failed fetches not candidates, rows deleted before the flush, rows deleted after a refused flush, discard never flushed. Live `NEWS_L1/L2/index.pkl` mtimes unchanged (21:43:25/26), `backups/` 29 before and after, tree clean.
  - Suites: unit **1,374 passed, 5 skipped** (from 1,335); integration **127 passed** (from 124).
  - Found on the way: (1) the save lock needs the index's parent dir (`FileNotFoundError …\NEWS_L1.lock` when the repro's `--dir` did not exist). Filed and fixed as **SYS-55 (#189)**; production was not affected. (2) A refused flush left `_pending_embedding_ids` on the long-lived worker repository, so the next source's flush would have written ids of vectors that were dropped with the reload. Fixed inside SYS-53, since only its reload made it reachable (before, a failed flush kept the vectors dirty in memory and the next flush saved them). (3) The run-lock check had to move **inside** the save lock. Outside it, a manual save that passed the check just before a run took the lock could swap in after the worker loaded, failing the run's first flush. (4) A restore that copies back the *same* generation (copy2 keeps mtime and size, in-place copy keeps the file id) is correctly not a change. My first "restore" test was wrong about that.
  - SYS-54 live state (read-only): still VIVA 5,292 sitemaps / 28,928 chunk rows queued with vectors; 9,427 newer pending VIVA rows ahead of the newest of them, so the crawl reaches them in ~5 days. It now removes vectors and rows for each one it settles terminal.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) For a whole-file save, record what you loaded and compare at save time under an OS file lock. A pid-file "is someone running" lock cannot answer "has it changed since I read it". (2) When a guard's check and its action are separate steps, check the other guard *under the same lock the other side takes* (the run-lock check inside the save lock). (3) A pid lock on Windows needs the process create_time. (4) Any per-block state kept on a long-lived object (pending ids) must be dropped on the failure path, not only consumed on success. (5) Turn a repro into a script the unit test runs, so the repro QA reruns and the regression test are one piece of code.
- **Links:** SYS-53 (#182) · OPS-31 (#184) · DBT-43 (#183) · DBT-44 (#186) · DBT-45 (#187) · DBT-46 (#188) · SYS-54 (#185) · SYS-55 (#189) · DBT-03 (#22) · commits 452a1b2, 7c2e188, c45486c, 783d231, 8f02803, 215b99a

### 2026-09-27 — QA audit of HANDOFF_2026-09-27: the 07:00 run never reached a flush; SYS-54's delete breaks DBT-10's torn-pair argument; 6 new tickets
- **Question / hypothesis:** Do the SYS-53 / OPS-31 / DBT-43..46 / SYS-54 / SYS-55 / DBT-03 closures hold on this machine and in the first live run (2026-09-27 07:00)? Does the 40/40 mutant claim reproduce, and are the branches the handoff calls uncovered really uncovered?
- **Method:**
  - Watched `logs/scheduled_crawl_2026-09-27_0700.log` live from 07:11; read-only SQL on `newsaggregator` via `docker exec newsaggregator-postgres psql`.
  - Suites after the run: `tests/unit`, `tests/integration`.
  - `qa_mutation_runner.py` (all 40) plus 4 QA mutants, from a tmp copy with `ROOT` pinned; then a second tmp runner for a *true move* of `refuse_foreign_writer()` above `with save_lock` (the runner's `sys53_run_lock_outside_save_lock` only deletes the call) and for the terminal-SQL arms against `tests/integration/test_discard_terminal_embeddings_live.py`.
  - `scripts/experiments/repro_torn_pair_after_delete.py` (new): tmp index with one-hot embeddings, `load_index`, delete one sitemap's vector (what `discard()` does), then `_atomic_save_local` with `os.replace` raising right after the `index.pkl` swap.
  - NTFS check: `os.replace` over a file with identical mtime/size, then compare `(st_ino, st_mtime_ns, st_size)`.
- **Result:**
  - **07:00 run:** sitemap phase 28/28 ok (07:00:35–07:08:16). Full_text: WARTAEKONOMI ran first (OPS-28 order works), but the probe was ~2× slower than on 09-26 19:00 for the same source (45–80 s per batch vs ~34 s; batch 1 159 s). Batches 1–9 took 737 s. DNS failed from ~07:21 (`Retry 0/1 to connect` on all batch-10 fetches), and the source was **killed at 900 s (07:23:17)**, so its 9 batches' vectors were never flushed (3,976 chunk rows NULL, 811 articles without vectors, the designed repairable state). Every respawn then failed in `init_crawler` on `huggingface.co` (`getaddrinfo failed`) although the model is cached, ~3.5 min each, so **27 of 28 sources failed** by 09:00. The Telegram summary failed to send, and **its error line contains the bot token** in plaintext. Store check: only `chunks_pending_embedding` / `chunks_not_in_faiss` growing (WARTAEKONOMI's), `l1/l2_no_articles_terminal` 0. `huggingface.co` answered 200 again by 11:53. **No flush ran, so SYS-53's generation check and SYS-54's discard have still not touched the live indexes** (NEWS_L1/L2 mtimes still 2026-09-26 21:43).
  - `NEWS_L1.lock`/`NEWS_L2.lock` are absent during and after a run: filelock 3.12.0's `WindowsFileLock._release` removes the file. The handoff's risk-1 check "they exist afterwards" is wrong on Windows (corrected on SYS-53).
  - NTFS: `os.replace` changes `st_ino` (…577 → …578) with identical mtime and size, so SYS-53's generation premise holds.
  - Suites: unit **1,374 passed, 5 skipped**; integration **127 passed**. Both match the handoff.
  - Mutants: **the dev team's 40: 40 KILLED** (claim reproduces; `sys53_run_lock_outside_save_lock` dies only via a 120 s pytest-timeout hang). QA's: `runlock_truly_moved` **SURVIVED** unit · `access_denied_is_owner` **SURVIVED** unit · `terminal_sql_no_fetch_cap` / `terminal_sql_no_not_exists` survive unit but are **KILLED by the integration test** (`assert {1} == {1, 2}`, `assert {5} == {1, 2}`), as the handoff said.
  - Torn-pair repro: `index.ntotal=6 index_to_docstore_id=5`, `query d2 -> d3 WRONG`, `query d3 -> d4 WRONG`, `query d5 -> KeyError`. LangChain 0.3.24 `FAISS.delete` renumbers `index_to_docstore_id`, so after SYS-54's delete a (new pkl, old faiss) pair is silently wrong, not stale-but-consistent. Nothing checks `ntotal` against the docstore.
  - Read-only SQL: WARTAEKONOMI settled 89 `rejected_filter` today, with 0 chunk rows. The fleet has 0 terminal no-article sitemaps with chunk rows, so today's kill orphaned nothing. The gap (SYS-57) is latent.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **DBT-10's torn-pair safety depends on "the crawl only appends". Any delete in a save that can be killed breaks it** (SYS-56). Re-check that argument whenever a writer starts deleting. (2) A cleanup triggered by in-memory events at the end of a killable unit is lost with the kill once the triggering state is already durable. Derive cleanup candidates from the store (SYS-57). (3) A worker rebuilt mid-run must not need the network for cached models; one timeout plus a DNS blip cost 27 sources (OPS-32). (4) Exception messages from HTTP clients carry the URL, and Telegram's URL carries the token (OPS-33). (5) A "moved outside the lock" mutant must also remove the inner call, or it is a no-op.
- **Links:** SYS-56 (#190) · SYS-57 (#191) · SYS-58 (#192) · OPS-32 (#193) · OPS-33 (#194) · DBT-47 (#195) · SYS-53 (#182) · SYS-54 (#185) · OPS-28 (#179) · `scripts/experiments/repro_torn_pair_after_delete.py`

### 2026-09-27 — SYS-56..59 / OPS-32 / OPS-33 / DBT-47: the QA list of 44df5b7 fixed; a torn pair cannot happen, a killed source is cleaned up next run, the worker builds offline
- **Question / hypothesis:** Can the six tickets of QA's 44df5b7 audit be fixed so that each Done-when is shown by a test that fails before the fix, and each fix is guarded by a killed mutant? Does HF_HUB_OFFLINE make the worker build without huggingface.co (OPS-32's first proposal)?
- **Method:**
  - SYS-56: `scripts/experiments/repro_torn_pair_after_delete.py` rewritten as the module its unit test runs (`TestKilledMidSave`). `--all` kills the save after each of its filesystem steps (copy, rename, replace) and then loads through `index_generation.load_index`. "Before" ran the same script against a `git archive HEAD` snapshot of `newscrawler/`.
  - SYS-57: the real `CrawlerServiceImpl` over the real repository (tmp FAISS, fake chunk table, fake sitemap store), with `SystemExit` raised from batch 2; then a second run of the source. `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` of the new query on the live DB, read-only.
  - OPS-32: `HTTPS_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:9 HTTP_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:9`: (a) `SentenceTransformer(MiniLM)` as before; (b) `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` plus the same; (c) `build_crawler_api()` after the fix.
  - OPS-33: unit test with a `ConnectionError` naming `/bot<token>/sendMessage`; `grep -rE "bot[0-9]+:[A-Za-z0-9_-]{30,}" logs/`.
  - Mutants: `qa_mutation_runner.py`, from a `git clone --local` in the scratchpad (see the trap below), all 69 mutants at c9d5a56 and after the runner fix.
- **Result:**
  - **SYS-56 repro.** Before: `ntotal=6 mapped=5`, `d2 -> d3`, `d3 -> d4`, `d4 -> d5`, `d5 -> KeyError(5)`. After: all 7 kill points give a whole pair (`old` for the 2 before the commit rename, `new` for the 5 after it), and every query is right. The live store check with the new `faiss_pair_mismatch` check: all 11 checks zero, 3.5 s. L1 46,540 / L2 158,429 vectors, 5,292 queued VIVA ids (info).
  - **SYS-59, found while working SYS-58.** Suppose a roll-forward's `os.replace` raises `PermissionError` (a scanner holds `index.faiss`). The same writer's next save then rolled its own journal forward and refused itself (`StaleIndexError`); `['a','b']` was on disk and "c" was dropped. After the fix (an owner file in the journal): `['a','b','c']`, and a foreign journal is still refused.
  - **SYS-57.** Killed run: 7 is `rejected_filter` in SQL, but its vectors and 3 rows are left. Next run: neither is left, store check zero. Live `terminal_with_chunks('VIVA')`: 33 ms, 0 rows (19,538 VIVA rejected/capped sitemaps probed).
  - **SYS-58.** Tests for disk-full-then-next-block and for L2-refused-after-L1-saved-then-next-block fail on HEAD~ (`7 in [7, 7]`). After the fix, both end with the store check at zero.
  - **OPS-32.** (a) `ProxyError ... huggingface.co ... /resolve/main/adapter_config.json` after **78 s**, with the model cached. (b) **HF_HUB_OFFLINE does not work:** `OfflineModeIsEnabled: Cannot reach https://huggingface.co/api/models/sentence-transformers/paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2`. The call comes from `transformers` 4.57.3 `tokenization_utils_base._patch_mistral_regex` → `is_base_mistral` → `huggingface_hub.model_info()`. It runs for every tokenizer with vocab > 100k (both our models), and it is skipped only when `_is_local`. That is the `/api/models/...` request in the 07:00 log. (c) Loading from the snapshot directory (`core/utils/hf_cache.resolve_model`): the whole `CrawlerAPI` builds in **17.6 s** with the network cut.
  - **OPS-33.** One leaked copy (`logs/scheduled_crawl_2026-09-27_0700.log:6488`), redacted in place; the grep is now empty. `git log --all -G` finds no token in history.
  - **Mutants:** 69 mutants, 69 KILLED, 0 SURVIVED (full run at c9d5a56 was 66 killed; 3 runner patterns re-targeted after SYS-58/59 changed their lines and then killed). All but one die on a failing test; sys53_run_lock_check_deleted still dies by the 120 s pytest-timeout hang, because under -x an earlier test hangs before DBT-47's spy assertion runs. On the first pass `ops32_refs_main_ignored` SURVIVED: the test's `main` snapshot was also first by name. The test was fixed (98068df).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. Two files cannot be replaced atomically. Commit a pair with one directory rename (the journal), and roll it forward before any read or write under the lock. A reader-side count check is only a backstop: a delete-plus-add keeps the counts equal.
  2. A journal needs an owner. Otherwise the writer's own interrupted commit looks like someone else's save.
  3. On a failure path, drop only the state that nothing needs any more. SYS-53 rightly drops the pending *ids*. The *discards* must be kept and re-applied, because nothing else re-finds them within the run.
  4. Do not use `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` for sentence-transformers on transformers 4.57.x; load from the snapshot directory.
  5. Run the mutation runner from a clone at a path that does not push the venv's site-packages past MAX_PATH, and check that the unmutated clone passes before trusting any KILLED. A collection error "kills" every mutant in 3 s.

### 2026-09-28 — QA audit of HANDOFF_2026-09-27_night (SYS-56..59, OPS-32/33, DBT-47) and the first live run of the FAISS journal
- **Question / hypothesis:** Do the closures hold beyond the dev's own tests and mutants, and does the new FAISS save path (SYS-56 journal, SYS-59 owner, SYS-57 sweep, OPS-32 offline build) work in the first scheduled run (2026-09-28 07:00)?
- **Method:**
  - Suites on the live tree; `check_store_consistency.py --report-dir <scratch>`; read-only `terminal_with_chunks(w)` for all 30 sources.
  - 29 QA mutants **not** in `qa_mutation_runner.py` (pair check, journal cleanup, owner file, `.bak` by move, flush dirty-set, discard rows, sweep order, SQL arms, breaker, outbox, redact, hf_cache), run from a `git -c core.autocrlf=false archive HEAD` clone with the real venv python by absolute path. The unmutated clone ran first (green once `test_repo_hygiene.py` was ignored: it needs `git ls-files`). The SQL arms were run under `tests/integration`.
  - Model pin: re-embedded 40 stored L1 chunks with each cached MiniLM snapshot and compared with `index.reconstruct` (cosine). `huggingface_hub.model_info` against the cached revisions.
  - OPS-32: `build_crawler_api()` with `HTTP(S)_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:9`.
  - Journal save on a copy of the real L2 (158,429 vectors): 5× delete-one + `_atomic_save_local`.
  - `scripts/experiments/repro_foreign_rollforward_stale.py` (new): SYS-59's case, but with a *different* process finishing the journal.
  - Watched the 07:00 run live: files after the first flush, pair check under the save lock, then the SUMMARY, store check, funnels, per-source timings against earlier runs, `fetch_failures` of this run's failures, cascade scan of every saved body.
- **Result:**
  - Suites 1,421/5 unit, 127 integration (reproduce). Store check 0 on all 11 before and after the run. Sweep query: 0 rows fleet-wide, slowest 33 ms.
  - QA mutants: 20 killed by `tests/unit`, plus the 2 SQL arms killed by `tests/integration` (as the handoff says). Survivors: `no_fsync` (known), `stop_exit_code_ignored` (equivalent: a stop always follows `init_failed` results), `failed_swap_not_tracked` (equivalent since SYS-59), `new_msg_jumps_queue` and `cli_message_queued` → **DBT-48 #199**. `finally_no_track` was a faulty mutant (a no-op), not a result.
  - Model pin: cos = 1.000000 for both snapshots (86741b4e and e8f8c211 embed identically). Hub `main` == cached for e5-large and MiniLM. Neither the probe json nor FAISS records the revision → **SYS-61 #198** (latent).
  - OPS-32: ProxyError on huggingface.co; crawler built in **17.5 s**. Live at 07:00: built in ~20 s from `…\snapshots\…` paths.
  - Journal save on the real-size L2: 1.8–2.6 s per save, no leftovers, `disk_pair_problem` None.
  - Repro: `A's save 2: StaleIndexError`, final `['a','b']` → **SYS-60 #197**. A same-volume `os.rename`/`os.replace` keeps `(st_ino, st_mtime_ns, st_size)` on NTFS, so the fix can track the staged generation.
  - **07:00 run:** 28/28 sitemap ok; 28/28 full_text ok; +1,036 sitemaps, +3,984 articles; 0 ERROR lines; 0 Stale/Corrupt/"interrupted"; no `NEWS_L*.tmp`/`.commit` left; lock released. First flush (WARTAEKONOMI) L1 0.9 s, L2 2.5 s. After it, L1 47,413 vectors, all mapped, no problem; L2 162,047 likewise. End: L1 50,524, L2 173,619, store check all 0. 0 of 3,984 bodies cascaded (SRC-54). No native crash or rebuild (SYS-52).
  - **No `removed N vector(s)` line.** VIVA's 5,292 June-vector sitemaps sit at queue ranks 7,596..~28,000 of 28,175 eligible (newest first, LIMIT 1000). The first is reachable in ~4–5 days (comment on SYS-54 #185).
  - **DETIK 885 s of 900** (583/594 s before). One batch: 9 of 100 extracted, 91 `ConnectionError after 5 retries` in ~4 minutes. All 92 were charged `fetch_failures=1` plus a 7-day cooldown, because the per-URL/source-level split runs only in a source-wide run → **SYS-62 #200**.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. SYS-56's journal, SYS-57's sweep and OPS-32's offline build work in production. The live run is the evidence, not the tests alone.
  2. A journal owner file only proves "mine" while the journal exists. Identity that survives someone else's roll-forward is the staged files' own (ino, mtime, size).
  3. Before planning "close on the first run's evidence", compute where the rows sit in the queue. A newest-first LIMIT queue reaches an old backlog only after the newer eligible rows drain.
  4. "Failure class decided by run-wide ratio" misses bursts. Decide by reason per failure.
  5. A mutant must be checked to change behaviour: a pattern that leaves the target line in the same block is a no-op that "survives".
