### 2026-09-28 — The QA list of f4b899e (SYS-60/61/62, DBT-48) fixed, and OPS-28's timeout sized from crawl_log
- **Question / hypothesis:** (1) SYS-62: are the fetch-failure strikes of a healthy run's bursts really on good URLs, beyond QA's 92 DETIK rows? (2) SYS-61: which encoder revisions built the committed probe and the live FAISS indexes, so they can be recorded rather than assumed? (3) SYS-60: does the staged files' identity survive the commit rename and roll-forward on this filesystem? (4) OPS-28: is DETIK's 885/900 s one source's problem or the fleet's?
- **Method:**
  - (1) Read-only SQL over `sitemaps` with `fetch_failures > 0`, grouped by source, reason and attempt hour. Then a live re-fetch of 12 random struck URLs per group, through `RequestsPageLoader.get_soup_with_reason` (the crawl's own call): KUMPARAN `HTTP 403`, VIVA `HTTP 502`, DETIK `ConnectionError after 5 retries`. Inline script; the query is in SYS-62's closing comment.
  - (2) `scripts/experiments/probe_encoder_revision.py` (new, read-only, offline). It re-embeds 40 gold test rows with the e5-large snapshot that `resolve_model` picks and compares them with the committed `tests/fixtures/probe_test_embeddings.npz`. `--faiss NEWS_L1` / `NEWS_L2` does the same for 40 stored vectors per index. It reads `index.faiss` memory-mapped with `IO_FLAG_READ_ONLY`, takes no lock and rolls nothing forward.
  - (3) `tests/unit/test_chunk_faiss_atomic_save.py::TestOwnInterruptedCommit::test_the_committed_generation_is_the_one_on_disk_after_the_roll_forward`, plus QA's `repro_foreign_rollforward_stale.py` before and after.
  - (4) `crawl_log` `full_text` durations per source since 2026-09-26 (read-only).
- **Result:**
  - (1) There are 929 good-URL strikes, not 92: DETIK 92 + PIKIRANRAKYAT 4 `ConnectionError after 5 retries`, KUMPARAN **572** `HTTP 403` (487 of them in one run, 2026-09-26 08:xx WIB), VIVA **253** + IDNTIMES 7 `HTTP 502` (spread over 3 runs), WARTAEKONOMI 1 `HTTP 500`. Every run involved was `completed` and saved articles. **36 of 36** sampled URLs fetched fine on 2026-09-28. The strikes are all 1 of 3, so nothing is retired yet.
  - (2) e5-large `3d7cfbda…`: cosine min/mean/max **1.000000** on 40 rows. MiniLM `e8f8c211…`: **1.000000** on 40 vectors of NEWS_L1 (50,524) and of NEWS_L2 (173,619), positions spread from the oldest to the newest.
  - (3) The committed generation equals the one on disk after the roll-forward (NTFS). Repro before: `A's save 2: StaleIndexError`, final `['a','b']`. After: `A's save 2: OK`, final `['a','b','c']`.
  - (4) Five sources run close to the timeout on every run, because each hits the 1,000-row cap: MEDIAINDONESIA 880–947 s (its own 1,200), JPNN 728–767, VIVA 676–754, WARTAEKONOMI 534–637, DETIK 583–624, then 885 with the burst. So one burst puts any of them within reach of the kill.
  - Mutation runner: the SYS-61 carry-forward mutant survived. It is equivalent: `roll_forward` moves only what the journal holds, so a save without an encoder already leaves `encoder.json` in place. The branch was removed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. SYS-62: decide a failure's strike by its reason, not by the run's failure ratio. 403, 429, 5xx and `<Error> after N retries` are transient. They strike only when the URL's previous recorded failure was transient too, so a URL that is dead for good still retires, one attempt later. There is no schema change: the previous reason is `last_fetch_error`. The 929 existing strikes are left for the owner to reset (dry run on SYS-62).
  2. SYS-61: "pinned to the cached snapshot" is a claim about `refs/main`, and `refs/main` moves. Record the revision in the artifact and compare it on load. Re-embedding a sample against the stored vectors is the way to find out what a legacy artifact was built with, before stamping it.
  3. SYS-60: identify your own commit by the files you committed (id, mtime_ns, size, read before the rename), not by a marker inside the journal. The marker disappears when someone else finishes the journal.
  4. OPS-28: size a kill timeout from the healthy distribution plus the known disturbance (here, one burst, about +300 s). Do not size it from one source's worst run.
  5. A mutant that survives can point at dead code, not only at a missing test. Check whether the branch can matter before writing a test for it.
- **Links:** SYS-62 (#200) 0de112a, f5f0a6b · SYS-60 (#197) d9f07ca, f459e57 · DBT-48 (#199) fde6a4b, b6e6acc · SYS-61 (#198) c37179b, c4ebfd7, 508035f · OPS-28 (#179) c64e2f0 · `scripts/experiments/probe_encoder_revision.py` · `scripts/experiments/repro_foreign_rollforward_stale.py`

### 2026-09-29 — QA audit of HANDOFF_2026-09-28 (SYS-60/61/62, DBT-48; OPS-28/SYS-52/SRC-54 third run)
- **Question / hypothesis:** Do the four dev closures and the three "third clean run" tickets hold on the tests, on QA's own mutants and on the live 2026-09-28 19:00 run? Is SYS-62's premise ("403/5xx/ConnectionError after N retries are transient, so strike only on a repeat") right for every source that produced them?
- **Method:**
  - Suites on `788f65d`. `tests/unit`, `tests/integration`.
  - 13 QA mutants, none from the dev's list, run in a `git -c core.autocrlf=false archive HEAD` clone with DB/FAISS/Telegram env neutralised. Runner inlined at `$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp/qa_mut.py`. Targets: SYS-62 note/repeat/source-wide/regex; SYS-60 tracking point, recover-before-check, generation tuple, staged-vs-live read; SYS-61 None revision, record revision, legacy warning, reload path.
  - The 19:00 log: worker pids, the count of saves per worker, ERROR/Stale/Corrupt lines, the `transient fetch failures` notes, the `encoder.json` contents.
  - `crawl_log` / `sitemaps` SQL: the transient rows are `fetch_failures=0`; `chunks.embedding_id IS NULL`; completed `full_text` rows per WIB day.
  - Per-batch failure position for KUMPARAN, VIVA and PIKIRANRAKYAT across four runs (awk over `Processing batch` / `articles extracted` / `Status Code` lines).
  - A read-only re-fetch through `RequestsPageLoader.get_soup_with_reason` of 20 random rows each: KUMPARAN `HTTP 403` at 1 req/s and VIVA `HTTP 502` at 2 req/s, taken from the 19:00 run's first-transient rows.
  - A same-size generation repro: two `load_index` copies, B deletes one doc and adds one of equal length, then saves. Is A refused, and do the file sizes collide?
- **Result:**
  - Suites: 1,471 passed / 5 skipped; 129 passed. Both reproduce.
  - The 19:00 run: 28/28 sitemap and full_text ok, 0 ERROR lines. **One worker process (pid 49376) served all 28 sources and saved each index 28 times, with no StaleIndexError.** That is live evidence for SYS-60's committed-generation tracking. `NEWS_L1/L2/encoder.json` = MiniLM `e8f8c211…`. The legacy warning was logged once per index, at build time.
  - SYS-62 live: 3 notes (PIKIRANRAKYAT 228 `ConnectionError after 5 retries`, KUMPARAN 96 `HTTP 403`, VIVA 120 `HTTP 502`), all `fetch_failures=0` with `last_fetch_error` set, matching `crawl_log.error_msg`. `check_crawlers` reads only `task='health_check'` rows, so the new non-NULL `error_msg` on completed rows is harmless.
  - Mutants: 10 killed. **3 survived:**
    - `q60_generation_size_only` and `q60_generation_no_ino`: no test separates two generations of equal size. The repro shows it is not reachable by a real save: `index.pkl` went 404 → 454 bytes on an equal-count delete+add, and HEAD refuses A as designed. Practically equivalent; no ticket.
    - `q61_reload_path_unchecked`: another writer can only commit a different `encoder.json` if it built with another revision, and its own build refuses that. Near-equivalent; no ticket.
  - **KUMPARAN:** 2026-09-27 19:00: batches 1–6 20/20, then 7–10 0/20 (all 403). 2026-09-28 19:00: batches 1–5 20/20, then 6–10 0/20. 2026-09-26: 117 ok, then 6×403. 2026-09-28 07:00 (73 rows): none. The re-fetch: **20/20 OK**. This is a positional rate limit (~100–120 fetches/run), not random transients. 572 KUMPARAN rows already carry one 403 strike (pre-SYS-62), and on their next visit they sit in the tail again.
  - VIVA 502s are scattered, ~12% of every batch; re-fetch 18/20 OK. PIKIRANRAKYAT was one burst (batches 5–8). SYS-62 fits both.
  - SYS-52: `embedding_id IS NULL` = 0, no native crash in 3 runs. SRC-54: EMITENNEWS 2,012 articles, 0 cascaded; 0 fleet-wide among 37,901 recently attempted. OPS-28: 28/28 HEALTHY sources completed on 09-26, 09-27 and 09-28; longest run VIVA 733 s of 1,200.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. SYS-60/61/62 and DBT-48 hold. SYS-52, SRC-54 and OPS-28 closed by QA.
  2. "Transient" is a property of the failure's *position and pattern*, not only its reason. A 403 wall at the end of every run is a rate limit, and SYS-62's repeat rule turns it into strikes on good URLs → **SRC-55 (#201)**. The dev's own SYS-62 note (487 of 572 KUMPARAN strikes in one run) was the same signal, read as "a burst".
  3. A surviving mutant on an identity tuple is worth a reachability repro before a ticket. Here the pickle size moved on its own.
- **Links:** SRC-55 (#201) · SYS-52 (#178), SRC-54 (#177), OPS-28 (#179) closed · SYS-60 (#197), SYS-61 (#198), SYS-62 (#200), DBT-48 (#199) · `logs/scheduled_crawl_2026-09-28_1900.log`

### 2026-09-29 — SRC-55: KUMPARAN's rate limit, measured; the 403-streak breaker live
- **Question / hypothesis:** Is KUMPARAN's 403 wall a per-run request count, a request rate, or a timed ban? How long does it last? Does the SRC-55 breaker stop a run at the wall and leave the unreached rows alone?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_rate_limit.py` (new, read-only; GETs links of `sitemaps` rows with the crawl's headers, writes nothing), run between scheduled windows with disjoint link offsets:
  - `--mode recover --interval 120 --offset 300` at 20:27, 32 min after the 19:55 scheduled wall.
  - `--mode burst --workers 10 --stop-after 5 --max 300 --offset 400` (the crawl's concurrency), then `--mode recover --interval 60 --offset 800`.
  - `--mode paced --rate 1 --max 250 --offset 1200` (sequential; each GET takes ~2.3 s, so the achieved rate is 0.43/s).
  - The 2026-09-29 19:00 scheduled run (the working tree carried the breaker), plus SRC-55's Done-when SQL on `sitemaps`.
- **Result:**
  - Recovery after the scheduled wall: first request at 20:27 answered 200. The block had lifted within 32 min.
  - Burst: 147 requests in 33.7 s (4.4/s); **first 403 at request #130, 32.0 s in**; 134 × 200, 13 × 403. No `Retry-After` or rate-limit headers on the 403. Recovery: 403 at t+1 s, **200 at t+63 s**.
  - Paced: 250 requests in 580 s (0.43/s): 248 × 200, 2 × 404, **no 403**.
  - Scheduled 19:00 run: `KUMPARAN: throttled (SRC-55): 10 x HTTP 403 in a row after 130 fetches; stopped this run. 20 throttled and 51 not fetched left untouched (no strike, no cooldown)`, 57 saved. `crawl_log.error_msg` carries the note. `last_fetch_error='HTTP 403' AND fetch_failures>0`: 572 before and after (no growth); rows stamped with a 403 in the last 12 h: 0. The 07:00 run (102 rows, below the wall) had no 403.
  - Also observed in the 19:00 run, unrelated to this change: EMITENNEWS and MERDEKA failed their flush with `PermissionError: [WinError 5] Access is denied: 'NEWS_L1.tmp' -> 'NEWS_L1.commit'` (first occurrence in any log; saves before and after succeeded; no other process had the repo open), and VIVA hit OPS-28's 1,200 s kill. Left for QA to ticket.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. KUMPARAN throttles on a sliding window of about 130 requests per ~minute, and lifts about a minute after the wall. It is not a per-run or per-day count. Under ~0.5 req/s it never walls.
  2. The breaker costs the rest of that run only, and the window explains why the old pre-breaker runs got 403 for the whole tail: the crawl kept hitting it faster than it cleared.
  3. If KUMPARAN's intake ever needs more than ~130 rows per run, pace it (a per-source minimum interval, ~1 req/s across workers) instead of raising the streak limit. Not built: SRC-55 lists it as optional, and the breaker alone ends the strikes.
  4. After the owner-approved reset (backup `backups/src55_sys62_reset_20260929T140350Z.json` + `pg_dump` `newsaggregator_20260929T140233Z.dump`), the 929 SYS-62 strikes and KUMPARAN's 96 first-transient markers are 0. SRC-55's "does not grow" query now starts from 0.
- **Links:** SRC-55 (#201) cb94682 · SYS-62 (#200) · `scripts/experiments/probe_rate_limit.py` · `logs/scheduled_crawl_2026-09-29_1900.log`

### 2026-09-29 — OPS-20 / SYS-39: extraction cost per article, measured, and priced
- **Question / hypothesis:** What does one knowledge-extraction call cost today, and what would the paid options cost at the current volume? Is production's `gemini-2.5-flash` spending thinking tokens on an extraction prompt?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_extraction_cost.py` (new). The extractor's own `_PROMPT_TEMPLATE`, text cut at 3,000 chars, the newest article per SYS-37 topic. Provider token counts (`usage_metadata`: prompt, candidates, thoughts; OpenAI `usage`), latency, JSON parse, entity Jaccard vs the first config. Run 1: n=6 × {flash, flash-nothink, 3.5-flash-lite, deepseek}; `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` answered `404 ... no longer available to new users ... use models/gemini-3.5-flash-lite`. Run 2: n=4 × {flash, flash-again (self-agreement), 3.5-flash-lite, deepseek}. Every call was counted in `api_usage_log`. Volume from `crawl_log` (full_text `article_count` per day) and `articles.knowledge_extracted`. Prices fetched 2026-09-29 from ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing and api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/pricing; SumoPod publishes none.
- **Result:**

  | config | in tok | out tok | thinking tok | s/call | parsed | Jaccard vs flash |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | gemini-2.5-flash (production: thinking on) | 751 | 722 | **1,932** | 12.1 | 6/6 | 1.00 (ref) |
  | same, second run (noise floor) | 756 | 701 | 1,427 | 10.3 | 3/3 | **0.53** |
  | gemini-2.5-flash, thinking_budget=0 | 751 | 669 | 0 | 4.0 | 6/6 | 0.67 |
  | gemini-3.5-flash-lite | 756 | 447 | 0 | 14.7 (503s under load) | 3/3 | 0.76 |
  | deepseek-v4-flash (SumoPod, thinking off) | 874 | 414 | 0 | 3.3 | 10/10 | 0.64–0.75 |

  Prices per 1M tokens (in / out, thinking billed as out): 2.5-flash $0.30/$2.50, Batch $0.15/$1.25; 3.5-flash-lite $0.30/$2.50, Batch $0.15/$1.25; 3.1-flash-lite $0.25/$1.50, Batch $0.125/$0.75; DeepSeek flash $0.15/$0.60 off-peak (cache miss), double in peak (01-04, 06-10 UTC weekdays).
  Cost per 1,000 articles: 2.5-flash as production calls it **$6.86** (Batch $3.43); thinking off $1.90 (Batch $0.95); 3.5-flash-lite $1.34 (Batch $0.67); DeepSeek $0.38 off-peak / $0.76 peak.
  Volume: full_text saved 6,578 / 9,104 / 8,142 on 09-27/28/29 (~8,000/day). `knowledge_extracted`: **0 of 58,876** articles ever extracted, and extraction is not scheduled. At ~240k articles/month: 2.5-flash as-is ≈ **$1,650/mo**; thinking off ≈ $456 (Batch $228); 3.5-flash-lite ≈ $322 (Batch $161); DeepSeek ≈ $91–182. The 58,876 backlog, one-off: $404 / $56 (flash nothink Batch) / $39 (lite Batch) / $22–45 (DeepSeek). The free tier (20 requests/day/model) covers 0.25% of one day's saves.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. Never run extraction on `gemini-2.5-flash` with default thinking: 73% of its output tokens are thinking, and it costs 5× the same model with `thinking_budget=0` for no measurable gain. Production's own run-to-run agreement (0.53) is below every cheaper option's agreement with it.
  2. At n ≤ 6 the quality question is not answered: the Jaccard spread is inside the noise floor. SYS-39's 200-article fixture bake-off decides quality. This entry decides cost.
  3. The cheapest option is DeepSeek via the key the owner already uses for the labeller (SumoPod's own rate is unknown; DeepSeek's list price is the bound). The cheapest option on the existing Gemini client is `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (or 3.1-flash-lite) through the Batch API.
  4. `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` is closed to new users; do not plan on it.
- **Links:** OPS-20 (#136), SYS-39 (#125) · `scripts/experiments/probe_extraction_cost.py`

### 2026-09-29 — DBT-05: extractor order, measured across the fleet
- **Question / hypothesis:** DBT-05 proposes layout-independent extraction first (JSON-LD `articleBody`, then trafilatura), with site selectors only as an override. Is that better than today's selector-first order?
- **Method:** (1) Read-only SQL: `articles.meta_data->>'extractor'` per source over sitemaps attempted in the last 7 days (OPS-02). (2) `scripts/experiments/probe_extractor_order.py` (new, read-only): the 4 newest saved articles per source, one fetch each, three extractors on the same soup (the crawler's `_get_whole_text`, JSON-LD `articleBody`, the crawler's trafilatura fallback). The reference is site text where usable (≥ `MIN_ARTICLE_CHARS`), else trafilatura. Per method: usable, word recall and precision vs the reference, and "Baca juga"-style boilerplate hits.
- **Result:**
  - Extractor mix (7 days): **13 of 28 sources are ~0–2% site** (ANTARANEWS, CNBC, CNN, IDNTIMES, INEWS, KONTAN, KUMPARAN, MERDEKA, OKEZONE, PIKIRANRAKYAT, SUARA, TEMPO, TIRTO: every article comes from trafilatura). The other 15 are 94–100% site.
  - JSON-LD `articleBody`: present on **2 of 28** (IDNTIMES recall 0.93; TEMPO 0.78, truncated).
  - Trafilatura where the site selector works (15): usable 60/60. Recall ≥0.96 on 14 sources, but **JPNN 0.65 recall / 0.73 precision**. Precision below 0.9 (text added) on **MEDIAINDONESIA 0.56, EMITENNEWS 0.75, TRIBUN 0.85, SINDONEWS 0.86, WARTAEKONOMI 0.89, BERITASATU 0.84**.
  - Boilerplate hits ("baca juga" etc.): site output BERITASATU 9, TVONENEWS 8, MEDIAINDONESIA 5; trafilatura output KONTAN 11, TRIBUN 10, ANTARANEWS 9, LIPUTAN6 9, PIKIRANRAKYAT 8, WARTAEKONOMI 8.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. JSON-LD-first is not viable here (2/28 publish a body). Do not build it.
  2. Trafilatura-first everywhere would cost precision on 6 of the 15 sources whose selectors work, and a third of JPNN's body. Keep a working selector.
  3. The 13 dead selectors are not 13 bugs. They are one decision: trafilatura is those sources' primary extractor, declared per crawler, so the health check reports `OK`, not `FALLBACK`, and the dead selector stops running. SRC-19/20/22/23/24/26 close together on this. A selector is only worth writing for a source where trafilatura measurably loses (JPNN-shaped).
  4. Boilerplate leaks through both paths; stripping it is a separate, shared step (not a reason to prefer either order).
- **Links:** DBT-05 (#24); SRC-19 (#99), SRC-20 (#100), SRC-22 (#102), SRC-23 (#103), SRC-24 (#104), SRC-26 (#106) · `scripts/experiments/probe_extractor_order.py`

### 2026-09-29 — OPS-19: the first four days of the unattended schedule, in numbers
- **Question / hypothesis:** Has the twice-daily `task=all` (registered 2026-09-25 23:07; first run 2026-09-26 07:00) run unattended for three consecutive days with a `crawl_log` row for every HEALTHY source, and what does a day of real volume look like?
- **Method:** Read-only. The `SUMMARY:` line of every `logs/scheduled_crawl_*.log` (per-run log files from 2026-09-26 19:00 on). `crawl_log` per WIB day and task (distinct websites, completed/failed, `sum(article_count)`). `schtasks /Query /TN "NewsAggregator Scheduled Crawl (morning)"`. `sitemaps` for the six once-never-crawled sources and OKEZONE's June rows.
- **Result:**

  | WIB day | sitemap: sources / completed / failed / new rows | full_text: sources / completed / failed / saved |
  |---|---|---|
  | 09-26 | 28 / 59 / 0 / 34,136 (first listing of the widened fleet) | 28 / 58 / 1 / 22,678 |
  | 09-27 | 28 / 56 / 0 / 3,013 | 28 / 28 / 1 / 6,578 (07:00 lost to the owner's DNS outage, OPS-32) |
  | 09-28 | 28 / 56 / 0 / 4,652 | 28 / 56 / 0 / 9,104 |
  | 09-29 | 28 / 56 / 0 / 5,386 | 28 / 53 / 3 / 8,142 (19:00: EMITENNEWS and MERDEKA `WinError 5` on the FAISS commit rename, VIVA the 1,200 s kill) |

  Run SUMMARY lines, 09-26 19:00 to 09-29 19:00: +2,381/+9,646, +949/+811 (27 failed, outage), +2,064/+6,578, +1,036/+3,984, +3,616/+5,120, +1,797/+3,804, +3,589/+4,720 (sitemaps/articles). Task Scheduler: `Last Result 0`, next run 2026-09-30 07:00. The six sources that had no rows before 2026-09-25 now hold BERITASATU 2,387, DETIK 17,414, EMITENNEWS 2,183, ERAID 29, INVESTORID 601, KONTAN 2,771. OKEZONE's June-era rows (posted ≤ 2026-06-02): 1,141, all settled (278 `saved`, 863 `rejected_filter`, 500 of them denied by category before any fetch under SYS-50, so they carry no `last_crawl_attempt`).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. Steady state is about **4,000–5,400 new sitemap rows and 6,500–9,100 saved articles per day** across 28 sources. The 09-26 numbers are the one-off first listing, not a rate. This is the volume SYS-39 (extraction: ~8,000/day), SYS-40 and SYS-41 size against.
  2. `crawl_log` has a row for every HEALTHY source on every day, including the outage day. A lost run shows as failures in the SUMMARY, never as silence.
  3. OKEZONE's "June rows get `last_crawl_attempt`" is met in intent, not literally: a category-denied row is settled without a fetch (SYS-50), so it has no attempt stamp. Check settled status, not the stamp.
- **Links:** OPS-19 (#132), OPS-32 (#193), OPS-28 (#179), SYS-50 (#165) · `logs/scheduled_crawl_2026-09-2*.log`

### 2026-09-29 — OPS-18: the first filter drift audit, and why 90% is the wrong alert floor
- **Question / hypothesis:** How often does an independent judge (the SYS-36 labeller, DeepSeek V4 Flash, same rubric and prompt) agree with a week of the live probe's decisions? Is the ticket's 90% alert floor right?
- **Method:** `scripts/audit_filter_drift.py` (new), first real run 2026-09-29 22:25. Last 7 days: 47,029 saved and 18,317 probe rejects (category-denied rejects excluded). Samples of 100 + 100, round-robin across sources, seeded by ISO week. 10 calls of 20 rows. Saves were judged on headline + body[:500], rejects on the headline alone. Report: `crawler_health/filter_drift_2026-09-29.json`.
- **Result:** agreement on saves **0.87**, on rejects **0.78**, topic agreement on saves **0.83**; 35 disagreements. With the flat 0.90 floor both sides alerted (sent once on Telegram). The disagreements are the probe's known boundary:
  - saved but judged soft: price tables (`Tabel Harga Emas Antam`), bansos/KPR how-to guides, corporate CSR PR, short opinion titles (`Idaman Swasembada`, `Sesko-War`);
  - rejected but judged news: science news (`Eksoplanet`, `Bintang Zombie Sinar-X`), a child-ISPA outbreak from haze, a `santri disetrika` assault, product and fintech launches.
  That matches the probe's measured test precision/recall (0.94 / 0.87) seen through a second labeller (labels' noise floor ~5%), plus the headline-only handicap on rejects.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. A healthy week agrees ~0.87 / ~0.78. The ticket's flat 0.90 would alert every week. The floors are now per side and calibrated: saved 0.80, rejected 0.70, about 2 SE (n=100: ±0.04) under this baseline (`AUDIT_MIN_AGREEMENT_SAVED` / `_REJECTED`). Re-calibrate after a few weeks of reports.
  2. Science and technology discovery stories are the probe's most visible recall gap in this sample. Pull them into the next SYS-36 re-label.
  3. At n=100 per side no source x category cell reaches 20 decisions, so the per-cell alert is dormant at this sample size. Raise `FILTER_AUDIT_PER_SIDE` if per-source signal is wanted; it costs ~1 call per 20 rows.
- **Links:** OPS-18 (#123) · `scripts/audit_filter_drift.py` · `crawler_health/filter_drift_2026-09-29.json`

### 2026-09-29 — SYS-51: gold set refreshed (297 URL categories) and the probe retrained; the e5-base near-miss
- **Question / hypothesis:** Does refreshing the gold set's 289 VIVA + 8 INEWS URL-shaped categories to their named branches change the probe, and do the SYS-37 gates still hold?
- **Method:** `label_filter_gold.py refresh --dry-run`, then `refresh` (297 categories refreshed, 1 stratum moved, 0 rows un-labelled, 0 URL categories left on named sources). Then `train_importance_probe.py`. The first run used **no `--encoder`** and trained on e5-base (see below). The second used `--encoder intfloat/multilingual-e5-large`.
- **Result:**
  - The first, flagless run wrote an **e5-base (768-d) artifact**. The trainer's default encoder is a SYS-47 design constant, not the committed sidecar's. Six `test_importance_filter.py` tests failed (`embedding dim 1024 != 768`). The artifact was restored with `git checkout` before any process loaded it; the next crawl was 07:00. Filed and fixed as DBT-49 (#203). Its `--no-write` gate numbers (recall_general 0.892, topic 0.810) were e5-base's and are not a comparison.
  - e5-large retrain, test split, old → new: recall_finance 0.9405 → 0.941, recall_general 0.910 → 0.898, precision_mixed 0.921 → 0.920, precision_allow 0.944 → 0.944, topic 0.822 → 0.822. `viva_general` recall 0.745 → 0.723: the refreshed categories send some VIVA rows through per-source `deny` (SRC-33's decisions), which is also what the live system does. Gates are met within the owner's 0.02 tolerance, as before.
  - `importance_probe.npz` is **byte-identical**. The probe trains on text + labels, and the refresh changed only categories (the gate evaluation). Only the sidecar changed (gold sha256 `d821b970…`, metrics). `tests/fixtures/probe_test_embeddings.npz` was re-keyed to the new gold sha; its `texts_sha256` check proves the matrix still matches the rows.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. A category-only gold refresh does not change the probe's weights; it changes what the gates measure. Expect a sidecar-only diff.
  2. Never run the trainer without checking the encoder it resolves to. Since DBT-49 the default is the committed sidecar's, and another one needs an explicit flag plus a WARNING.
- **Links:** SYS-51 (#170), DBT-49 (#203) e19bc28 · `tests/fixtures/filter_gold.jsonl`

### 2026-09-29 — SYS-40: DBSCAN chains a busy topic into one "story"; average linkage does not
- **Question / hypothesis:** Once the digest is sectioned by topic, does DBSCAN (eps 0.25, min_samples 2, cosine) still give one cluster per news event on a general-news day?
- **Method:** the 2026-09-28 digest (3,637 articles) rendered with the SYS-40 code. Then an inline script over that day's L1 embeddings (MiniLM, the digest's own `_encode`): cluster sizes per topic for DBSCAN eps ∈ {0.25, 0.20, 0.15, 0.12}, and for `AgglomerativeClustering(metric="cosine", linkage="average")` at distance ∈ {0.35, 0.30, 0.25}, with sample headlines from the biggest clusters.
- **Result:**
  - DBSCAN eps 0.25: markets n=372 → top cluster **221**; law_crime n=838 → **245**; politics n=472 → 117. The rendered top "Pasar" cluster had 22 portals and mixed a minimum-price rule, an IHSG call and a PER table: chaining.
  - Tightening eps trades chaining for noise: at 0.12 law_crime is 68 clusters with **625 of 838** unclustered, and markets still has a 40-article cluster.
  - Average linkage at 0.30: markets 46 clusters (top 51/42/42/38), law_crime 150 (top 30/20/19), politics 77. The top two markets clusters are one story each: the weekly IHSG slide, and the Rp1 minimum share price.
  - Re-rendered 09-28 with average linkage at 0.30: 614 clusters in 12 sections. The shown Pasar clusters are distinct events (rupiah near Rp18,000, the Rp1 floor, Antam gold, IHSG outlook, GOTO, the BEI watchlist, short selling from 1 Oct).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. Don't tune DBSCAN's eps for the digest. On a busy topic it either chains or drops most articles into noise. Average linkage cuts at a whole-cluster distance, so it can't chain.
  2. `NEWSLETTER_CLUSTER_DISTANCE=0.30` and `NEWSLETTER_MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE=2` replace `DBSCAN_EPS`/`DBSCAN_MIN_SAMPLES`. Agglomerative is O(n²) in memory per topic; the largest topic-day here is 838 articles, which is trivial. Recheck if one topic ever passes ~10k articles a day.
- **Links:** SYS-40 (#126) · `digests/2026-09-28.md`

### 2026-10-03 — SYS-41: the FAISS store at 1×/2×/5× today's size, and the real growth rate
- **Question / hypothesis:** At general-news volume, when does the in-RAM flat FAISS store stop being acceptable, and on which axis first: disk, save time, load time, search latency, or RAM?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_vector_store_scale.py` (new). It copies NEWS_L2 to a scratch dir (taken at 18:07 with no writer running) and grows the copy to 2× and 5× with unit-norm random vectors whose docstore entries reuse real chunk texts. It measures `save_local`/`load_local` wall time, file sizes, brute-force search p50/p95 (k=20, 50 queries) and process RSS. Real growth comes from `chunks.created_at` per WIB day.
- **Result:**

  | factor | L2 vectors | index.faiss | save | load | search p50 / p95 | RSS |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1× (today) | 311,557 | 479 MB (+322 MB pkl) | 2.9 s | 2.7 s | 17.9 / 20.7 ms | 1.40 GB |
  | 2× | 623,114 | 957 MB | 4.8 s | 4.8 s | 35.4 / 40.2 ms | 2.32 GB |
  | 5× | 1,557,785 | 2,393 MB | 12.0 s | 10.5 s | 101 / 112 ms | 5.13 GB |

  The grown pickle under-counts, because repeated texts are pickled once; the real pkl costs ~1.03 KB per vector, plus 1.54 KB per vector of index.
  Growth, 2026-09-27..10-02: L2 **22.7k–34.9k vectors a day** (~28k), L1 ~6–9k a day. That is ~72 MB/day of L2 index + pkl, about **2.2 GB a month**.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. Everything scales linearly, and none of it hurts at 2×. 5× (~1.56M L2 vectors) arrives in **~45 days** at today's intake (mid-November 2026). There, the binding constraint is **RAM**: ~5 GB for L2 in every process that loads it (the crawl worker, which also holds e5-large; the web UI holds a second copy) on a 31.4 GB machine that also runs Docker/Postgres. Next is save time: DBT-10 flushes once per source, so 28 sources × ~15 s ≈ 7 min of every run at 5×, inside the per-source timeouts. Search at ~100 ms is fine for the UI.
  2. Recommendation (owner decision on SYS-41): **a retention window for L2** (the ticket's option (a)), e.g. the last 60 days live in FAISS. Older chunks stay in Postgres, where FTS still finds them, and can be re-embedded on demand. That caps L2 near 1.7M vectors (~5.5 GB RSS, ~110 ms search) with no change to the index type or the SYS-53/56/60 save machinery. Quantisation (SQ8, 4× smaller) or HNSW only if the window is not enough. Decide before ~2026-11-15.
- **Links:** SYS-41 (#127) · `scripts/experiments/probe_vector_store_scale.py`

### 2026-10-03 — SYS-48 / SYS-38: the cosine-era corpus cleaned at P(soft) ≥ 0.9, and its rejects re-queued; what the live filter then did
- **Question / hypothesis:** How much of the old cosine filter's output is soft under today's probe, and how much of what it rejected is news? Does a headline-only re-score predict what the body-based live filter keeps?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_sys48_bands.py` over the 11,847 articles fetched before 2026-09-22 02:10 UTC. A 100-row hand read of the P(soft) ≥ 0.8 band (seed 20260930). The owner chose to delete the ≥ 0.9 band: `cleanup_soft_by_probe.py --scope cosine-era --recompute --min-p-soft 0.9`. `requeue_rejected_by_probe.py` (headline P(soft) < 0.7, pre-probe rejects since 2026-07-02), then six scheduled runs fetched the re-queued rows.
- **Result:**
  - Soft today: 2,865 (24.2%). ≥0.6: 1,224, ≥0.7: 807, ≥0.8: 421, ≥0.9: 40. The ≥0.8 band is ~95% PIKIRANRAKYAT regional brands. Hand read of 100 at ≥0.8: **0 clear false-soft**, 3 borderline culture features.
  - Deleted: 40 articles, NEWS_L1 −40 / NEWS_L2 −54. The first attempt died with the stalled Docker Postgres at its backup `SELECT`, writing nothing; the retry completed. Store check 11/11 zero before and after.
  - Re-queue: 1,299 pre-probe rejects → 436 past the category gate → **290 re-queued**, 146 kept; a second pass re-queued 0. After fetching: **199 saved (68.6%)**. IDNTIMES 197/261 (75.5%); LIPUTAN6 0/18, CNBC 0/5, INEWS 2/5. A 30-row hand read of the saves was all hard news.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. The cosine era's soft residue is concentrated (PIKIRANRAKYAT's regional brands) and confidently scored. At ≥0.8 the probe made no clear mistake in 100 rows, so a wider delete is safe if the owner wants one later.
  2. A headline-only lenient re-score is a good recall net for desks whose news was wholesale-rejected (IDNTIMES `news`, 75% kept), and a poor one for finance desks with promo-heavy bodies (LIPUTAN6 `bisnis`, 0/18). Let the live filter decide with the body; never act on the headline score alone.
- **Links:** SYS-48 (#144) 3d3c997, SYS-38 (#122) ffddacd · `backups/sys45_soft_by_probe_cosine-era_p0.9_20260930T060625Z.json`, `backups/sys38_requeue_20260930T023819Z.json`

### 2026-10-03 — QA audit of HANDOFF_2026-09-29 (24 ready-for-qa tickets): 21 closed, 1 sent back, 2 held; 11 new tickets
- **Question / hypothesis:** Do the dev list's 24 closures hold on production and under mutation, and what did the dev's 9 run-watching findings turn into?
- **Method:**
  - **Suites:** in a clean `git -c core.autocrlf=false archive de824d3` copy (the 19:00 crawl was running, so the live tree was never mutated): `tests/unit` 1,580 passed / 5 skipped (3 repo-hygiene tests fail in a non-git copy only), `tests/integration` 156 passed.
  - **31 QA mutants**, none in the dev's runner list, with the same textual-replace runner as `qa_mutation_runner.py`. The unit side ran with `POSTGRES_DB_PORT=1`, scratch `VECTOR_DB_NAME_L*`, blank Telegram token and **`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`** (see the trap below).
  - **Read-only production SQL**, a live `check_crawlers.py` of the 13 trafilatura-first sources, and the DBT-16 live check: uvicorn on :8765, query before and after the crawler's FAISS save.
  - **Offline SYS-40 re-run:** `NewsletterGenerator(ds, api_key=None).build_sections(arts, g._encode([a["l1_text"] for a in arts]))` on `get_articles_for_date(20260928)`, plus the average cosine distance between every pair of shown clusters.
  - **OPS-18 silent paths:** `audit_filter_drift.main([])` with `load_decisions` and `SQLAlchemyArticleDataSource` stubbed, and `make_labeller` returning a labeller whose `call` returns `""`, or raising `SystemExit("OPENAI_API_KEY is not set")`.
- **Result:**
  - **Mutants: 26 of 31 killed.** Survivors:
    - OPS-18 `if found:` → `if False:` (the alert is never sent from `main`);
    - OPS-18's 7-day SQL window ignored;
    - OPS-18's per-source shuffle removed;
    - SYS-20 recount `DRIFT_SQL` without its `first_seen_at` clause (the test uses its own copy of the query) → DBT-50 #214;
    - DBT-16 `is_current` true when untracked: equivalent, since every index the datasource builds is tracked.
  - **DBT-05:** all 13 sources were already 100% trafilatura before (e.g. CNBC 1,891/1,891, KONTAN 1,130/1,130). Char averages move 1–11% in both directions, like the site-first sources. Live check: OK = 11, FALLBACK = 0, TEMPO BROKEN (Cloudflare 403).
  - **DBT-16 live:** the crawler saved L1 86,452 → 86,474. The next query (> 30 s later) logged `Reloaded FAISS index(es) saved by another process: NEWS_L1, NEWS_L2` and `faiss_in_memory` equalled disk.
  - **SYS-40 (2026-09-28):** 3,643 articles, 12 sections, 96 shown clusters, 1,051 articles shown. The big clusters are single stories (rupiah 42/19 sources, Rp 1 stock floor 42/18, Antam 38/19, BI rate 18/15). **2 shown cross-section pairs are at or under 0.30 average distance** (0.263 BPJS, 0.272 Kopdes), 0 within one section → SYS-65 #211.
  - **OPS-18:** the largest source × category cell over 12 weekly seeds on production was **8**, against `AUDIT_CELL_MIN_N` = 20, so the per-cell alert can't fire. Stub runs: the empty labeller gives `rc 0`, `completed`, n = 0, no send; the missing key gives `SystemExit` escaping, no `log_crawl_failed`, no send → OPS-37 #210.
  - **Boilerplate:** in 7,708 of the 15,963 articles saved since 2026-10-01, e.g. BERITASATU 97% "Baca juga", KONTAN 95%, DETIK 98% promo tail → SYS-63 #204.
  - **Retries:** TIRTO failed 3 fetches "after 5 retries" in 0.1 s. 1,047 rows are parked for 7 days by a first transient failure, PIKIRANRAKYAT 989 of them from a 727/896 refusal burst on 09-29 → SYS-66 #213.
  - **Dev findings → tickets:** WinError 5 → SYS-64 #208; TEMPO → SRC-56 #205; Docker stall → OPS-36 #209; sitemap `ok` on a dead index → OPS-35 #207; KUMPARAN backlog (51 → 391 not fetched per run, 563 never attempted) → SRC-57 #206; the newsletter was never scheduled → OPS-38 #212.
  - **Dev findings, no ticket:** VIVA's 1,200 s kill has not recurred. INEWS 98 × 404 are SRC-29 mirror residue at `fetch_failures = 2`.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. **Restore a mutated file with `git checkout` (new mtime), or run with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`.** `shutil.copy` + `shutil.move` restored a same-length mutant (`TOPICS_FINANCE + TOPICS_NEWS` ↔ `TOPICS_NEWS + TOPICS_FINANCE`) with an mtime in the same second as the mutant's `.pyc`. Python kept the mutant's bytecode, and 7 later mutants were "killed" by an unrelated SYS-40 test. Tell-tale: many mutants killed by the same unrelated test.
  2. A Done-when bar that a sampling design can't reach (OPS-18's per-cell n ≥ 20 with ~7 rows per source) is dead code. Measure the reachable n on production before accepting the threshold.
  3. Dev's SYS-38 70% gate: QA accepted on evidence. LIPUTAN6's 18 re-rejects were 16 clear soft and 2 arguable, all caught by the body pass.
- **Links:** closed SRC-55 #201, OPS-34 #202, SRC-32 #117, DBT-27 #88, DBT-21 #72, SRC-10 #65, DBT-16 #51, DBT-02 #21, DBT-05 #24, SRC-19/20/22/23/24/26 #99–#106, DBT-49 #203, OPS-19 #132, SYS-51 #170, SYS-48 #144, SYS-38 #122, SYS-54 #185. Sent back: OPS-18 #123. Held: SYS-20 #41 (no extraction run since 2026-06-02), SYS-40 #126 (owner read + SRC-34). New: #204–#214.

### 2026-10-03 — SRC-56: TEMPO block probed; a firewall/IP block page, not BISNIS's JS challenge; rss.tempo.co is stale
- **Question / hypothesis:** Is TEMPO's 403 (since the 2026-09-30 19:00 run) the BISNIS-style Cloudflare challenge, or something narrower, and is there an unblocked listing? (The owner decided not to retire TEMPO.)
- **Method:** `curl -L -A "<Chrome 129 UA>" -H "Accept-Language: id-ID"` on www.tempo.co's `/sitemap.xml`, `/sitemap-news.xml`, `/news-sitemap.xml`, `/rss`, `/feed`, `/amp` and homepage, on a stored article URL, on `nasional.`, `koran.`, `rss.`, `en.tempo.co`. Then `chrome.exe --headless=new --virtual-time-budget=15000 --dump-dom https://www.tempo.co/`. About 15 requests in total.
- **Result:**
  - Every www path, articles included, gives **403** with a 5,481 B `Attention Required! | Cloudflare` page ("Why have I been blocked?…"), and **no `cf-mitigated` header**. Real headless Chrome gets the same page.
  - `nasional.`/`koran.` redirect into the block.
  - `rss.tempo.co` gives 200, but the feed is stale (newest item 2026-09-27, 50 items), summary-only, and its links redirect into the block.
  - `en.tempo.co` gives 200, but it's the English edition (out of scope).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  - This is not BISNIS's managed challenge (`cf-mitigated: challenge`, "Just a moment…"). A block page shown to a real browser engine fits a **firewall/IP rule**, plausibly against this network after weeks of twice-daily parallel fetching.
  - Decisive next test (owner): a normal browser on this network vs a phone on mobile data.
  - `rss.tempo.co` is no workaround: it's stale and its links hit the block.
  - Do not rotate IPs or proxies around a ban.
- **Links:** SRC-56 (#205), SRC-03 (#14) precedent, SRC-57 (#206) / SYS-66 (#213) pacing

### 2026-10-03 — SYS-66/SYS-70: bot User-Agent across the fleet, fetch share of run time, and PIKIRANRAKYAT's "refusal burst" was dead DNS
- **Question / hypothesis:**
  - Can the crawler identify itself honestly without losing sources?
  - How much per-source concurrency can it give up within the 1,200 s kill?
  - Was PIKIRANRAKYAT's 727/896 `ConnectionError` burst (2026-09-29) a host refusing us, as SYS-66 assumed?
- **Method:**
  - `scripts/experiments/probe_bot_ua.py` (new, read-only, sequential, 1 s pauses): each source's sitemap index and its newest saved article with the old browser UA vs `NewsAggregatorBot/1.0`, plus `robots.txt` (`Crawl-delay`, `can_fetch`).
  - Fetch share from the 2026-10-01..03 run logs ("Processing batch" → "Batch N has X articles extracted" vs the `[full_text]` seconds).
  - `nslookup` + `curl` on 4 PIKIRANRAKYAT hosts, and the `requests` exception chain for a dead host vs a refused port.
- **Result:**
  - **Bot UA:** refused (403) by BERITASATU (index + article), INVESTORID (index + article) and TRIBUN (article only). Served by the other 23 working sources; BISNIS and TEMPO are 403 to both UAs. **No source sets `Crawl-delay`; `robots.txt` allows every index and article.** After the change, `check_crawlers.py` over the fleet gives OK = 27, FALLBACK = 0 (bot UA + browser UA for the three).
  - **Fetch share at 20 threads:** VIVA 418 of 794 s, MEDIAINDONESIA 478 of 705 s (serial already), JPNN 115 of 180 s, DETIK 136 of 583 s, all others < 40 s. A fleet cap of 2–4 would push VIVA/DETIK past the 1,200 s kill, so the owner chose to keep throughput and pace only where measured.
  - **PIKIRANRAKYAT:** `jurnalgarut.`, `kendalku.`, `portalbangkabelitung.`, `utaratimes.pikiran-rakyat.com` do **not resolve** (curl exit 6, no DNS answer). The 989 parked rows are on retired regional brand subdomains (13 in the top 15 hosts by count), newest row id 35,577 (June residue, no longer listed). A dead host raises `ConnectionError → MaxRetryError → NewConnectionError → socket.gaierror`; a refused port has `ConnectionRefusedError` there instead.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:**
  1. Not a refusal burst. SYS-66's ticket text was wrong on this point. Those rows are dead URLs, and SYS-62's repeat rule striking and retiring them is correct.
  2. Counting every `ConnectionError` in SRC-55's breaker would have **starved PIKIRANRAKYAT for good**: 10 dead-host rows in a row on every run, never stamped. The loader now names the dead-host case `NameResolutionError after N retries`, and the breaker ignores it.
  3. Do not switch to a fleet-wide bot UA without `BROWSER_UA_HOSTS`, and do not cap concurrency fleet-wide without per-source timeouts measured first.
  4. Retries with backoff need `REQUEST_MAX_RETRIES` ≤ 3: `.env` had 5, which is ~23 s per dead-host URL.
- **Links:** SYS-66 (#213), SYS-70 (#218), SRC-57 (#206) · `scripts/experiments/probe_bot_ua.py` · commit e9840d2
